# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/)
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).
See [`docs/deployment/versioning.md`](docs/deployment/versioning.md) for the full
SemVer policy and what counts as a Major / Minor / Patch change for this project.

## Conventions

Each released version uses `## [MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH] - YYYY-MM-DD`. Within a
release, entries are grouped under these section headings (Keep a Changelog
v1.1.0, in this canonical order):

- **Added** — new user-visible features, modules, or CLI surfaces.
- **Changed** — non-breaking changes to existing behaviour or wording.
- **Deprecated** — APIs slated for removal in a future Major; still functional.
- **Removed** — APIs deleted in this version. Always paired with a Major bump
  unless pre-1.0 (see versioning.md §Pre-1.0 caveat).
- **Fixed** — bug fixes that don't change documented behaviour.
- **Security** — vulnerabilities fixed; reference CVE / advisory if any.

### Marking breaking changes

Any change that would force operators to do work to upgrade — schema rename,
CLI flag removal, default-value flip, default-backend swap, frontmatter
contract change — gets a `### BREAKING` callout under the version's section.
The callout names the symptom an operator would observe, the migration
path (typically a `python -m atomic_agents.migrate --to vN` invocation),
and a one-line rationale. Example:

```
## [0.2.0] - 2026-06-01

### BREAKING

- **Memory schema bumped 2 → 3.** Frontmatter `confidence` field is now an
  enum (`high`/`medium`/`low`) rather than a freeform string.
  Migrate: copy `docs/migrations/v2_to_v3.py` into `<vault>/_migrations/`,
  then run `python -m atomic_agents.migrate --to v3`.
  Why: enables typed conformance tests in the eval runner.
```

The pre-merge expectation is: if a PR's diff would trigger a `### BREAKING`
callout, the PR title starts with `feat!:` / `refactor!:` and the PR body
includes the callout text verbatim, ready to paste into the next release's
CHANGELOG entry.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **GoalBackend write-path ADOPTION + `AtomicAgent.goal_backend` live-wiring + archive data-loss fix** ([#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448) PR1 of 3 — Goal write-path adoption, COARSE-ROUTE). Routes `GoalManager`'s three persistence paths through the `GoalBackend` Protocol: `load()` → `backend.load_goal()`, `save()` → `backend.save_goal()`, `_append_goal_history_jsonl()` → `backend.append_history_event()`. Pure in-memory computation (`mark_*` mutators, `evaluate_completion`, `next_sub_goal`, cycle detection) stays above the Protocol per Principle #2/#3. `apply_transition()` is RESERVED for #448 PR3 (coordinator + cost gate) — routing dispatch through it this PR would break golden tests because `apply_transition` uses `date.today()` vs. GoalManager's injectable `self.today` clock. **`AtomicAgent` now exposes `self.goal_backend`** as a live-wired public attribute + `goal_backend: GoalBackend | None = None` constructor kwarg (kwarg-wins-over-env, default `get_default_goal_backend(agent_root)` — exact mirror of `journal_backend`). `GoalManager.__init__` gains matching `goal_backend: GoalBackend | None = None` kwarg with the same precedence. `GoalBackend` imported at module level in `agent.py` so the type annotation resolves under `typing.get_type_hints()` (mirrors `JournalBackend` pattern). **INTENTIONAL data-loss fix (A3 ruling):** `GoalManager.archive()` hand-rolled a frontmatter dict that silently dropped `deadline`, `parent_goal`, `related_atomic_notes`, `related_decisions`, `related_canon_pages` — verified bug (Principle #12). Fixed in place: swapped for `build_goal_frontmatter(self._goal)` + `archived_at` + `archive_reason`, keeping the injectable `self.today` clock. Full archive-path adoption through `backend.archive_goal()` is deferred to [#483](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/483) (requires injectable-clock surface on the backend). Frozen-test exception: `test_goal.py:test_archive_moves_goal_to_archive` updated to assert all five preserved optional fields (`deadline`, `parent_goal`, `related_atomic_notes`, `related_decisions`, `related_canon_pages` — the shared `agent_with_goal` fixture gains `parent_goal` + `related_canon_pages` so the fix is fully guarded); this is the one sanctioned exception to the 4-file freeze and the file is otherwise byte-frozen (no incidental reformatting). **`save()` validation hardening (conscious delta, spec/41 MUST 5-aligned, NOT silent):** the pre-#448 `save()` wrote `goal.md` with NO frontmatter validation; routing through `backend.save_goal()` → `_write_goal()` now runs `validate_goal()` before the durable write and **fails closed** (raises `SchemaValidationError`, writes nothing) on an in-memory `Goal` whose serialized frontmatter the backend's own `load_goal()` would reject. This is **byte-identical for any valid goal** (validation is read-only; every `mark_*`/`add_sub_goal` mutator produces valid state) — it only changes the failure mode on already-invalid state from "write invalid bytes" to "raise", closing the write-time/read-time validation asymmetry MUST 5 requires of EVERY write path (`save_goal` AND `apply_transition`). The one reachable public path that hits it is `mark_blocked()` with a forward reference (sub-goal A, listed first, `blocked_by` a later-listed B), which the old unvalidated `save()` persisted; guarded by `test_save_forward_ref_blocked_by_fails_closed` (+ a backward-ref-still-persists boundary partner). **`noqa: ARG002` misplacements fixed** in `FilesystemGoalBackend.append_history_event()` and `archive_goal()` — suppressors were on `event` and `reason` (used parameters) instead of `agent_id` (the unused one). **Doc fixes:** `docs/protocols-shipped.md` archive path corrected (`goal_archive/<slug>/goal.md` → `goal_archive/<slug>.md` — doc-only bug); stale "deferred to #448" language removed from `protocols-shipped.md`, `spec/41`, and `goal/__init__.py:get_default_goal_backend` docstring. `spec/41` status line updated to reference both PRs; Goal-outcome composition section updated to reflect coarse-route adoption. Follow-up [#483](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/483) filed inline (full archive-path adoption). New test file: `tests/test_goal_adoption_golden.py` (pins goal.md + goal_history.jsonl bytes across all 6 CLI subcommands + dispatch-outcome with injected today= for determinism — including the dispatch audit line's `total_cost_usd`/`iterations` cost-tracking fields per Principle #5; AtomicAgent goal_backend wiring tests build a REAL constructible agent and assert outside any try/except so the wiring is genuinely exercised; the save-validation forward/backward boundary; scoped to file contents — `.goal.lock` sidecar excluded). **`/ship` review pass:** added the `FilesystemGoalBackend` symlink-containment guard (see ### Security) + its regression suite `tests/test_goal_backend_containment.py`; strengthened `tests/test_goal_adoption_golden.py` to route `next`/`report`/`dispatch-outcome` through the `goal_main` CLI (not just direct `GoalManager` calls), cover all four `dispatch_as_outcome` terminal branches (satisfied/interrupted/max_iterations_reached/failed), assert the A3 optional-field preservation through the CLI archive path, and prove a minimal goal (no optional fields) archives with those keys ABSENT (common-case byte-identity); removed the now-dead `GoalManager._serialize_sub_goal` static method (orphaned when `save()` started routing through `backend.save_goal()`) + its unused import; fixed stale comments to match reality (Principle #13) — the `outcome_backend` block now says "wired in #448 PR2" (not "#448"), and the `_goal_impl.py` `goal_backend` resolution mirrors `agent.py`'s `is None` shape with a corrected cross-reference. Follow-ups filed inline: [#485](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/485) (`append_history_event` ts-first reorder conformance test) and [#486](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/486) (`GoalManager.archive()` unresolved-`agent_root` containment). Test suite: **4,428 collected** (4,356 passed / 72 skipped; net +41, 2026-06-13).

- **OutcomeBackend write-path ADOPTION + `OutcomeRunner.outcome_backend` kwarg + spec/42 LOCKED** ([#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448) PR2 of 3 — Outcome write-path adoption, COARSE-ROUTE). Routes `OutcomeRunner.run()`'s single write site through `self.outcome_backend.write_result(agent_name, run_id, result)` — the backend owns the canonical path `agent_root/outcomes/runs/<run_id>/result.json` from `run_id`. `OutcomeRunner._write_result_json` stays intact as the reference serializer (TEST 30 in `test_outcome_backend_conformance.py` depends on it for byte-identity pin). **`OutcomeRunner.__init__` gains `outcome_backend: OutcomeBackend | None = None` kwarg** (keyword-only, default `get_default_outcome_backend(self.agent_root)`, kwarg-wins-over-env — exact mirror of PR1's `GoalManager.goal_backend` kwarg). Two separate imports with two separate topologies: `OutcomeBackend` (the type, for the kwarg/attribute annotation) is imported under `TYPE_CHECKING` from `.backend` — sound because `from __future__ import annotations` makes the annotation a string (the import is itself cycle-free, `_outcome_impl → .backend → .types`, no path back, so it could sit at module level, but `TYPE_CHECKING` matches the in-file convention for the sibling backend types and avoids the E402 suppression). The default-factory `get_default_outcome_backend` lives in `outcome/__init__.py`, which imports `_outcome_impl` at module level — so a module-level `from . import get_default_outcome_backend` in `_outcome_impl` WOULD cycle; it is therefore imported LAZILY inside `__init__` (guarded by `if outcome_backend is None: from . import get_default_outcome_backend`) to break that cycle. **INTENTIONAL behavior fix (A1 ruling):** when an operator passes a custom `--output-dir`, `result.json` now lands at the canonical `outcomes/runs/<run_id>/result.json` instead of `output_dir/result.json`. The custom-output_dir `result.json` was previously **invisible to `list_runs`/`read_result`/`export`** (orphan bug — custom `result.json` was never enumerated by `FilesystemOutcomeBackend`). Agent ARTIFACT files still go to `output_dir` — only the audit envelope relocates. DEFAULT case (output_dir == canonical) is **byte-identical-location**. Guarded by `tests/test_outcome_adoption_golden.py` (4 tests: default-output_dir routing proof + spy asserts `write_result` called exactly once with `(agent_name, run_id, result)`; custom-output_dir relocation — also asserts the agent artifact file STAYS in the custom dir; kwarg-wins-over-env — sets `ATOMIC_AGENTS_OUTCOME_BACKEND` and proves the injected kwarg backend wins; error-propagation — a backend whose `write_result` raises makes `run()` propagate, pinning the no-catch discipline). **NO try/except around the routed write** (no split-brain / fail-open — spec/42 MUST 9 write-once and Principle #5 audit trail). `AtomicAgent.outcome_backend` remains the per-agent HANDLE for operator inspection and the future PR3 coordinator — it is NOT the write path. `AtomicAgent` does NOT construct or feed an OutcomeRunner; the two instances resolve independently from the same `agent_root`. Two-instance shape documented in comment at write site for the PR3 author. **`get_default_outcome_backend` docstring updated** (removed stale "deferred to #448" wording). **`agent.py` comment block updated** from scaffolding-only to LIVE-WIRED shape (mirrors the `journal_backend`/`goal_backend` LIVE-WIRED blocks at lines 656–692). **spec/42 LOCKED** (drift audit passed: all 9 MUSTs covered by conformance suite, no inline TODOs in spec, scaffolding-only language removed, "deferred to post-#448" paragraph removed, line 262 updated with A2 nuance — OutcomeRunner write path active; AtomicAgent.outcome_backend the coordinator handle, not the write path). Legit-permanent deferrals stay in LOCKED spec (supports_run_query #454, single-run export #454 — true-today). `docs/protocols-shipped.md` OutcomeBackend section updated to reflect PR1 + PR2 write-path adoption. `filesystem.py` `export()`/`export_all()` docstrings updated to replace "this scaffolding PR" with "by deliberate design (see spec/42 §Export fidelity and follow-up #454)". **`/ship` review pass:** (security) `FilesystemOutcomeBackend.write_result` now resolves its path via `_validated_result_path(run_id)` (not raw `_run_dir / "result.json"`) so the now-live write path refuses a symlinked `result.json` exactly as `read_result`/`export` do — one containment posture across read AND write (no exploit before — POSIX `os.replace` doesn't write through a symlink — but the asymmetry mattered once the path became load-bearing); (tests) the error-propagation golden added + the custom-output_dir and kwarg-wins-over-env goldens hardened (above); (maintainability, Principle #13) stale "this PR" temporal comments made permanent in `outcome/backend.py`, `agent.py` (×2); stray capital `Write_result` → `` `write_result` `` in `outcome/__init__.py`; a co-maintenance note added to `OutcomeRunner._write_result_json` flagging it as the verbatim-lift source TEST 30 pins (do-not-delete). Test suite: **4,432 collected** (4,360 passed / 72 skipped; net +4 golden adoption tests, 2026-06-13).

- **`QueueBackend` Protocol + `FilesystemQueueBackend` reference impl + DRAFT spec/44** ([#428](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/428) — the seventeenth backend Protocol, PR 1 of 1, SCAFFOLDING-ONLY). Carves the cascade work-queue cluster (`claim_next_queued`, `release_claim`, `move_to_dead_letter`, `recover_stale_claims`, `renew_lease`, `_sidecar_path`, `_write_sidecar`) from `atomic_agents/_cascade.py` into a swappable `QueueBackend` Protocol. Zero internal runtime callers wired this PR — the `_cascade.py` free-function API is preserved via a thin non-deprecated shim; POSIX-rename semantics and on-disk layout are byte-identical to the pre-carve codebase. New `atomic_agents/queue/` package: `backend.py` (`@runtime_checkable QueueBackend` Protocol with `claim_next` / `release` / `move_to_dead_letter` / `renew_lease` / `list_claimed` / `export` / `capabilities`), `types.py` (abstract `QueueItem` Protocol type + `FilesystemQueueItem` subtype carrying `path` + frozen `QueueCapabilities` + `QueueExport` as an `ExportableResult` subclass), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemQueueBackend` reference impl), `__init__.py` (registry: `register_queue_backend` / `get_queue_backend` / `list_queue_backends` / `unregister_queue_backend` + `get_default_queue_backend(project_root)` factory). `FilesystemQueueBackend` is **project-scoped** (scope token is `project_root`, NOT `agent_root` — the one project-scoped backend in the v1.5 wave, matching spec/06 where the queue is a shared project resource). `claim_next` atomicity is the queued→claimed POSIX rename (one winner under a concurrent race; the loser gets `FileNotFoundError` and tries the next candidate). The `.lease.json` sidecar write is best-effort; `recover_stale_claims()` falls back to the work file's mtime for torn/legacy sidecars. `QueueItem` carries NO `path` field (that filesystem detail lives on `FilesystemQueueItem`) so future Redis/SQS/DB backends don't fake a path. Symlink containment mirrors the journal/outcome siblings: resolve to CHECK `is_relative_to(project_root)`, then return the UNRESOLVED path for file ops so `item.path` stays byte-identical to the pre-carve cron API. `export()` re-asserts containment on every durable leaf; export embeds DURABLE subset only (`queued/` + `done/` + `dead-letter/`) — in-flight `claimed/` and `.lease.json` sidecars are structurally excluded (runtime-bound/ephemeral, mirrors `LockExport` precedent). `QueueExport` re-exported from `atomic_agents.export` package root; `FilesystemQueueBackend` registered in the spec/40 export conformance harness (`tests/test_export_protocol_conformance.py` + `tests/test_export_capability_advertisement.py`). `recover_stale_claims` is a shared free-function built only from Protocol calls (`list_claimed` + reclaim) so every backend runs the same recovery with no drift. Operator override: `ATOMIC_AGENTS_QUEUE_BACKEND` env var (default `'filesystem'`). Doctor: `check_queue_backend(agent_root)` derives `project_root` via `detect_cascade()`, validates the configured backend resolves, and WARNs (not FAILs) when `single_host_only=True` with `ATOMIC_AGENTS_MULTI_HOST=true`. Additive `role` key in `.lease.json` sidecar (ignored by legacy readers) enables `list_claimed(role=...)` filtering. 12 normative MUSTs in spec/44 Implementer Contract (base-8 PersonaBackend pattern + 4 queue-unique axes: atomic-claim-via-rename / no-double-claim-under-race; lease-expiry-recovery correctness; dead-letter terminal-transition; `single_host_only` capability honesty). Conformance suite: `tests/test_queue_backend_conformance.py` (claim-race tests + doctor + export + import-equivalence) + `tests/test_queue_filesystem.py` (POSIX-rename + `.lease.json` sidecar + legacy-mtime-fallback specifics). **Closes TENSIONS T4** (queue-claim atomicity locked to POSIX rename): with the Protocol carved out, a Redis/SQS/DB backend providing cross-host atomicity can drop in without forking the claim logic, lifting the single-host constraint for multi-host deployments. Test suite: **4,387 collected** (4,315 passed / 72 skipped; net +135 from conformance + export harness + filesystem-specific tests + the `/ship` perimeter-containment security regression suite — see ### Security, 2026-06-12).

- **`JournalBackend` Protocol + `FilesystemJournalBackend` reference impl + ADOPT-NOW wiring (bundle.py / agent.py / dream.py) + DRAFT spec/43** ([#427](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/427) — the sixteenth backend Protocol, PR 1 of 1). Abstracts the three hand-synced `rglob` journal-read blocks that were scattered across `bundle.py`, `agent.py`, and `dream.py` behind a single `JournalBackend` Protocol. **ADOPT-NOW ruling:** all three read sites are wired in the same PR as the Protocol definition (unlike Goal/Outcome which were SCAFFOLDING-ONLY). The motivation: the three sites are distributed duplicates with no single flat module to carve — leaving them unwired would re-create the maintenance burden the Protocol eliminates. New `atomic_agents/journal/` package: `backend.py` (`@runtime_checkable JournalBackend` Protocol with `append_entry` / `query_by_date` / `list_entries` / `export` / `export_all` / `capabilities`), `types.py` (canonical **frozen** `JournalEntry` + `JournalCapabilities` + `JournalExport` as an `ExportableResult` subclass — **frozen is the deliberate INVERSE of goal/outcome's mutable exception**: journal entries are append-only immutable episodes, not state-machine objects; the correct analog is `LogEntry`, not `Goal`), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemJournalBackend` reference impl), `__init__.py` (registry: `register_journal_backend` / `get_journal_backend` / `list_journal_backends` / `unregister_journal_backend` + `get_default_journal_backend(agent_root)` factory + `_redact_for_error_message` credential-safety helper). `JournalCorrupted` exception added to `atomic_agents.exceptions` (mirrors `GoalCorrupted`/`OutcomeCorrupted`). **`JournalEntry.path` carries the caller's UN-resolved path shape** (NOT resolved/absolutized — relative if `agent_root` was relative, symlinked if it was symlinked) to stay byte-identical with the legacy `rglob` callers (resolving it would shift the bundle backtick render line + the `_source_paths`/`_staleness_paths` set that drives cascade `.lease.json` freshness) — storing `path.name` was a latent `dream.py:294` subdir-loss bug (month subdir `2026-06/` dropped) that this backend fixes; callers that need the filename string use `entry.path.name` explicitly; `export()` handles relativization. **Append atomicity (MUST 9):** exclusive `fcntl.flock` on a `.journal.lock` sidecar at `agent_root` level serializes the entire read-modify-append sequence (read current file → append text → `atomic_write`) — preventing the lost-update race where two concurrent callers read stale content and the second write clobbers the first. The `.journal.lock` sidecar lives at agent_root level (NOT inside `journal/`); `rglob('*.md')` never matches it; doctor MUST NOT treat it as corruption. **Date-query correctness (MUST 10):** `query_by_date(start, end)` returns exactly the entries in `[start, end]` plus all entries whose filename stem cannot be parsed as an ISO date (include-on-unparse fallback — matches legacy `dream._read_journal_entries`; the one deliberate divergence is that legacy caught only `OSError` and would PROPAGATE a `UnicodeDecodeError` (crashing consolidation), whereas `query_by_date` also skips non-UTF-8 files — strictly safer than legacy). **`list_entries()` sort order:** sort by full `Path` descending (NOT by `JournalEntry.date`) — path-based sort preserves the lexicographic ordering that all three legacy `rglob` callers relied on; sorting on `date` alone swaps positions at month boundaries, silently breaking bundle's staleness hash. **Symlink containment:** `_journal_dir()` raises `PathTraversalError` when `journal/` resolves outside `agent_root` (mirrors `FilesystemOutcomeBackend._runs_root()` pattern; load-bearing per MEMORY.md `feedback_cross_model_catches_same_family_blind_spots` — cross-family Codex review confirmed this HIGH-severity gap). Individual symlinked `.md` ENTRY files (symlink-to-file resolving under `agent_root`) are NOT filtered — they are selected and read through exactly as the three legacy `rglob` callers did (`is_file()`/`_safe_read_text`/`read_text` all follow symlinks); per-entry filtering would shift the newest-N selection (the byte-identity regression the ADOPT-NOW ruling forbids). Only the symlinked `journal/` DIRECTORY escape is refused. **STORAGE-ONLY boundary:** the backend owns the data layer; formatting STAYS at each call site. The divergence between bundle format (WITH backtick path line `\`{entry.path}\``) and agent format (WITHOUT) is **LOAD-BEARING** — byte-identity golden tests in the conformance suite (TESTS 44–45) freeze both render formats. Do NOT unify them. **Bundle / agent / dream call-site changes:** `bundle._render_journal_breakpoint()` and `bundle._source_paths()` / `bundle._staleness_paths()` replace their `rglob` blocks with `JournalBackend.list_entries()`; `agent._load_recent_journal()` replaces its `rglob` block with `list_entries()`; `dream._run_pipeline()` and `dream.start()` replace `_read_journal_entries()` with `query_by_date()` + a dict-adapter (`{"filename": entry.path.name, "text": entry.text}`) that preserves all 13+ downstream callers in `dream.py` unchanged. `AtomicAgent.__init__` gains `journal_backend: JournalBackend | None = None` kwarg + `self.journal_backend` attribute (LIVE-WIRED this PR, unlike outcome_backend which was scaffolding-only). `DreamRunner.__init__` gains the matching `journal_backend` kwarg. **spec/02 ownership reconciliation:** `spec/02` and `spec/24` both claimed "MemoryBackend retains exclusive ownership of memory/ and journal/" — **both incorrect** as of the legacy codebase; both corrected in this PR to "MemoryBackend retains exclusive ownership of memory/ only; journal/ is carved out to JournalBackend (spec/43)." **spec/35 + wizard.py latent typo fixed:** `journal/*.jsonl` → `journal/*.md` (journal files are always `.md`, never `.jsonl`). Operator override surface: `ATOMIC_AGENTS_JOURNAL_BACKEND` env var (default `"filesystem"`) + `get_default_journal_backend(agent_root)` factory + `doctor.check_journal_backend()` (dual-probe: `list_entries(limit=1)` liveness + `entry.path.read_bytes()` only when entries exist) + public `AtomicAgent.journal_backend` attribute. `JournalExport` is re-exported from the `atomic_agents.export` package root (`from atomic_agents.export import JournalExport`), registered in the spec/40 export conformance harness (`tests/test_export_protocol_conformance.py` + `tests/test_export_capability_advertisement.py`). DRAFT spec/43 ships at 10 normative MUSTs in the Implementer Contract (base-8 PersonaBackend pattern + MUST 9 append atomicity + MUST 10 date-query correctness incl. include-on-unparse fallback). No re-export shim needed: grep confirms no public `atomic_agents.journal` import path existed before this PR (Principle #14 shim mandate is conditional-and-void). Conformance suite: `tests/test_journal_backend_conformance.py` (60 tests, all 10 MUSTs + registry/doctor/golden-render coverage — including byte-identity golden tests for both bundle and agent render formats, plus a `_source_paths`/`_staleness_paths` journal-selection-parity test that pins the cascade `.lease.json` freshness set entry-for-entry against the legacy `rglob` top-N (is_file-filtered) selection; the agent render test drives the REAL `AtomicAgent._load_recent_journal` against a reproduced-legacy reference, not a re-inlined format copy), symlinked-entry SELECTION parity (bundle + dream), the dream dict-adapter shape, a relative-`agent_root` un-resolved-path assertion, a concurrent-append MUST 9 stress test, and a normalized permissions-class `fix_hint` pair asserting `check_journal_backend`'s list-probe and read-probe surface IDENTICAL remediation on a `PermissionError`) + `tests/test_journal_filesystem.py` (filesystem-specific tests: lock placement, month-subdir minting, symlinked-entry SELECTION parity in list/query (legacy followed symlinks), symlinked-`journal/`-DIRECTORY-containment FAIL paths, unresolvable-`agent_root` graceful construction (deferred resolve), symlink-loop list/query `[]` + append fail-loud, `.tmp` sidecar exclusion, skip-unreadable OSError/UnicodeDecodeError, export relative paths, `..` in agent_root rejection). **Resolution-failure guard:** `FilesystemJournalBackend.__init__` defers `agent_root.resolve()` to first use (a lazy `_agent_root_resolved` property) so construction stays side-effect-free and cannot crash agent construction on an unresolvable `agent_root` (symlink loop / inaccessible ancestor); `_journal_dir()` wraps the `.resolve()` calls so `(OSError, RuntimeError)` map to `PathTraversalError`, which reads degrade to `[]` (restoring the legacy `exists()`/`rglob()` graceful-empty behavior) and `append_entry` re-raises (fail-loud). `doctor.check_journal_backend` deliberately does NOT FAIL on an individual symlinked `.md` entry that resolves outside `agent_root` — the runtime follows such an entry through (byte-identity with the legacy `rglob` callers), so doctor agrees with the runtime contract rather than contradict it (`feedback_doctor_dual_probe_pattern`); only a symlinked `journal/` DIRECTORY escape is refused (caught by doctor's direct `_journal_dir()` probe, since `list_entries()` itself returns `[]` on a directory escape). Two MUST 4 fs-specific tests close the append write-failure (`atomic_write` raises `OSError` → propagate, no partial day file) and new-day-into-existing-month-subdir subcases. **Pre-landing review hardening:** the `_safe_read_entry` / `bundle._safe_read_text` graceful-degrade helper (kept byte-identical, [#464](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/464)) now (a) guards the `errors="replace"` re-read against a TOCTOU `OSError` so a mid-read deletion degrades-but-keeps instead of crashing the now-hot `agent.call()` journal path, and (b) names the OSError failure mode by exception type only — it no longer embeds `str(exc)` (which carries the absolute filesystem path) into the warning comment that flows into the agent system prompt and out to an external LLM provider. A `dream.start()` real-call-site regression test pins a month-subdir journal entry reaching the pipeline through `query_by_date` as `path.name`. Test suite: 4,180 passed / 72 skipped → **4,252 collected** (+92 net, 2026-06-12).

- **`OutcomeBackend` Protocol + `FilesystemOutcomeBackend` reference impl + DRAFT spec/42** ([#426](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/426) — the fifteenth backend Protocol, PR 1 of 2). Carves the flat `outcome.py` module into a proper Protocol + storage backend, following the pattern of GoalBackend (spec/41). New `atomic_agents/outcome/` package supersedes `atomic_agents/outcome.py`: `backend.py` (`@runtime_checkable OutcomeBackend` Protocol), `types.py` (canonical mutable `OutcomeResult` / `IterationRecord` dataclasses with `to_dict`/`from_dict` + frozen `OutcomeCapabilities` + `OutcomeExport` as an `ExportableResult` subclass), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemOutcomeBackend` reference impl), `_outcome_impl.py` (`OutcomeRunner` runtime behavior relocated from flat `outcome.py`), `__init__.py` (registry: `register_outcome_backend` / `get_outcome_backend` / `list_outcome_backends` + `get_default_outcome_backend(agent_root)` factory + backward-compat module-level re-exports). `from atomic_agents.outcome import OutcomeRunner` is a **supported, non-deprecated** compatibility re-export (Principle #14); no `DeprecationWarning`. `OutcomeResult` and `IterationRecord` are **mutable** dataclasses (deliberate divergence from frozen-DTO convention; the outcome layer is a state machine DURING the run — `status`/`explanation`/`iterations`/`output_files` are mutated across the `OutcomeRunner` loop; freezing would break the loop). `OutcomeCapabilities` is frozen (pure value object). `OutcomeExport` carries `(run_id, result_json_bytes, artifact_refs, backend_id, scope)`; `artifact_refs` are relative-to-`agent_root` strings (or absolute fallback when the artifact lives outside the agent vault). `FilesystemOutcomeBackend` implements `Exportable` with `supports_canonical_export=True` and `supports_artifact_storage=True`. **Artifact-reference portability (Option C):** on-disk `result.json` stays **BYTE-IDENTICAL** — `OutcomeRunner._write_result_json` is untouched; the NEW `FilesystemOutcomeBackend.export()` emits portable refs by rebasing absolute artifact paths to relative-to-`agent_root` via `path.is_relative_to(self._agent_root)` guard. `OutcomeCorrupted` exception added to `atomic_agents.exceptions` (mirrors `GoalCorrupted`). Operator override surface: `ATOMIC_AGENTS_OUTCOME_BACKEND` env var + `get_default_outcome_backend(agent_root)` factory + `doctor.check_outcome_backend()` (dual-probe: `list_runs` lightweight + `read_result` only when runs exist) + public `AtomicAgent.outcome_backend` attribute (scaffolding-only this PR — set at construction, never read internally). Do NOT add `outcome_backend=` kwarg to `OutcomeRunner.__init__` until [#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448) (the write path is not wired; a kwarg would be dead code). `OutcomeExport` also registered in the spec/40 export conformance harness (`tests/test_export_protocol_conformance.py`) and the capability-advertisement harness (`tests/test_export_capability_advertisement.py`). 9 normative MUSTs in spec/42 Implementer Contract (side-effect-free construction, capability honesty, `list_runs` returns `[]` when absent, atomic writes, round-trip fidelity, `OutcomeCorrupted` on corrupt result.json, `AtomicAgentsError` on absent run, stable `backend_id`, **write-once/result-immutability** as the unique 9th axis — `write_result()` raises on second write for same `run_id`). Conformance suite: `tests/test_outcome_backend_conformance.py` (76 tests, all 9 MUSTs + filesystem-specific + path-traversal/symlink-containment + corruption-net regression tests). Per-iteration telemetry STAYS in `LogBackend` (`PRIMITIVE_OUTCOME_ITERATION` / `_append_iteration_log`) — `OutcomeBackend` covers only the terminal `result.json` envelope. `OutcomeExport` is re-exported from the `atomic_agents.export` package root (`from atomic_agents.export import OutcomeExport`), mirroring `GoalExport`. `export()`/`export_all()` are deliberately scoped to the single most-recent run (the single-run `OutcomeExport` shape) for this scaffolding PR — a documented divergence from the spec/40 `export_all()` "all records" wording; a multi-run export shape is filed as [#454](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/454). Artifact-ref rebasing is against `agent_root` (the agent's own root, for whole-agent portability), a deliberate departure from the arc ruling's literal `agents_root` pattern, recorded in spec/42 §"Artifact-reference portability". DRAFT spec/42 ships at 292 lines; spec/14 §Outcomes cross-reference addendum added; spec/27 `outcome-backend` doctor-check section added (`grep -cE '^def check_[a-z_]+_backend\b'` = 14, `check_outcome_backend` last). The issue body's "spec/13" cross-ref citation is a typo; the correct cross-reference is spec/14 (§Outcomes); the PR body documents the correction. The two pre-existing outcome tests (`tests/test_outcome.py`, `tests/test_goal_outcome_composition.py`) remain UNTOUCHED as the zero-behavior-change regression guard. Goal-outcome coordinator wiring (OutcomeRunner write path through `self.outcome_backend.write_result()`) deferred to [#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448). Test suite: 4,088 passed / 72 skipped (net +85 from conformance + export harness + path-traversal/corruption-net regression tests, 2026-06-11).

### Changed

- **GCP deployment guides: corrected goals/outcomes protocol status (docs-only).** `extras/gcp/README.md` and `extras/gcp/compute-vm/README.md` still described **goals** and **outcomes** as "no Protocol yet; #383" across their per-layer state-mapping tables (both Cloud Run and Compute-VM topologies), the Group A / Group B split, the Phase 3 scale-out row, and the VM disk-layout tree. Both protocols shipped after those guides were written — `GoalBackend` (DRAFT spec/41, [#425](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/425)) and `OutcomeBackend` (DRAFT spec/42, [#426](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/426); write-path wiring deferred to [#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448)). Updated both surfaces to "Protocol shipped (filesystem reference impl); cloud adapter pending" (mirroring the existing Memory-row phrasing), moved goals/outcomes from Group B (no protocol) to Group A (protocol exists, cloud adapter writable), and split the grouped rows. **As of this PR, `JournalBackend` (DRAFT spec/43, [#427](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/427)) ships, so journal got the same treatment** — its state-table rows in both READMEs move from "no Protocol yet; #383" to "Protocol shipped; cloud adapter pending," journal moves from Group B to Group A, and the Phase 3 row + the VM disk-layout tree line are updated; the "Journal, cascade (#383)" adapter-readiness bullet is split so cascade keeps `#383` while journal cites `#427`/spec/43. Only **dreams** and the **cascade queue** retain the still-accurate "no Protocol yet; #383" status. No code, no import-graph impact. Matches Principle #13 (documentation matches reality, not aspirations).

### Fixed

- **`doctor` no-`--agent` mode now emits SKIP for `goal-backend`, `outcome-backend`, and `journal-backend`.** The no-agent enumeration in `run_doctor()` was missing the agent-scoped backend checks, so `run_doctor(agent_name=None)` silently produced *no* entry for them (the other backends correctly emit SKIP). The guard test `test_run_doctor_no_agent_skips_agent_checks` used a subset assertion that did not assert them, so the omission passed blind (the exact silent-omission false-PASS class the test's own docstring claims to defend against). `goal-backend` was a pre-existing miss from #425; `outcome-backend` was introduced by #426; `journal-backend` would have been introduced by this PR. All three are now in the SKIP enumeration and the test's assertion set. `check_journal_backend` also normalizes its `list_entries` and `read_bytes` probes to a single shared permissions-class `fix_hint` so a `PermissionError` surfaces identical remediation regardless of which probe trips first.

### Security

- **`FilesystemGoalBackend` symlink-containment guard — brought to parity with its sibling backends** ([#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448) PR1). #448 PR1 makes `load_goal`/`save_goal`/`append_history_event` load-bearing for the first time (they were decorative scaffolding from #425), so `/ship`'s security review flagged that `FilesystemGoalBackend` was the one backend in the #425–#428 wave that shipped WITHOUT the perimeter-containment guard its siblings have (`FilesystemOutcomeBackend._runs_root()`, `FilesystemJournalBackend._journal_dir()`). A symlinked `goal.md` / `goal_history.jsonl` / `goal_archive/` / `.goal.lock` pointing OUTSIDE the resolved `agent_root` would otherwise be followed on read/write — a perimeter escape (a write landing outside the agent vault). Fixed with ONE consolidated invariant `_require_within_root(path, label)` (resolve + `is_relative_to(agent_root)` → `PathTraversalError`, folding resolution failure — symlink loop / inaccessible ancestor — into `PathTraversalError` rather than crashing agent construction now that `get_default_goal_backend()` runs in `AtomicAgent.__init__`), applied at EVERY I/O boundary: `load_goal`, `_write_goal` (save), `_append_jsonl` (history), `_goal_lock`, `archive_goal`, `list_archived`, `read_schema_version`, `goal_text`, `export`. Consolidated (one invariant, all paths) per `feedback_containment_reframe_not_whackamole`, NOT per-path point-checks. Zero behavior change for legitimate paths (the guard only fires on attack-shaped symlinks; the macOS `/var`→`/private/var` symlink is handled because both sides are resolved). New regression suite `tests/test_goal_backend_containment.py` (12 tests: per-path symlinked-escape → `PathTraversalError`, plus a positive control proving a normal vault never trips the guard). Cross-family Grok review confirmed the guard is sound for its perimeter contract; the residual within-perimeter TOCTOU (symlink swap between resolve and write) is the framework-wide [#480](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/480) item, explicitly out of scope per the trust model (an actor who can plant a symlink inside `agent_root` is already inside the trust boundary). The live in-place `GoalManager.archive()` path (unresolved `agent_root`, pre-existing) is the distinct companion gap, filed as [#486](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/486).

- **`FilesystemQueueBackend` perimeter containment hardened to a single sound invariant** ([#428](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/428)). The pre-carve `_cascade.py` queue had **no** path containment at all; carving it into a Protocol newly exposed it as a backend surface, so `/ship`'s multi-round adversarial review (Claude subagent + cross-family Codex) reproduced and closed every escape *outside* `project_root` — the sole in-scope security boundary. Closed and regression-tested: per-child symlinked `claimed/<token>/` dir leaking/exfiltrating via `list_claimed`/`recover` (the #426 ancestor-escalation class); `original_name` path-traversal in `release`/`move_to_dead_letter`/`renew_lease`; a symlinked source file claimed by `claim_next`; the `_cascade.py` shims bypassing validation by calling the `_at_path` sinks directly; a forged `QueueItem` whose `path` or source-*state* (`claimed/` vs `done/`/`dead-letter/`) escaped; symlinked work-file *leaves* and symlinked parent state dirs; and a symlinked `.lease.json` sidecar read outside the project. The accumulated point-checks were then consolidated into **one** invariant — `_require_canonical_source()`: a work file's RESOLVED path must equal EXACTLY its canonical location under the resolved queue root, be a regular file, and not traverse a symlink (exact-canonical-equality subsumes containment + source-state + basename + symlink-leaf + symlinked-parent). spec/44 gained a **"Security model — trust boundary"** section: the boundary is `project_root`; within-queue state integrity against an actor who already has write access to `queue/` is explicitly **out of scope** (that actor is inside the trust zone and can corrupt state directly), and adversarial/multi-host deployments use a real-authz backend (Postgres/Redis/SQS per T4), not a hardened filesystem backend. Residual within-perimeter items tracked as follow-ups: [#473](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/473) (renew_lease shim, frozen signature), [#477](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/477) (export `rglob` symlinked-subdir DoS), [#478](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/478) (claim-token clobber), [#479](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/479) (sidecar write symlink-leaf), [#480](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/480) (framework-wide `openat`/`O_NOFOLLOW` dir-fd TOCTOU hardening). No live vulnerability — the queue has no internal runtime caller this PR; this hardens the newly-exposed surface before any consumer wires it.

- **`FilesystemOutcomeBackend` refuses path traversal and symlink escape on `run_id` → path handling**, matching the framework invariant "Path traversal refused per `_io.safe_resolve_under`" and the sibling `memory/filesystem.py`. `write_result`/`read_result` validate `run_id` via `safe_resolve_under` + a single-direct-child check (refusing `../escape`, absolute paths, and in-root degenerate `''`/`'.'`/nested `a/b`); `list_runs`/`export`/`read_result` refuse symlinked run directories, symlinked `result.json`, and a symlinked `outcomes`/`runs` **ancestor** (containment anchored at `agent_root.resolve()` so a symlinked ancestor cannot become the trusted root). `read_result` and `export` also reject valid-JSON-wrong-shape `result.json` (a list/scalar, or non-list `output_files`/`iterations`) as `OutcomeCorrupted` / degrade-to-empty instead of leaking `AttributeError`. Hardening of a new, not-yet-runtime-wired surface (no live vulnerability); the doctor check fail-closes a traversing run rather than masking it as a benign vanished-run race.

- **`GoalBackend` Protocol + `FilesystemGoalBackend` reference impl + DRAFT spec/41** ([#425](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/425) — the fourteenth backend Protocol, PR 1 of 1). Carves the flat `goal.py` module (GoalManager, Goal/SubGoal dataclasses, CLI, schema constants all in one file) into a proper Protocol + storage backend, following the pattern of MemoryBackend (spec/20), LogBackend (spec/22), and SecretBackend (spec/38). New `atomic_agents/goal/` package: `backend.py` (`@runtime_checkable GoalBackend` Protocol), `types.py` (canonical mutable `Goal`/`SubGoal` dataclasses + single `validate_goal()` + `GoalCapabilities` + `GoalExport` as an `ExportableResult` subclass), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemGoalBackend` reference impl), `__init__.py` (registry: `register_goal_backend` / `get_goal_backend` / `list_goal_backends` + `get_default_goal_backend(scope)` factory). `GoalManager` relocated to `_goal_impl.py`; the documented public `from atomic_agents.goal import GoalManager` import path is preserved as a **supported, non-deprecated** compatibility re-export (no `DeprecationWarning` — path is intentionally permanent, matching Principle #14). `apply_transition(sub_goal_id, to_status, fields)` is the load-bearing atomic Protocol method: one durable status flip + optional output/blocked_by fields + one `goal_history.jsonl` audit line under a filesystem file lock (the same atomicity guarantee a Postgres backend delivers via a SQL transaction — this is what puts it on the Protocol per the T15 ruling). Pure computation (legal transition checks, cycle detection, `evaluate_completion`, `next_sub_goal`, `progress_report`) stays in `GoalManager` above the Protocol. `read_schema_version()` is a diagnostic-only probe (returns `int | None` from `goal.md` frontmatter) — goal.md is explicitly NOT registered as a `MigratableUnit`; the spec/03 `unit_type` is locked to `Literal["memory","wiki"]` and goal schema evolution is backend-internal. `GoalExport` is a composite `ExportableResult` carrying `(goal_md_bytes, history_records_with_bytes, archived_goals_with_bytes)`; `FilesystemGoalBackend` implements `Exportable` with `supports_canonical_export=True` and is registered in the spec/40 export conformance harness. Operator override surface (partial this PR): `ATOMIC_AGENTS_GOAL_BACKEND` env var + `get_default_goal_backend(scope)` factory + `doctor.check_goal_backend()` uses the dual-probe pattern (`list_archived` + `load_goal`) per `feedback_doctor_dual_probe_pattern`. The `AtomicAgent(goal_backend=...)` constructor kwarg + `self.goal_backend` attribute are deferred to the runtime-wiring follow-up ([#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448)). Goal-outcome composition (`dispatch_as_outcome` coordinator wiring GoalBackend + OutcomeRunner with a pre-dispatch cost gate) was built in round 1 but removed after verification: the implementation was dead (no callers), untested, and FAIL-OPEN (unpacked `CostCheckResult` as a 2-tuple, causing `TypeError` silently swallowed as `allowed=True`, a Principle #4 cost-gate escape); maintainer ratified the defer and filed [#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448). `dispatch_as_outcome()` DOES ship with a correctness fix: it now persists `goal.md` before its `goal_history.jsonl` audit line (spec/41 MUST 6 ordering), with a dedicated regression test in `tests/test_goal_dispatch_audit_ordering.py`. 9 normative MUSTs in spec/41 Implementer Contract (charset validation, side-effect-free construction, capability honesty, atomic `apply_transition`, no-data-loss archive, collision-safe archive identity, idempotent archive retry, spec/41 MUST 8 stable `backend_id`, MUST 6 goal.md-before-history-line audit ordering). Conformance suite: `tests/test_goal_backend_conformance.py` (all 9 MUSTs, 887-line parametrized suite) + `tests/test_goal_filesystem.py` (filesystem-specific) + `tests/test_goal_doctor.py` (dual-probe, PASS/FAIL/SKIP ladder) + `tests/test_goal_dispatch_audit_ordering.py` (MUST 6 regression). The 4 pre-existing goal test files (`test_goal.py`, `test_agent_goal_loading.py`, `test_dashboard_goals.py`, `test_goal_outcome_composition.py`) kept intact as the zero-behavior-change regression guard. GoalBackend also registered in the spec/40 export conformance harness (`tests/test_export_protocol_conformance.py` + `tests/test_export_capability_advertisement.py`). DRAFT spec/41 ships at 267 lines; spec/12 §"Goal and sub-goal schema" cross-reference addendum added; spec/27 `check_goal_backend()` section added. **Pre-merge hardening (arc adversarial rounds + `/ship` review-army + cross-model adversarial — Claude + Codex):** `apply_transition()`'s `fields` channel is an explicit allow-set (`SUB_GOAL_TRANSITION_FIELDS`) so it can never rewrite sub-goal identity (`id`/`label`) or status; `_write_goal()` (the single serializer both `save_goal` + `apply_transition` use) now runs `validate_goal()` BEFORE the durable write — spec/41 MUST 5 write/read-validation symmetry — closing a triple-confirmed P0 where `fields={"blocked_by": "<unknown-id>"}` could persist a `goal.md` that `load_goal()` then rejects, locking the agent out of its own goal; the audit line is serialized before the goal.md write (fail-closed on non-JSON input, no partial commit, MUST 6 ordering preserved); `goal/_redact_for_error_message` gained the schemeless-DSN heuristic the other five backends already had (credential-echo parity); `doctor.check_goal_backend` treats a `goal.md` that vanished mid-check (concurrent archive) as PASS not a spurious FAIL; the `from atomic_agents.goal import main` back-compat path was restored. Follow-ups filed: [#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448) (coordinator + runtime wiring), [#449](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/449) (forward-only `blocked_by` two-pass validation), [#450](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/450) (dispatch-ordering test flake harden), [#451](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/451) (`GoalExportQuery` bounded export), [#452](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/452) (archive idempotency vs no-active-goal semantics). Conformance index 53 cases. Test suite: 3,981 passed / 72 skipped → **4,075 collected** (4,003 passed / 72 skipped, +94 net, 2026-06-11).

### BREAKING

- **Migration script contract changed (issue #429 — T13 refactor).** The old path-shaped `MigrationScript` Protocol (`applies_to(path: Path)` / `migrate(path: Path, dry_run: bool)`) is **removed**. Operator-authored migration scripts must be rewritten to the new per-unit handle contract: `applies_to(unit: MigratableUnit) -> bool` / `migrate(unit: MigratableUnit) -> dict` (no `dry_run` parameter — the handle enforces the gate). The old free-function API (`run_migration()`, `build_migration_plan()`, `create_snapshot()`, `restore_snapshot()`, `find_content_files()`, `get_current_vault_version()`, `discover_scripts()`) is removed from `atomic_agents.migrate`. New API: `from atomic_agents.migration import FilesystemMigrationBackend, MigratableUnit` — construct `FilesystemMigrationBackend(agents_root)` and call `backend.run_migration(target_version, dry_run)`. The `python -m atomic_agents.migrate` CLI entrypoint is **unchanged**. Migration upgrade path table: `docs/spec/03-file-formats.md §Migration upgrade path`. Rationale: backend-neutral Protocol so future DB backends satisfy the same contract without forking the runner (TENSIONS T13).

### Added

- **`MigrationBackend` Protocol + `FilesystemMigrationBackend` reference impl — spec/03 §Schema-migration re-LOCKED (issue #429, T13 refactor).** New `atomic_agents/migration/` subpackage: `MigrationBackend` (`@runtime_checkable` Protocol), `MigratableUnit` (per-unit handle with `unit_type` discriminator + `dry_run`-baked-in `write_frontmatter()` no-op gate), `MigrationSnapshotRef` (opaque snapshot handle), `MigrationCapabilities`, `MigrationPlan`, `MigrationResult`, `MigrationEvent` (JSONL audit record). `FilesystemMigrationBackend` wraps today's vault-walk and tar+swap logic behind the Protocol: `enumerate_units()` (full walk, dedup by resolved path, `unit_type` set from directory), `read_schema_version()` (min across ALL units — no 50-file sample cap), `apply_unit()` (calls `applies_to(unit)` / `migrate(unit)`, verifies schema_version bump post-apply), `snapshot(target_version)` (atomic temp-then-rename tar+gz; filename embeds the **target** version), `restore(ref)` (atomic staging swap, preserves snapshots + audit log), `run_migration()` (pre-flight fail-close → exclusive lock → plan → snapshot → apply → validate → unlock → emit audit event). `MigrationRollbackUnavailable` raised pre-snapshot when `supports_transactional_rollback=False` on a real (non-dry-run) run. Vault-level exclusive lock via `FilesystemLockBackend` prevents concurrent migration runs. `MigrationEvent` JSONL appended to `<agents_root>/_migrations/migration.jsonl` on **every** exit — success, validation rollback, mid-apply exception, OR early pre-plan refusal (no-rollback fail-close, broken/absent script chain, target-below-current); pre-plan failures (those raised before the plan is built) record sentinel `from_version=-1` + the error string. A lock-busy refusal happens *after* the plan is built, so it records the resolved `from_version`, not the sentinel (spec/03 MUST 8). The audit emit is isolated from lock-release so an abnormal unlock never suppresses it; the JSONL line is written through `_io.atomic_append_jsonl` (flush + `os.fsync`), matching LogBackend's durable-append discipline so the forensic record of a destructive op survives an OS-level crash. Snapshot path emitted to STDOUT before any writes; the snapshot filename embeds the **target** version (`..._pre_v{target}_migration.tar.gz`, e.g. `pre_v2` for a run to v2), matching the spec/03 recovery examples. Post-migration validation covers **every enumerable unit** (not just the touched subset) — a script that skips a unit leaves it at the old version, and the half-migrated vault that produces is caught and rolled back (no-half-migrated-vault guarantee). 8 normative MUSTs in spec/03 §Schema-migration Implementer Contract (MUST 1: `read_schema_version()` vault-wide read-only; MUST 2: `enumerate_units()` dedup + `unit_type`; MUST 3: atomic writes; MUST 4: `snapshot(target_version)`/`restore()` protocol methods; MUST 5: fail-close on no-rollback; MUST 6: stable `backend_id`; MUST 7: version-bump post-apply verification; MUST 8: audit event on every exit, isolated from lock-release). `atomic_agents/migrate.py` updated to construct `FilesystemMigrationBackend` internally; `python -m atomic_agents.migrate` CLI entrypoint and all flags unchanged. The operator-facing `--rollback` CLI path is also audited: a manual rollback records its own `MigrationEvent` (`rolled_back=True` on success, `rolled_back=False` + the error on failure) so a forensic recovery action stays queryable (MUST 8 covers `run_migration()` OR `restore_and_audit()`). `snapshot()` **fail-closes** on a vault whose unit dir is a symlink with an absolute/escaping target — such a vault captures an unrestorable link member, so snapshot refuses at creation time (pre-mutation, naming the member + remediation) rather than handing the operator a false safety net that only fails at rollback (MUST 4 recoverable-checkpoint + MUST 5 fail-close); `restore()` wraps any unsafe-member `tarfile` rejection in `AtomicAgentsError` so it honors its documented exception contract. Symlink-dereference support for such vaults is tracked as follow-up [#444](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/444). 83 conformance tests in `tests/test_migrate.py` (82 pass + 1 capability-gated skip): Protocol surface (isinstance, backend_id, capabilities), `read_schema_version` (empty/mixed/full-walk-no-sampling), `enumerate_units` (count, dedup/symlink, unit_type, cascaded layout), `apply_unit` (dry-run noop, real bump, missing-bump raises, **`migrate()`→`{}` stays falsy/skip-counted on dry-run, falsy-migrate-after-`applies_to`→`True` raises on real run + rolls the vault back**, **operator-script exceptions in `applies_to()`/`migrate()` wrapped in `AtomicAgentsError` naming script+unit with the original chained via `__cause__`**), `snapshot`/`restore` (round-trip, corrupt-tar atomic safety, mid-extract atomic safety, unknown-id raises, fresh-instance, target-version filename, **snapshot fail-close on escaping-symlink vault leaves no artifact**, **restore wraps unsafe-member tar rejection in `AtomicAgentsError`**), `restore_and_audit` (manual-rollback audit on success/failure, **pre-restore read failure degrades `from_version` to the -1 sentinel + still restores**, post-restore read failure degrades `to_version` to the sentinel + still audits), fail-close path (no-rollback stub isinstance, real-run raises, dry-run proceeds, fail-before-apply), `run_migration` integration (dry-run no-change + audit, **happy-path real run completing with `validation_passed=True`/`rolled_back=False` via monkeypatched `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`**, rollback-on-validation-failure, **skipped-unit validation triggers rollback**, **audit written on pre-plan failures**, **audit survives lock-release failure**, **lock-busy refusal records the resolved `from_version`, not the -1 sentinel (MUST 8)**, wiki-schema dispatch, target-below-current, no-scripts, audit-real-run), custom-frontmatter-field preservation, capability-gated conformance matrix (filesystem runs; no-rollback stub SKIPS), breaking-signature assertions, **CLI dispatch smoke (`main()` routes `--status` / `--list-snapshots` / no-arg-help through a freshly constructed backend with correct exit codes — the operator entrypoint preserved across the refactor)**. The standard validators reject any `schema_version != CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`, so a real cross-version migration completes end-to-end only once the package bumps that constant (documented in spec/03 §Validation); until then a real migration to a not-yet-adopted target validates-and-rolls-back by design. Legacy (pre-`schema_version`) v0→v1 support tracked as follow-up [#439](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/439).

- **`Exportable` companion Protocol + canonical-shape export contract — spec/40 (LOCKED)** ([#379](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/379) — PR 1 of 1). Delivers the T15 Position B spine: the canonical file shape is always reconstructable as a tested round-trip contract. New `atomic_agents/export/` package: `Exportable` (`@runtime_checkable` Protocol — NOT bolted onto the 13 locked backends, structural composition only), typed `ExportableResult` hierarchy (`MemoryExport`, `LogExport`, `MandateExport`, `CorpusExport`, `LockExport`, `SecretExport`), per-backend query types (`MemoryExportQuery`, `LogExportQuery`, `MandateExportQuery`, `CorpusExportQuery`, `LockExportQuery`, `SecretExportQuery`), `SecretExportRef` (logical-key + hint, NEVER plaintext), and shared per-type renderer (`render_run_record_bytes`, `render_note_bytes_from_raw`/`from_object`, `render_corpus_page_bytes_from_raw`/`from_object`, `render_mandates_md`, `render_secret_export_bytes`). Filesystem identity export impls for five state backends (`export_memory`, `export_log`, `export_mandate`, `export_corpus`, `export_lock`) plus `export_secret` in `atomic_agents/export/filesystem.py`. `supports_canonical_export: bool = False` (LAST field, backward-compatible default) added to `LogCapabilities`, `LockCapabilities`, `MandateCapabilities`, `CorpusCapabilities`, `SecretCapabilities`, and `MCPServerRegistryCapabilities`; `FilesystemMemoryBackend` uses a `@property` (matching `supports_semantic_search` shape, MemoryCapabilities dataclass convergence deferred to [#431](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/431)). All six PR1 filesystem impls advertise `True`. 9 normative MUSTs in spec/40 Implementer Contract: Protocol composition, capability-claim honesty, `export_all()` thin wrapper, Tier A byte-exact fidelity, UTF-8/LF/no-BOM, state-enumeration-not-retrieval, snapshot-consistency bound, RunRecord key-order (`to_dict()` insertion order NOT `canonical_json()` sorted), and SecretBackend never-leak invariant (ABSOLUTE, unconditional). `MandateExport` includes `meta_by_scope` for the project-root `## _meta` policy block (security boundary: dropping it silently reverts `forbidden` → `open`); `render_mandates_md(mandates, meta=None)` emits and re-parses the block losslessly. `LockExport.lock_file_names` is always `[]` (ephemeral runtime state, never migrated). The full `MandateConstraints` surface round-trips byte-exactly — all USD caps, `allowed_targets`/`blocked_targets` (with custom kinds), `time_window` (seconds preserved), `action_class`, `unconstrained` + justification, and revocation fields. `export_log` returns every matching line verbatim for `export_all()` (unbounded); a date-bounded query prefilters shards for performance, with the verbatim caveat for hand-misfiled lines documented in spec/40. 91 new tests: `tests/test_export_capability_advertisement.py` (capability advertisement, not capability-gated — prevents skip-all-by-accident) and `tests/test_export_protocol_conformance.py` (round-trip conformance with one shared `assert_canonical_roundtrip(backend, write_fn, expected_bytes_fn)` helper, capability-gated skips, per-backend sections). Cross-spec addenda shipped in spec/20 (MemoryBackend), spec/21 (LockBackend), spec/22 (LogBackend), spec/29 (MandateBackend), spec/34 (CorpusBackend), spec/38 (SecretBackend). Per-backend SQLite/Postgres/Redis/GCP/HTTP-registry export impls deferred to later PRs. `atomic-agents export` CLI deferred to [#430](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/430). `include_versions` history scan deferred to [#433](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/433). Custom operator keys for SecretBackend export deferred to [#432](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/432). Shard-window prefiltering for bounded LogExport disk I/O deferred to [#434](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/434). Tier B object-graph renderers (no callers until structured backends land) and export error-path edges deferred to [#435](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/435). spec/40 is **LOCKED** on filesystem proof (contract-first ruling); CLAUDE.md Principle #1 and TENSIONS.md T15 now cross-reference it as the implementation contract.

## [1.1.0] - 2026-06-10

Security-fix + GCP-delivery release. Closes three HIGH advisories (GHSA-xhcr-cqfr-m3hv, GHSA-rm43-82j9-r4mj, GHSA-j659-8xh6-5pq5) and the public hardening issues #400-#406, ships the GCP Secret Manager backend (#340 PR 2, spec/38 LOCKED) + the corrected GCP v0 deployment topology (#395), and bumps dependency floors. Upgrade from 1.0.0 is recommended for all deployments; the three HIGH fixes default-on with no config change.

### Security

- **Cleartext-HTTP MCP catalog → MITM → local RCE hardened (GHSA-xhcr-cqfr-m3hv)** — two new defense-in-depth gates close the path where a network man-in-the-middle on a cleartext `http://` MCP catalog injects an arbitrary `command` field into an `MCPServerSpec`, which `MCPClientPool` then spawns as a subprocess. (1) **Transport scheme gate (spec/36 MUST 11):** `HTTPMCPServerRegistryBackend` now refuses cleartext `http://` for non-loopback catalog hosts unless the operator explicitly opts in via `ATOMIC_AGENTS_MCP_SERVER_REGISTRY_ALLOW_HTTP=1` (only the literal `"1"` counts). Loopback hosts (`127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`, literal `localhost`) are always allowed — loopback traffic cannot be intercepted by a network adversary. The loopback check uses `urlparse().hostname` (not `.netloc`) to resist userinfo-injection (`http://127.0.0.1@evil.com/`); the opt-in flag is resolved once at construction (no TOCTOU); a URL too malformed to classify fails closed. The gate fires on every I/O method — `list_mcp_servers`, `load_mcp_server`, `load_all_mcp_servers`, `validate`, **and the write paths `install`/`uninstall`** — before `_ensure_probed()`, so a refused scheme blocks even when the capability cache is warm. (2) **Spawn gate / command-basename allowlist (spec/36 MUST 12):** `MCPClientPool` validates every spec's `command` basename against an allowlist before constructing `StdioServerParameters` — no subprocess is spawned for a disallowed command. Default set `{npx, uvx, python, python3, node, docker}`. Operators replace (not extend) the set via a `## Allowed commands` section in the agent's `mcp.md`; an empty section is explicit deny-all (with a loud warning). `MCPCommandNotAllowed` (subclass of `MCPServerConnectFailed`) propagates out of `connect_all()` fail-closed — it is NOT soft-skipped. The allowlist is wired end-to-end on the live agent path: `agent.call()` resolves it from `self._profile.mcp_md_raw` and threads it into the pool. A spawn-gate refusal writes a JSONL run record carrying `run_id` + the error class (Principle #5 audit trail). **Honest scope limit:** the spawn gate blocks direct shell/exec-binary injection but does NOT prevent code execution through an allowlisted interpreter invoked with a code-exec flag (`python -c`, `node -e`); operators handling untrusted catalogs should narrow `## Allowed commands`. spec/36 re-locked: MUSTs 1-10 covered by the parametrized conformance suite, MUST 11 by `tests/test_mcp_server_registry_http_backend.py`, MUST 12 (a `MCPClientPool` pool-layer invariant, not a backend method) by `tests/test_mcp.py`. New tests across `tests/test_mcp.py` and `tests/test_mcp_server_registry_http_backend.py`. Default behavior unchanged for existing deployments (https and loopback http keep working; default command set covers the common MCP runners).
- **Deploy-doc hardening: perimeter identity-header stripping (#405) + shared-Redis AUTH/TLS (#406).** Two documentation-only hardening notes from the 2026-06-09 security review. `docs/deployment/serve.md` now states explicitly that the perimeter MUST strip or overwrite the configured identity header on every inbound request (the framework trusts it unconditionally, so a forwarded client-set header spoofs the audit-trail caller identity); a generic header name like `X-Forwarded-User` or a plain forwarding proxy is the risk. `docs/spec/21-lock-backend.md` adds a shared-Redis hardening note: require `rediss://` (TLS), AUTH credentials, and a distinct `key_prefix` per deployment when one Redis is shared, and flags the spec's `redis://localhost` examples as loopback-only illustrations. No framework-side JWT verification is added (spec/37 MUST 6 keeps perimeter auth final). No code change.
- Hygiene version floor bumps for three transitive dependencies. CVE paths were each triaged as unreachable in this framework (the vulnerable code paths are not exercised by atomic-agents-stack's use of these libraries); bumps are preventive hygiene, not emergency fixes.
  - `idna>=3.15` (was 3.13; CVE-2026-45409 — internationalized domain name codec; not reached via any agent call path).
  - `pyjwt>=2.13.0` (was 2.12.1; PYSEC-2026-175/177/178/179 — JWT decoding; atomic-agents-stack uses pyjwt only as a transitive dep of `mcp`, which uses it for OAuth token validation in MCP server connections, not for any user-facing JWT decode path).
  - `starlette>=1.0.1` (was 0.27; PYSEC-2026-161 — Starlette request handling; the `[serve]` extra floor was raised from the old 0.27 baseline to 1.0.1 and the lock resolved to 1.2.1). Floor encoded in `pyproject.toml` `[project.optional-dependencies] serve`; `idna` and `pyjwt` encoded as `[tool.uv] constraint-dependencies` (transitive floor pins that do not add to user-visible install requirements).

### Added

- **`MemoryBackend` operator override surface — `ATOMIC_AGENTS_MEMORY_BACKEND` env var + `AtomicAgent(memory_backend=...)` kwarg + `get_default_memory_backend` factory + doctor coherence check** ([#382](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/382) — PR 1). Wires operator selection of the `MemoryBackend` to match the shipped `LockBackend` / `LogBackend` / `AgentProfileBackend` override surface exactly. `ATOMIC_AGENTS_MEMORY_BACKEND` env var (default `"filesystem"`) selects the backend; `AtomicAgent(..., memory_backend=...)` constructor kwarg wins when supplied. Unknown backend ids fail-fast at agent construction time (`BackendNotRegistered`) with the full known-id list — no silent filesystem fallback. New public `get_default_memory_backend(agent_root, *, lock_backend=None)` factory in `atomic_agents.memory` routes all state-writing construction sites (`agent.py`, `dream.py`, `tuning.py`, `_capture.py`, `_versioning.py`) through one selection seam; `lock_backend` is threaded through so `apply_staging` and `agent.call()` share the same lock backend instance. Uniform construction contract: every registered `MemoryBackend` MUST accept `(agent_root: Path, *, lock_backend=None)` — written into spec/20 as a normative MUST and enforced by a registry conformance test. Delegate threading deliberately absent: memory is per-agent state (each agent has its own `memory/` directory), so `AtomicAgent.delegate()` never threads the coordinator's memory backend to a child — each child resolves its own via the same process-global env selection (distinct from persona/corpus fleet-scoped config, which IS threaded). `DreamRunner` guards against non-filesystem backends at construction time and raises `NotImplementedError` rather than breaking silently at apply time (routing through a backend-agnostic staging-adopt path deferred to [#396](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/396)). Two new doctor checks mirror the `check_lock_backend` / `check_locks` pair: `check_memory_backend_config` (coherence — known id, constructs for non-filesystem) and `check_memory_backend` (liveness — factory resolves and `stats()` returns); both route through `get_default_memory_backend` (doctor-reuses-factory invariant). `ATOMIC_AGENTS_MEMORY_BACKEND_URL` companion var deferred to [#258](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/258) (the first non-filesystem memory backend that needs a connection URL). spec/20 updated: `## Operator override surface` section added + spec re-locked via the drift gate. 29 new tests in `tests/test_memory_operator_override.py` covering: factory env-var path, kwarg-wins, lock threading, registry helpers, uniform construction contract + registry conformance, doctor coherence + liveness checks including a registered-backend PASS. Default stays filesystem; zero behavior change for existing deployments.
- **`VertexGeminiLLMBackend` — fourth reference LLM backend (Gemini on Google Cloud Vertex AI)** ([#345](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/345)). New `atomic_agents/llm/vertex_gemini.py` implements the `SyncLLMBackend` Protocol over the `google-genai>=1.0` SDK with `vertexai=True`. `provider_id="vertex-gemini"`; routes `vertex/gemini-*` model ids (scoped to `gemini-` so the upcoming `vertex/claude-*` backend lands without prefix collision). Registers lazily on the first model lookup behind a guarded try/except — missing `google-genai` logs at DEBUG (not WARNING, so a non-Vertex deployment sees no first-call noise) and continues. Optional `atomic-agents-stack[vertex]` extra (`google-genai`). **Auth is Application Default Credentials (ADC) only** — no API-key string; operators run `gcloud auth application-default login` or attach a service account (express-mode API-key auth deferred to [#378](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/378)). Per-family honest capability table: `tools`/`tool_results=True` for flash/pro (False for flash-lite, which lacks function calling); `cache_control=False` for all families (Vertex context caching is a separate resource API incompatible with the `CacheDirective` pattern — [#377](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/377)); `streaming=False` (deferred to `StreamingLLMBackend`); `vision=False` for all families (no image-block translation path yet — advertising True would violate conformance rule 4; [#376](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/376)); longest-prefix family resolution so dated aliases like `gemini-2.0-flash-lite-001` resolve to the correct (more specific) family. `call()` translates system prompt via `GenerateContentConfig.system_instruction` (not a message), strips JSON-Schema `title` keys (Gemini's OpenAPI subset rejects them), extracts token counts from `usage_metadata.prompt_token_count`/`candidates_token_count`, and **fails loud (raises `AtomicAgentsError`) when a content-producing response omits usage_metadata** rather than reporting 0 tokens and silently disarming the cost guardrails (`usage_reporting=True` honesty). `format_tool_results()` emits the two-turn continuation (model-role `function_call` echo + user-role `function_response`) Vertex requires — a `function_response` not immediately preceded by its matching `function_call` is rejected `400 INVALID_ARGUMENT`; every result is transmitted (orphan results are not silently dropped). `count_tokens()` is a conservative char/3 heuristic that accounts for both `content`- and `parts`-shaped messages so multi-iteration tool loops are not under-counted. Four `vertex/gemini-*` entries added to `_costs.PRICING` (verified against the Vertex AI pricing page). New `doctor check_vertex_credentials()` resolves ADC and mints an OAuth token via `credentials.refresh(Request())` — proves credentials are usable without a billable LLM call; WARN (not FAIL) when `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` is unset (Cloud Run / GKE auto-resolve it). spec/31 updated with the `VertexGeminiLLMBackend` reference description; README / `protocols-shipped.md` / explainer / CLAUDE.md updated from three to four reference backends.
- **OpenTelemetry trace export — off-by-default tracing seam + `[otel]` optional extra + DRAFT spec/39** ([#341](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/341) — PR 1 of arc). Emits one `atomic_agents.call` span per `agent.call()` when `ATOMIC_AGENTS_TRACING_ENABLED=true` AND `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` is set; otherwise installs no TracerProvider and leaves the OTel API's default `ProxyTracerProvider` in place — zero cost, zero background threads, no warnings. `opentelemetry-api>=1.20,<2.0` added to core dependencies (genuine no-op path; importing `tracing.py` costs nothing when tracing is off). SDK + OTLP/HTTP exporter (`opentelemetry-sdk`, `opentelemetry-exporter-otlp-proto-http`) are opt-in behind the new `atomic-agents-stack[otel]` extra — HTTP exporter only, no gRPC (avoids the grpcio dependency weight). New `atomic_agents/tracing.py` module: `_ensure_tracer_provider()` (installs SDK provider only when the global is still the bare `ProxyTracerProvider` — never clobbers a host provider), `get_tracer()`, locked span/attribute name constants (`SPAN_AGENT_CALL`, `ATTR_AGENT_NAME`, `ATTR_TRIGGER`, `ATTR_RUN_ID`, `ATTR_MODEL`, `ATTR_OUTCOME`, `ATTR_COST_USD`, `ATTR_INPUT_TOKENS`, `ATTR_OUTPUT_TOKENS`, `ATTR_TOOL_ITERATIONS`), and pre-declared child-span / `gen_ai.*` constants for later arc PRs. `agent.call()` span lifecycle: opened immediately after `run_id` reset (span carries the fresh `run_id`), attached as active OTel context, ended on all exit paths via idempotent finalizer — normal return, exception, lock_busy, pre-loop cost-skip, mid-loop cost-cap. Every span mutation (open-path `start_span`/`context.attach`/`set_attribute`, all `set_status`/`record_exception`/`set_attribute` / `span.end` calls in the finalizer) is wrapped so a tracing failure can never propagate, crash `call()`, or prevent lock release. Outcome precedence: `error > lock_busy > skipped > deferred > ok`. DRAFT spec/39 ships at 253 lines with 9 normative MUSTs in the Implementer Contract. 24 new tests in `tests/test_otel_tracing.py` covering: base import without `[otel]` extra; disabled/no-endpoint zero-span paths; successful call `ok` span with cost/token/iteration attributes; exception re-raise with `error` span + partial spend (MUST 1 pin); lock_busy / pre-loop cost-skip / mid-loop cost-cap outcomes; OTel context baseline after call; host-provider non-clobber (MUST 6); span-open fault tolerance (MUST 3 symmetric); OTLP exporter constructed with no `endpoint=` arg so the exporter reads env vars itself. Child spans (`gen_ai.client.chat`, tool / helper / delegate) are constants-declared but not yet emitted — deferred to later arc PR.
- **`GCPSecretManagerBackend`: second SecretBackend reference impl + `[gcp]` extra + `check_gcp_secret_backend()` + LOCKED spec/38** ([#340](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/340), PR 2 of 2). New `atomic_agents/secret_backend/gcp.py` with `GCPSecretManagerBackend` + `make_gcp_secret_backend_from_url` factory. Resolves the `latest` version of each GCP Secret Manager secret live on every `get()` call (no caching, `supports_rotation=True`); key-to-secret-name mapping `key.lower().replace('_', '-')` (e.g. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` → `anthropic-api-key`); resource path `projects/<project_id>/secrets/<name>/versions/latest`. Auth via ADC / workload identity (side-effect-free `__init__`; gRPC channel deferred to first `get()` via `_get_client()`). Thread-safe lazy client construction via double-checked locking + `threading.Lock`; idempotent `close()` releases the gRPC transport. GCP error mapping: `NotFound` → `SecretNotFound`; all other `GoogleAPICallError` → `SecretError` (fail-closed, base-class catch). Trailing newline/whitespace stripped from decoded payload (MUST 7; prevents silent auth failures from secrets created via `echo` instead of `printf '%s'`). MUST 4 secrecy: exception messages name the key and resource path only, never the resolved value. `locate()` source label: `gcp-secret-manager:projects/<id>/secrets/<name>` (built from request path, not API response). `capabilities`: `supports_rotation=True`, `supports_audit_logging=False` (GCP Cloud Audit Logs are operator-configured, not automatic; https://cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/audit-logging), `persists_plaintext=False` (AES-256 encrypted payloads in Google-managed KMS). New `[gcp]` optional extra (`google-cloud-secret-manager>=2.16`; lazy-imported, base startup does not load the SDK). `get_default_secret_backend()` gcp branch updated from placeholder-raise to real `make_gcp_secret_backend_from_url` construction; `ImportError` when `[gcp]` extra absent raises `SecretBackendNotRegistered` (not raw `ImportError`) naming the extra. New `check_gcp_secret_backend()` doctor function (ADC liveness probe via `credentials.refresh(Request())`, non-billable, mirrors `check_vertex_credentials()`; WARN when `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` absent); wired from `check_secret_backend()` when `backend_id == "gcp"`. `check_secret_backend()` fix_hint updated (removed stale "ships at PR 2" reference; added known ids `filesystem` + `gcp`). spec/38 LOCKED: status block, shipping plan, module layout tree, capability tables, operator surface table, MUST 2 scoped to filesystem with GCP addendum, GCP doctor check section, test-count claim all reconciled per LOCK ceremony. `docs/deployment/secret-backend.md` migration guide added (filesystem default as permanent first-class choice; GCP as one concrete opt-in subsection). `google-cloud-secret-manager>=2.16` added to `[dependency-groups].dev` (mirrors fakeredis/psycopg/otel-sdk precedent; `google.api_core.exceptions` must be importable for error-mapping branches). Conformance fixture extended to `params=['filesystem', 'gcp']` with `make_present` fixture; all three fixtures (`backend`, `force_absent`, `force_other_sources_absent`) have GCP branches; `monkeypatch.setenv`-only live-key injections replaced with `make_present` calls. 74 new tests (49 GCP-specific in `tests/test_secret_backend_gcp.py` + 25 new `[gcp]` parametrized conformance tests). Total secret backend test count: 149 (56 conformance [25 parametrized x 2 backends + 6 non-parametrized charset tests] + 49 GCP-specific + 11 CLI + 33 filesystem-specific). Plus 8 new doctor tests for `check_gcp_secret_backend()` + the `check_secret_backend()` gcp delegation, including a delegated-FAIL backend_id-propagation test (`tests/test_doctor_gcp_secret_backend.py`). protocols-shipped.md SecretBackend section updated from DRAFT/PR-1-of-2 to LOCKED with GCPSecretManagerBackend.
- **`SecretBackend` Protocol + `FilesystemSecretBackend` reference impl + `atomic-agents secrets` CLI + DRAFT spec/38** ([#340](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/340) — PR 1 of 2, the thirteenth backend protocol). Abstracts credential resolution behind a Protocol so alternate secret substrates (GCP Secret Manager, AWS Secrets Manager, HashiCorp Vault) drop in without forking. New `atomic_agents/secret_backend/` package with `__init__.py` (registry: `register_secret_backend` / `get_secret_backend` / `list_secret_backends` + `get_default_secret_backend` factory + `_redact_for_error_message` helper), `types.py` (`SecretRef` frozen dataclass + `SecretCapabilities` frozen dataclass), `backend.py` (`@runtime_checkable SecretBackend` Protocol + `SecretError` / `SecretNotFound` / `SecretBackendNotRegistered` exception hierarchy), and `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemSecretBackend` resolving env vars → macOS Keychain → `~/.config/atomic_agents/keys.json` in fixed non-configurable priority).
