Added `test_agent_load_indexes_protocol_path_exception_soft_degrades` to `tests/test_corpus_migration_regression.py` with a `_RaisingCorpusBackend` stub whose `render_index_summary` raises `sqlite3.OperationalError`, verifying soft-degrade + log marker + backend-class-name-in-warning. Round 2 findings F1 (broad except misdirects with sqlite-URL hint on non-storage errors), F2 (programmer errors silently degrade wiki context with no hard crash), F7 (sqlite-specific URL remedy in error message): defended as trade-off calls per CLAUDE.md "best, not cheapest" -- production stability over development-experience clarity, with the cause type included in the error message for developer debugging. Test suite: 2888 -> 2889 passing, 48 skipped, zero regressions.

- **CorpusBackend Round 1 adversarial review fixes** ([#65](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/65) PR 3 of 4). Round 1 hunted in the wiring blast radius and caught 3 high-confidence FIXABLE bugs + 5 INVESTIGATE findings, all addressed in the same merge. A1: the SQLite branch's default URL construction at `corpus/__init__.py:261` did not URL-encode `agent_root.name`. Names containing URL metacharacters (spaces, `+`, `&`, `?`, `=`) would either crash `AtomicAgent.__init__` with an uncaught `ValueError` from the URL factory's query-param parser OR silently corrupt the `agent_scope` (e.g., `my+agent` decodes as `my agent` via `parse_qsl`, causing cross-scope contamination with a real agent named `my agent`). Fix: `urllib.parse.quote_plus(agent_root.name)` at the URL construction site. A2: `FilesystemCorpusBackend.render_index_summary` at `corpus/filesystem.py:699-702` caught only `OSError`. Any wiki/INDEX.md with a stray non-UTF-8 byte (Latin-1 import, BOM, mixed encodings) would raise `UnicodeDecodeError` uncaught through both `_load_indexes` and `_render_memory_breakpoint`, crashing agent construction. The pre-PR-3 bundle path used `_safe_read_text` which catches `UnicodeDecodeError`; the Protocol path did not. Fix: widen the except to `(OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)` for symmetry with the bundle's legacy soft-degrade. A4: the Protocol path in `agent.py:_load_indexes` at line 2964-2970 had no exception boundary. A custom backend whose `render_index_summary` raised `sqlite3.OperationalError` (db locked), `CorpusError`, `KeyError`, or any other exception would propagate uncaught and crash agent construction. Fix: broad `except Exception` around the Protocol call, soft-degrading to `""` with a logged warning marker `wiki_index_unreadable` so operators see the degraded state (matching the legacy path's pattern). The `corpus/__init__.py:264` sqlite construction try/except widens to `Exception` for the same reason: previously caught `(OSError, PermissionError)` missed `ValueError` (malformed URL, invalid agent_scope charset) and `sqlite3.OperationalError` (WAL transition failure on NFS, db locked at cold start); now any construction failure re-raises as `CorpusBackendNotRegistered` with the URL remedy in the message. Doctor: the URL-without-backend WARN at `doctor.py:2601-2616` previously asserted "the URL is being ignored," which was factually incorrect for the filesystem-URL case (the filesystem branch in `get_default_corpus_backend` consumes the URL through its own factory). Message rewritten to "The URL was used with the default backend resolution. Set ATOMIC_AGENTS_CORPUS_BACKEND explicitly to make the binding clear." The docstring at `doctor.py:2438-2447` aligned to match; the test at `tests/test_corpus_doctor.py` asserts on stable substrings rather than the verbatim wording. Bare `assert wiki_stats is not None` / `assert raw_stats is not None` at `doctor.py:2583-2584` replaced with a defensive conditional that returns `CheckResult(status=FAIL)` on the (logically unreachable) None state, preserving the always-returns-CheckResult contract even under `python -O` optimized builds. A6 follow-through: the legacy direct-read branch in `_load_indexes` is now technically unreachable in production (post-PR-3 `self.corpus_backend` is always non-None because `__init__` always default-resolves via `get_default_corpus_backend`); the branch is retained as a documented safety net for any future refactor that removes the auto-resolve, with its existing OSError soft-degrade + logged warning still in place. The IRON RULE regression tests in `test_corpus_migration_regression.py` exercise the safety-net branch directly by forcing `corpus_backend=None` post-construction. All Round 1 fixes verified by the full test suite at 2888 + 48 skipped (zero regressions). A7 (cascade-layout `corpus_backend._agent_root` vs `instance_root` divergence in bundle rendering) and A8 (potential CLI env-var silent backend switch for operator scripts that assumed filesystem) deferred to PR 4 follow-up backlog per scope discipline; the CLI behavior change is documented under Changed below.

- **SQLiteCorpusBackend Round 2 adversarial review fixes** ([#65](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/65) -- CorpusBackend arc **PR 2 of 4**, second correctness pass). Round 2 hunted IN the Round 1 fix surfaces (the new `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction wrap + snapshot reordering + FTS-inside-transaction + Case 3 compensation) and caught 2 CRITICAL + 2 HIGH + 3 MEDIUM + 2 LOW findings introduced by the Round 1 fixes. R2-C1: the compensation SELECT for the pre-UPSERT row state missed the `title` column; on `atomic_write` failure during a CAS overwrite, the operator's authored title was silently destroyed. Added `title` to the SELECT inside the transaction so compensation preserves it. R2-C2: `atomic_write` does `os.replace()` then `_fsync_dir()`; if the rename succeeds but the post-rename parent-directory fsync raises (rare EIO on the journal device, partial filesystem unwind), the rename has committed and the new body is on disk while compensation restores the old SQL row, producing a permanently inconsistent state. After `atomic_write` raises during a Case 3 overwrite, the backend now re-reads the body file and computes its SHA-256; if the on-disk content matches the incoming content, the rename succeeded and the new SQL row is preserved (the post-rename fsync failure is logged as a warning instead of triggering a destructive rollback). R2-H1: Case 3 compensation switched from `INSERT OR REPLACE` (which rotates SQLite's implicit rowid and forces the FTS5 row to be deleted-and-reinserted with a fresh rowid) to `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT(agent_scope, corpus, name) DO UPDATE SET ...`, matching the initial UPSERT shape and preserving rowid + reusing the same trigger pathway. R2-H2: `restore_version`'s existence check and the inner `write_page` call are in two separate transactions; a concurrent delete (if a `delete_page` primitive ever ships) could fall through to Case 1 fresh-write and bypass the "must exist" guarantee. Added a code comment documenting the race so a future-PR author addresses it when delete lands. R2-M1: `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` now checks `conn.in_transaction` first and raises a clearer `RuntimeError` (instead of letting sqlite3's opaque "cannot start a transaction within a transaction" leak) if a reentrant `write_page` is attempted on the same threading.local connection. R2-M2: when the compensation transaction itself raises (disk full on the SQL journal, FTS5 index damaged), the original `atomic_write` error is no longer the one that surfaces; a `CorpusCorrupted` "manual recovery required" exception is raised that names both the original error and the compensation error, so operators see a loud compound failure instead of a silent log line. R2-L1: dropped a redundant `k != "title"` clause from a filter (since `_KNOWN_FRONTMATTER_FIELDS` already excludes title). R2-L2: `query(top_k=True)` previously slipped past the `isinstance(int)` guard because `bool` is a subclass of `int` in Python; added an explicit `isinstance(top_k, bool)` rejection. 3 new tests added (`test_compensation_preserves_title_on_atomic_write_failure_during_cas`, `test_atomic_write_partial_failure_fsync_only_keeps_new_state`, `test_query_top_k_bool_raises_value_error`). Test suite: 2850 to 2853 passing, zero regressions. Two Round 2 findings deferred to follow-up issues: a pre-existing connection-leak window when `_ensure_schema` raises `CorpusCorrupted` on first connection (the leaked connection isn't tracked in `_all_conns` so `close()` can't reach it), and the Case 2 idempotent-no-op fast-path optimization (currently holds the `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` reserved lock during the disk read for the hash compare; could be hoisted out for a parallel-flood throughput win but is correctness-neutral today).

- **SQLiteCorpusBackend Round 1 adversarial review fixes** ([#65](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/65) -- CorpusBackend arc **PR 2 of 4**, post-merge correctness pass). Addresses 3 CRITICAL + 4 HIGH + 4 MEDIUM/LOW findings from the Opus Round 1 adversarial review. C1: `write_page` now wraps the entire read-validate-UPSERT-FTS sequence in a single `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, eliminating the TOCTOU window between the existence check and the SQL UPSERT that a concurrent writer could exploit. C2: `_sqlite_take_snapshot` signature changed from `body_path: Path` to `body_content: str`; the caller reads the OLD body text from disk before the UPSERT fires and passes it as a string, so the auto-snapshot on CAS overwrite captures the pre-overwrite state rather than the (not-yet-written) new body path. C3: the FTS5 explicit upsert with real body text is now INSIDE the `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction; if FTS indexing fails, the whole transaction rolls back (no SQL row lands, no body file written) -- `supports_full_text_search=True` now means writes index or they fail loudly, with no silent FTS degradation. Disk-write compensation for `atomic_write` failure after COMMIT: Case 1 (fresh write) uses DELETE; Case 3 (CAS overwrite) restores the prior row + FTS entry from state captured before the UPSERT. H4: `_ensure_schema` now raises `CorpusCorrupted` (not `RuntimeError`) on schema version mismatch. H7/H8: `query()` validates `top_k` (must be a non-negative integer; `top_k=None` or negative raises `ValueError`; `top_k=0` short-circuits to `[]`). H11: `restore_version` checks that the target page exists before delegating to `write_page`, raising `CorpusPageNotFound` rather than silently creating a phantom page via the fresh-write path. M1: `_TYPED_FIELDS` / `_NAMED_FM_FIELDS` frozensets lifted to a single module-level `_KNOWN_FRONTMATTER_FIELDS` constant. M2: the `test_busy_timeout_is_set` test now checks the live connection before closing, not after. M3: `import frontmatter as _fm` moved to the top of the module. M4: `_row_to_corpus_page` decodes `frontmatter_json` once instead of twice. 9 new tests added (categories 15-20): `test_busy_timeout_is_set_on_live_connection`, `test_schema_mismatch_raises_corpus_corrupted`, `test_query_top_k_none_raises_value_error`, `test_query_top_k_negative_raises_value_error`, `test_query_top_k_zero_returns_empty_list`, `test_restore_version_missing_page_raises_corpus_page_not_found`, `test_fts5_upsert_failure_rolls_back_write`, `test_cas_overwrite_snapshot_captures_old_content`. Test suite: 2842 to 2850 passing, zero regressions.

- **SQLiteCorpusBackend with FTS5 full-text search** ([#65](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/65) -- CorpusBackend arc **PR 2 of 4**). Operators with large wiki or raw corpora can now pin `ATOMIC_AGENTS_CORPUS_BACKEND=sqlite` to get `O(log N)` indexed full-text search via SQLite FTS5 instead of the linear substring scan in `FilesystemCorpusBackend`. The new backend at `atomic_agents/corpus/sqlite.py` is a hybrid storage shape: SQL metadata and FTS5 virtual table live in a single `.db` file; page bodies and version snapshots live on disk at `<content_root>/<agent_scope>/<corpus>/<name>.md` (same pattern as `SQLiteToolRegistryBackend`'s handler bodies -- keeping the SQL store small and version listing a filesystem glob, not a JOIN). Typed SQL columns for date fields (`captured`, `last_seen`, `expires_at`, `ingested_at`) are re-parsed to `date` / `datetime` on `read_page` (not reconstructed from `frontmatter_json`), so the date round-trip SEV-4 correctness requirement is met end-to-end. FTS5 cross-corpus isolation is enforced via a JOIN back to the `pages` table with `WHERE agent_scope = ? AND corpus = ?` on every query -- FTS match alone cannot isolate by scope/corpus. Every other SQL statement (SELECT, UPDATE, DELETE, INSERT target) carries the same double discriminator (`agent_scope` + `corpus`) as the primary key. The `write_page` guard order follows the SEV-3 sequence from the prep brief: validate name + corpus, check CAS / collision, `safe_resolve_under`, `_enforce_corpus_write_policy`, snapshot (if CAS overwrite), INSERT SQL row, `atomic_write` body -- policy violations never create orphan snapshots. The INSERT-first + `atomic_write`-on-success-only atomicity is the same pattern as `SQLiteToolRegistryBackend` -- a compensating DELETE rolls back the SQL row on disk-write failure so no orphan SQL rows survive a crash. After the disk write succeeds, an explicit FTS5 upsert inserts the real body text (external-content triggers insert `''` for body because triggers cannot read filesystem files; the explicit upsert fixes this so `query()` searches actual page content). FTS5 uses `tokenize='unicode61'` and wraps user-supplied query text in double-quotes for phrase-search mode, doubling internal double-quotes (handles `O'Brien`, `cat"dog`, unterminated quotes, unicode combining, whitespace-only input). The WAL retry loop from `registry/sqlite.py:366-377` (7-attempt exponential backoff, matching on `sqlite_errorcode` not message text) is applied around `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL`, resolving the multi-process cold-start race. `close()` is idempotent: tracks all created connections in a list (TLS is not enumerable), sets a `_closed` flag on first call, and returns immediately on subsequent calls. URL factory `make_sqlite_corpus_backend_from_url` validates 6 distinct error conditions (non-sqlite scheme, netloc present, fragment present, duplicate query parameter, unknown query parameter, empty or root-only path), each wrapped through `_redact_url` so operator-pasted credentials never appear in exception text. `"sqlite"` is registered in the backend registry at import time alongside `"filesystem"`. spec/34 `## SQLiteCorpusBackend storage layout` section updated with the typed-column schema, 3 FTS5 external-content triggers, and the revised 6-site `ValueError` count. 35 SQLite-specific tests in `tests/test_corpus_sqlite_backend.py` (constructor validation, schema init, WAL ordering, FTS5 escaping, hybrid storage failure modes, cross-corpus isolation, URL factory 6 ValueError sites, date round-trip, snapshot guard order) plus the parametrized conformance suite extended from `params=["filesystem"]` to `params=["filesystem", "sqlite"]`, running all 45 conformance test functions against both backends. Test suite: 2762 + 48 skipped (PR 1) to 2842 + 48 skipped, zero regressions.

- **CorpusBackend Protocol scaffolding + FilesystemCorpusBackend reference impl** ([#65](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/65) — CorpusBackend arc **PR 1 of 4**). Operators can now ingest distilled knowledge into `<agent>/wiki/` and source documents into `<agent>/raw/` and have those substrates exposed through a Protocol contract rather than the previous direct filesystem walks at `agent.py:2937-2939` and `bundle.py:_render_memory_breakpoint` (those call sites migrate in PR 3). The new module at `atomic_agents/corpus/` exposes the 12-method `CorpusBackend` Protocol (capabilities + list_pages + read_page + render_index_summary + write_page + list_versions + read_version + restore_version + snapshot + query + stats + close) with `corpus: Literal["wiki", "raw"]` discriminator on every method, 4 dataclasses (`CorpusCapabilities`, `CorpusRef`, `CorpusPage`, `CorpusStats`) at `corpus/types.py`, and the registry surface (`register_corpus_backend` / `unregister_corpus_backend` / `get_corpus_backend` / `list_corpus_backends` / `get_default_corpus_backend`) at `corpus/__init__.py` per the dominant 6-of-10 placement precedent (locks/llm/logs/profile/registry/judge). `FilesystemCorpusBackend(agent_root)` is side-effect-free at construction (lazy walk on first method call, mirroring PersonaBackend's pattern), enforces a charset `[a-zA-Z0-9_.+@-]+` at the API boundary with 5-step path-traversal refusal mirroring `_validate_persona_id` verbatim, routes every page write and version snapshot write through `_io.atomic_write` (no `.write_text()` calls), and ships a `make_filesystem_corpus_backend_from_url("filesystem:///path")` factory with `_redact_url` wrapping every `ValueError` for credential safety. The `write_page()` method ships the documented 4-case behavior table (fresh write / content-identical idempotent no-op / explicit overwrite via CAS / collision raises `CorpusPageExists` or `CorpusPreconditionFailed`); the `query()` method ships the locked capability precedence rule (`supports_semantic_search=True` wins; `supports_full_text_search=True` is the second tier; both False falls back to case-insensitive substring + frontmatter-tag scan). Versioning uses `<agent>/{wiki,raw}/.versions/<page-stem>/<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSffffffZ>_<8hex>.md` matching MemoryBackend's spec/20:228-233 pattern. Raw corpus is **flat-only in v1.0** (nested raw subdirectories are skipped with a debug log per finding R2-NEW-1; the Protocol contract that `list_pages` refs must be resolvable by `read_page` is honored). `read_version()` and `list_versions()` apply `safe_resolve_under` on the decoded `VersionRef.backend_id` components so a crafted `VersionRef` cannot escape the agent root. The CLI gains 5 corpus subcommands (`atomic-agents corpus list / show / query / version / restore`) with zero LLM calls. spec/34 ships as RFC with a draft Implementer Contract (N MUSTs provisional; locks at PR 4 per PersonaBackend precedent). 67 new tests across 3 files (`tests/test_corpus_protocol_conformance.py`, `tests/test_corpus_filesystem_backend.py`, `tests/test_corpus_registry.py`); test suite: 2686 → 2762 passing + 48 skipped, zero regressions. Eleven of twelve backend protocols now have their first PR landed; CorpusBackend (PRs 2-4) and MCPServerRegistryBackend ([#201](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/201)) remain for v1.0 close. Per /office-hours Approach C decision: `SQLiteCorpusBackend` with FTS5 ships in PR 2; `PgvectorCorpusBackend` defers to the #258 Postgres-adapter family for coordinated release alongside `PgvectorMemoryBackend` (semantic-search coverage stays symmetric across both substrates). Methodology track record extended: 4 parallel /plan-subagent prep pass caught 5 SEVERE + 19 HIGH + 7 MEDIUM findings pre-impl (2.3x the 10-arc SEVERE rate); 3 rounds of /ship adversarial review caught + closed 3 CRITICAL bugs pre-merge (WritePolicy not enforced; `read_version` path-traversal; raw corpus nested-file name collision) + 1 R2-introduced MEDIUM bug (orphan snapshot when policy blocks CAS overwrite) + 1 confirmed-empirically MEDIUM bug (date round-trip lossy via `.isoformat()`). All addressed before merge.

- **`pytest-cov` added to dev dependencies** ([#65](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/65) PR 1 of 4 prep). `pyproject.toml`'s `dev` extras gain `pytest-cov>=7.1.0` so /ship's Step 7 coverage audit can produce real line-coverage numbers instead of heuristic estimates. Test command unchanged (`uv run pytest`); coverage runs via `uv run pytest --cov=atomic_agents.corpus` when needed.

- **`doctor.check_mandate_backend` implementation** ([#235](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/235)). Closes the documented-but-unimplemented doctor check that the #124 MandateBackend arc declared but never shipped in `atomic_agents/doctor.py`. The new scope-scoped Protocol-coherence check mirrors `check_policy_backend` and `check_persona_backend`: PASS / WARN / FAIL ladder, capability snapshot in the detail dict (`supports_revocation`, `supports_external_state_change_notification`, `durable`, `supports_crash_recovery`, `mandate_count`, `mandates_md_exists`), URL credential redaction via `urlparse` + `_replace` stripping both username and password from netloc (covers token-as-username managed-service URLs), and WARN-no-opinion path when `mandates.md` is absent at `scope_root` so single-agent home users do not see a noisy FAIL. The check is wired into `run_doctor` between `check_tool_registry_backend` and `check_policy_backend` matching the spec/27 catalogue order, and added to the agent-name-None SKIP enumeration so scope-scoped checks run even when no agent is selected. The probe uses `list_mandates("project:doctor")` (the `project:` kind discards the name component per `atomic_agents/mandate/filesystem.py::_mandates_path`, so this resolves to `<scope_root>/mandates.md` without recursing into per-agent dirs). spec/27's mandate-backend entry has its "Implementation status (2026-05-28)" disclaimer removed (the disclaimer was added in PR #295 pointing at this issue; with the impl now shipped, spec/27 describes shipped behavior again). Adds 5 new tests at `tests/test_doctor_check_mandate_backend.py` covering the WARN-no-mandates-md path, the PASS-with-mandates-md path, the unknown-env-var FAIL path, the URL-credential-redaction FAIL path, and the capability-snapshot-in-detail-dict invariant. CLAUDE.md's MandateBackend lock-paragraph claim "`doctor.check_mandate_backend` validates operator-config coherence" is now true at runtime, not just in docs. Test suite: 2686 → 2691 passing + 48 skipped, zero regressions. Step 11 Opus adversarial: 2 rounds, both converged at "fix and ship; no Round 3 required" after Round 1 fixes folded (1 P1 spec/27 missing WARN-bullet + 2 P2: dual-except collapse + docstring cite swap).

- **Persona snapshot/restore lifecycle.** Operators can version a shared persona record, roll back to an earlier version, list the persona's history, and clone a persona as the starting point for a new one ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) — PersonaBackend arc PR 3 of 4). The new `atomic-agents persona` CLI exposes the full surface: `list`, `show`, `snapshot --label "..."`, `list-snapshots`, `restore`, `clone`. Each subcommand operates against the configured PersonaBackend, defaulting to filesystem under `<scope_root>/.personas/`. Snapshot records live nested inside the persona's own directory at `<personas_root>/<persona_id>/.snapshots/<snapshot_id>/` so removing a persona removes its history cleanly. Snapshot IDs follow the AgentProfile shape `snap_<timestamp>_<12hex>` (48-bit entropy) for cross-Protocol uniformity. `FilesystemPersonaBackend.capabilities().supports_snapshot` flips from `False` to `True` with this release. Closes prep issues [#287](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/287), [#288](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/288), [#289](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/289), [#290](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/290).

### Deprecated

- **`atomic_agents._locks` shim** continues to emit `DeprecationWarning`. Sunset deferred to v1.1; operators using `_locks.AgentLock` or `_locks.acquire()` should migrate to `atomic_agents.locks.get_default_lock_backend(agent_root)` before v1.1 lands.

### Changed

- **`install(spec)` on `HTTPMCPServerRegistryBackend` now rejects literal env values with `ValueError` instead of warning.** Callers MUST pass `MCPServerSpec.env` with unresolved `$VAR` references. This is a v1.0 contract upgrade (Decision A) to prevent the `load_mcp_server -> install` pipeline from accidentally exfiltrating resolved secrets to the catalog server's request body. Operators using `install()` with raw `$VAR` refs see no change.

- **Legacy wiki/INDEX.md direct-read path now soft-degrades on `OSError` + `UnicodeDecodeError` instead of propagating** ([#65](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/65) PR 3 of 4). Pre-PR-3, `AtomicAgent._load_indexes` read `<agent_root>/wiki/INDEX.md` via `Path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")` with no exception handler. A transient `OSError` (permission glitch on a fileserver, NFS handle stale, EACCES from a chmod race) OR a `UnicodeDecodeError` (Latin-1 import, BOM, mixed encodings in operator-authored INDEX) would propagate uncaught and crash `AtomicAgent.__init__`. After PR 3, the legacy branch catches `OSError` (its sibling fallback also catches `UnicodeDecodeError` via the `FilesystemCorpusBackend.render_index_summary` Protocol path) and soft-degrades to an empty wiki section in the system prompt, logging a `wiki_index_unreadable` warning so the degraded state is observable to operators (matching the bundle's pre-existing `_safe_read_text` soft-degrade pattern). **An operator whose wiki/INDEX.md is briefly unreadable now sees a logged warning and an agent missing its wiki context, not a hard crash on construction.** The Protocol path (the common production case after PR 3 default-resolves `corpus_backend` at `__init__`) gets a broad `except Exception` boundary for symmetry: any custom-backend exception (`sqlite3.OperationalError`, `CorpusError`, `KeyError` from a buggy implementer) soft-degrades the same way. Trade-off: a wiki/INDEX.md that becomes silently unreadable could go unnoticed without operator log monitoring. Operators wanting strict-fail behavior should monitor for the `wiki_index_unreadable` log marker.

- **CLI `atomic-agents corpus` subcommands now honor `ATOMIC_AGENTS_CORPUS_BACKEND`** ([#65](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/65) PR 3 of 4). Pre-PR-3, `_cmd_corpus` at `cli.py:887` hardcoded `FilesystemCorpusBackend(agent_root)`. An operator who pinned `ATOMIC_AGENTS_CORPUS_BACKEND=sqlite` and ran `atomic-agents corpus list --corpus wiki` would read from the filesystem wiki, NOT the SQLite backend their agent runtime was actually using. The CLI silently diverged from runtime. After PR 3, the CLI routes through `get_default_corpus_backend(agent_root)` so the same env var resolution applies to both surfaces. **Operators with `ATOMIC_AGENTS_CORPUS_BACKEND=sqlite` exported in their shell environment will see the CLI corpus commands operate against SQLite instead of filesystem.** Operators who want CLI commands forced to filesystem regardless of env can `unset ATOMIC_AGENTS_CORPUS_BACKEND` before invocation or run in a subshell that does not inherit the var. Acceptance is one-way: the pre-PR-3 silent CLI-vs-runtime divergence was the bug; the env-var honoring is the fix.

- **AgentProfile snapshots drop persona text for externally-owned agents** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 3, D3). When an agent's persona is owned by PersonaBackend (signaled by `persona.link.md` on filesystem, or non-null `agents.persona_id` on SQLite), `AgentProfileBackend.snapshot()` now writes empty strings into `persona_identity`, `persona_soul`, `persona_user`. The persona has its own snapshot history via `PersonaBackend.snapshot/restore`; AgentProfile snapshots become config-only snapshots for externally-owned agents. Internally-owned agents see byte-identical behavior. Applies to both `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend` and `SQLiteAgentProfileBackend`.

- **AgentProfile.restore warns when dropping persona text on the migration window** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 3, D-PP-13). When restoring a pre-PersonaBackend snapshot (carrying non-empty persona text) into an agent that is NOW externally owned, the framework drops the snapshot's persona text and emits a one-time `agent_profile_restore_dropped_persona_fields` warning per `(agent_id, snapshot_id)` pair via stdlib logging. Operators wanting the snapshot's persona to win can first remove `persona.link.md` (reverting to legacy ownership), then restore. Dedup uses a thread-safe per-process set.

- **Framework status flipped to "ten backend protocols shipped"** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62), PersonaBackend arc **PR 4 of 4**, closes the arc). The framework's status now reads "ten backend protocols shipped"; only `CorpusBackend` and `MCPServerRegistryBackend` remain for v1.0 close. CLAUDE.md adds the canonical PersonaBackend lock-paragraph mirroring the 9 prior shipped-protocol bullets, flips the inline ASCII architecture diagram (`Persona ✅ (locked at #62 PR 4)`), and updates the "Where things live" table-row spec-doc count from "29 locked + 3 drafts" to "30 locked + 2 drafts". README.md backend-protocols table row flips from "In progress (PR 3 of 4 merged)" to "✅ Shipped" with the lock-paragraph shape; the comparison-matrix and repository-structure locked-docs counts flip from "29 locked docs (+ 3 RFCs)" to "30 locked docs (+ 2 RFCs)"; the spec-doc list parenthetical "(RFC, locks at PR 4 of #62)" on spec/33 drops; the Status block paragraph flips from "Nine of twelve backend protocols shipped" to "Ten of twelve backend protocols shipped". Both the repo-root `ROADMAP.md` (public strategic narrative) and the vault `ROADMAP.md` (project planning canonical) refreshed for the PR 4 lock close: the "Ten of twelve backend protocols have shipped" string, the table-row removal/relocation for the now-shipped #62, the "Two remain" prose, and the `last_review` frontmatter timestamp. Stale "ships in PR 3" temporal markers in `atomic_agents/persona/filesystem.py` docstrings (PersonaCapabilities snapshot trio reference at the class-level + the capability-method docstring) scrubbed to present-tense factual statements, completing the per-PR-marker consolidation sweep across spec body + reference impl + strategic docs.

- **spec/24 line 436 `TemplateProfileBackend` reservation removed** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 4, D5 cross-spec propagation). PR 1's spec/33 RFC documented the retirement (templates are PersonaBackend's domain because they are persona-centric: operators bring their own model and tools to a persona); PR 4 propagates the change to spec/24 itself by deleting the `TemplateProfileBackend` entry from §"Reserved future capabilities". `PersonaCapabilities.supports_templates` is the canonical home for the capability; a future persona-template marketplace (`pip install atomic-personas-starters` or a curated GitHub registry) registers a backend with `supports_templates=True` without forking the framework.

- **Framework status flipped from "ten of twelve" to "eleven of twelve backend protocols shipped"** ([#65](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/65), CorpusBackend arc **PR 4 of 4**, closes the arc). Only `MCPServerRegistryBackend` ([#201](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/201)) remains for v1.0 close. Operators can pin SQLite for indexed FTS5 query at GB scale via one env var (`ATOMIC_AGENTS_CORPUS_BACKEND=sqlite`), fall back to filesystem keyword scan with a doctor WARN that names the remedy at the 1000-page cliff, and trust that no existing `AtomicAgent(...)` construction site changed behavior (IRON RULE byte-identity pinned by 5 explicit regression assertions across 166 sites). The 4-PR arc shipped: Protocol scaffolding + `FilesystemCorpusBackend` (PR 1 / [#297](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/297), ~67 tests), `SQLiteCorpusBackend` with FTS5 (PR 2 / [#298](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/298), ~46 tests through 3 rounds of Opus adversarial), wiring + per-runner kwargs + `delegate.py` explicit-only threading + doctor + IRON RULE regression suite (PR 3 / [#304](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/304), ~35 tests through 3 rounds of Opus adversarial converging at Round 3 LOW), spec/34 LOCK + status flip + doc-release sweep (this PR). Arc total: ~150 net new tests; test suite 2691 to 2937 collected (2889 passing + 48 skipped). CLAUDE.md adds the canonical 11th CorpusBackend lock-paragraph mirroring the 10 prior shipped-protocol bullets, flips the ASCII architecture diagram (`Corpus 🟡` to `Corpus ✅ (locked at #65 PR 4)`), and bumps the spec-doc count from "30 locked + 2 RFCs" to "31 locked + 2 RFCs". README.md backend-protocols table row flips to "✅ Shipped". Both ROADMAPs refreshed (repo-root + vault). Ten follow-up issues filed inline during prep ([#305](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/305) OSError data-loss fallback, [#306](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/306) SQLite connection leak on schema-init failure, [#307](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/307) cascade-layout `corpus_backend._agent_root` divergence gap, [#308](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/308) CLI integration tests, [#309](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/309) title-derivation DRY refactor, [#310](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/310) `read_version` DRY refactor, [#311](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/311) idempotent no-op lock-hoist optimization, [#312](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/312) redundant `CorpusInvalidName` re-raise tuple, [#313](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/313) `UnicodeDecodeError` partial-content test gap, [#314](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/314) `bundle.py:_source_paths` v1.1 Protocol-aware staleness tracking).

- **spec/24 Decision 7 addendum: `CorpusBackend` named as the source of truth for `wiki/` and `raw/`** ([#65](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/65) PR 4 cross-spec propagation). spec/24's existing Decision 7 "Why" paragraph previously said `MemoryBackend` owned `wiki/`, `memory/`, and `journal/`. With CorpusBackend locked, the addendum clarifies: `MemoryBackend` retains exclusive ownership of `memory/` and `journal/`; `CorpusBackend`, when registered, owns `wiki/` and `raw/`. The two backends compose at prompt assembly (`agent.py:_load_indexes()` reads from both). Closes the cross-spec ownership ambiguity that surfaced during PR 3 wiring review.

#### BREAKING

- **`ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP` env-var default flipped from `false` to `true`** ([#89](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/89) — PolicyBackend arc **PR 4 of 4**, closes the arc; spec/32 LOCKED). Operators authoring `policy.md` with tool / MCP-server / model surfaces now see those surfaces **enforce by default**: denied tools produce a synthesized `policy_blocked` `ToolCallResult` (no handler execution); denied MCP servers are filtered before `MCPClientPool` construction (no subprocess spawn); Policy's `get_effective_model()` return replaces the pre-Policy effective model for the LLM call. Cost caps were already enforcing (PR 3a) and are unaffected.

  **What an operator observes:** PR 3b shipped these three surfaces in log-only mode (audit events emitted, action proceeded). On PR 4 merge, an operator whose `policy.md` declares `tools.deny: [delete_file]` and whose agent attempts `delete_file` will see the tool call refused instead of executed; the audit log entry's `enforced` field is now `true`. Identical operator-config text, different runtime behavior.

  **Migration to keep old behavior:** set `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP=false` in the deployment environment. The env-var reader is unchanged — only the no-env-var default flipped. Documented falsy values: `false`, `0`, `no`, `off` (case-insensitive). Any other value (including unset, empty string, whitespace, or unrecognized text) reads as `True` under the new semantic — operators using the env var for an explicit value should review their setting before PR 4 ships.

  **Silent-surprise group: empty-string env vars.** Operators with `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP=""` (e.g., from CI configs that use `export VAR=""` as a "set but no value" idiom, or from explicit-unset patterns) previously read as `False` (log-only) under the pre-PR-4 "documented truthy → True, everything else → False" semantic. Under the PR 4 "documented falsy → False, everything else → True" semantic, empty string reads as `True` (enforce). Same applies to whitespace-only values. Operators in this group will flip from log-only to enforce on PR 4 merge **without any explicit env var change**. To preserve log-only behavior, set the env var to one of the documented falsy values (`false`, `0`, `no`, `off`).

  **Why:** PR 3b's log-only soak window closed the verification gap (operators saw which surfaces their `policy.md` would deny without action-blocking on misconfigured fleet rules). PR 4 turns the lock — the four-PR arc closes the "operator with a fleet" cliff that motivated the entire #89 arc, and the framework's status flips from "eight backend protocols shipped" to **"nine backend protocols shipped"**.

- **spec/32 LOCKED.** `docs/spec/32-policy-backend.md` flipped from RFC to LOCKED at PR 4 of #89. The §"Out of scope" PR 4 deliverable line is removed (no longer out of scope — this PR did it). §"`enforced` field semantics" amended to document the new default (`true` for non-cap surfaces unless `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP=false`). §"Policy decision event schema" now lists `model_from_per_call_override`. New §"Per-call dedup invariant" documents the #273 dedup contract. The 7 normative MUSTs in §"Implementer contract for policy backends" are now final — the provisional footnote is removed. SaaS / Postgres / org-admin-console adapters have a frozen contract to target.

### Fixed

- **Skills lookup on externally-owned agents** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 3, D-PP-12). `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend.list_skills()` and `load_skill_body()` now recognize agents whose persona is externally owned (with `persona.link.md`, no local `persona/IDENTITY.md`). Previously these methods raised `AgentProfileNotFound` because their agent-dir sentinel check predated the D-PP-1 fix from PR 2 that taught `load_profile`, `list_agents`, and `exists` about the shared-persona layout. Completes the D-PP-1 sweep.

- **Snapshot history survives concurrent `save_persona` under contention** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 3 Round 1 adversarial). `FilesystemPersonaBackend._save_persona_group_atomic` now merges the backup directory's `.snapshots/` subtree into the new persona directory entry-by-entry instead of via a single-directory rename. A concurrent `snapshot()` call landing between the persona-dir-replace and the snapshots-restore steps no longer destroys the entire snapshot history with only a warning log. 48-bit-entropy snapshot IDs make filename collisions during the merge probabilistically impossible.

- **`list_snapshots` refuses symlinked entries pointing outside the persona dir** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 3 Round 1 adversarial). Defense-in-depth path-confinement check now matches `restore()`'s guard. Operator-edit-mistakes or hostile filesystem manipulation can no longer surface bogus persona snapshots from outside `<personas_root>/<persona_id>/`.

- **`atomic-agents persona` CLI catches `PersonaError` subclasses + `OSError` / `PermissionError`** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 3 Round 2 adversarial). Operators on a full disk, read-only filesystem, or under group-atomic-rename retry exhaustion now see a one-line `Error: <message>` on stderr with exit 1 instead of a Python traceback. Covers `PersonaCorrupted`, `PersonaLinkInvalid`, `PersonaOwnershipConflict`, and any future `PersonaError` subclass.

- **Migration-window event dedup is now atomic under concurrent restore** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 3 Round 1 adversarial). `AgentProfileBackend.restore`'s `_warned_restore_drop` check + add is wrapped in a `threading.Lock` per backend instance, restoring the "exactly once per `(agent_id, snapshot_id)` per process" promise D-PP-13 stated. Save-side D-PP-8 dedup keeps its existing shape; the asymmetry is documented + filed as follow-up [#291](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/291).

### Documentation

- **spec/33 (Persona Backend RFC) gains snapshot-layout + ID-format sections** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 3, D-PP-10 + D-PP-11). New §"Snapshot storage layout" documents the nested `<personas_root>/<persona_id>/.snapshots/<snapshot_id>/` shape. New Implementer Contract MUST #8 documents the `snap_<YYYY-MM-DDTHHMMSS>_<12hex>` format and `metadata.json` schema, matching spec/24 Implementer Contract #8 for cross-Protocol uniformity.

- **spec/24 Implementer Contract #8 + the line 85 example drop a stale `+TZ` annotation** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 3 Round 2 adversarial) that diverged from the actual `strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H%M%S')` implementation across all three snapshot Protocols (AgentProfile filesystem + sqlite, Persona filesystem). Line 85 example also corrected from `<6hex>` to `<12hex>` to match the 48-bit entropy budget. Eliminates cross-Protocol drift; future Postgres / SaaS adapters reading spec/24 now mirror the actual format.

- **spec/33 LOCKED at PR 4** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) PR 4, closes the arc). `docs/spec/33-persona-backend.md` flipped from RFC to LOCKED. ### Added

- **PersonaBackend Protocol scaffolding** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) — PersonaBackend protocol arc **PR 1 of 4**). New `atomic_agents/persona/` module ships the `PersonaBackend` `@runtime_checkable` Protocol surface (9 methods: `load_persona`, `save_persona`, `list_personas`, `exists`, `clone`, `snapshot`, `restore`, `list_snapshots`, `capabilities` + `backend_id` class attribute), the `FilesystemPersonaBackend(personas_root)` reference impl, the registry primitives (`register_persona_backend`, `get_persona_backend`, `list_persona_backends`, `unregister_persona_backend` per D9 fold #3 fixture hygiene, `get_default_persona_backend(scope_root)` honoring `ATOMIC_AGENTS_PERSONA_BACKEND` + optional `ATOMIC_AGENTS_PERSONA_BACKEND_URL` env vars), the `make_filesystem_persona_backend_from_url("filesystem:///path")` URL factory with credential redaction across all `ValueError` sites, and the three frozen dataclasses (`Persona`, `PersonaSnapshot`, `PersonaCapabilities`). Storage layout `<personas_root>/<persona_id>/{IDENTITY,SOUL,USER}.md` plus `metadata.json` sidecar (`schema_version: 1`, version, label, created_at). `persona_id` charset `[a-zA-Z0-9_.+@-]+` enforced at API boundary with path-traversal / control-char / newline / leading-dot / empty-string refusal. Side-effect-free construction (no filesystem walk in `__init__`; lazy read on first method call) so the 166 existing `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites stay byte-identical when no `persona.link.md` exists. Six persona exceptions in `atomic_agents/exceptions.py` per D-PI-1 (NOT in `persona/types.py`): `PersonaError`, `PersonaNotFound`, `PersonaExists`, `PersonaCorrupted`, `PersonaSnapshotNotFound`, `PersonaOwnershipConflict`, `PersonaLinkInvalid`. Cross-module placement matches the `AgentProfileNotFound` / `ToolNotInRegistry` convention because `PersonaOwnershipConflict` is raised by profile backends (PR 2 wiring) and `PersonaLinkInvalid` by the `persona_link_md.py` parser (PR 2 per D-ER-3).

  **`save_persona` is group-atomic.** All four persona files write to a sibling temp directory via `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory`; the temp dir is then renamed into place atomically. `overwrite=False` claims the dir via `mkdir(exist_ok=False)` for race-free fresh-create (concurrent writers: exactly one wins, others raise `PersonaExists`). `overwrite=True` uses swap-and-delete with a 20-iteration retry loop sized for 16-thread contention on macOS APFS `ENOTEMPTY` semantics (validated clean across 100 trials at 16 threads). Round 3 closed an orphan-backup leak on contended overwrite=True via best-effort `shutil.rmtree(backup, ignore_errors=True)` on the rename-restore failure path. `load_persona` and `list_personas` both call `atomic_agents._io.safe_resolve_under()` on every persona file to defend against symlinks planted in `personas_root` pointing outside (matches CLAUDE.md aesthetic "Path traversal refused per `_io.safe_resolve_under`"). `load_persona` raises `PersonaCorrupted` (distinct from `PersonaNotFound`) when `metadata.json` is unparseable JSON, missing a required key, has an unsupported `schema_version`, or contains non-int / non-UTF8 bytes.

  **Spec/33 RFC** ships at `docs/spec/33-persona-backend.md` documenting the Protocol surface, capability semantics, D2a ownership rule (`persona.link.md` presence triggers PersonaBackend ownership; conflict with legacy `<agent>/persona/IDENTITY.md` raises `PersonaOwnershipConflict` at profile load), D-ER-4 format (YAML in code block with `kind: shared` + `persona_id: customer-support-v3` — the colon-prefixed `shared:customer-support-v3` was rejected at /plan-eng-review because the colon violates the `persona_id` charset from D4), D-ER-2 threading category (`delegate.py` threads `persona_backend` ONLY when explicitly supplied, mirroring Policy's `_policy_backend_was_explicit` precedent at `agent.py:401`; default-resolved PersonaBackends do not leak coordinator's `personas_root` to delegates), D-ER-1 ownership-check method (`AgentProfileBackend.is_persona_externally_owned(agent_id) -> bool` lands in PR 2), D-ER-3 parser placement (`persona_link_md.py` lands in PR 2 alongside composition wiring), D5 TemplateProfileBackend retirement (`supports_templates` capability field on `PersonaCapabilities` retires spec/24 line 436's reservation; templates are PersonaBackend's domain), and the 7 normative MUSTs in §"Implementer contract for persona backends" marked DRAFT (finalized at PR 4 lock). spec/33 status flips RFC → LOCKED at PR 4.

  **Methodology** (CLAUDE.md taste rule 11; Codex skipped per standing project rule, methodology flip commit `ec3dfa5`): /office-hours scope-design (2026-05-22) locked D1-D7 + D1a + D2a (premise 1: ship PersonaBackend as a separate Protocol over subsuming into AgentProfile — architectural merit overrode cost framing once `feedback_atomic_agents_best_not_cheapest` flagged the cost anchor). /plan-eng-review (2026-05-25) locked D-ER-1 through D-ER-5 and tightened D1a's SQLite v1→v2 race-loser handling for PR 2 (`catch sqlite3.OperationalError "duplicate column name" + re-read schema_version`). Pre-impl prep pass (2026-05-26) caught 2 HIGH pre-implementation risks before code was written (exception placement -> D-PI-1 amendment; D-ER-4 syntax inconsistency in 4 doc spots -> design + test plan amendments). Three rounds of Opus adversarial review post-implementation: Round 1 surfaced 2 P0 + 4 P1 + 4 P2 (dead `_url_factory_registry` extension surface; metadata corruption error type; `@property` vs class attribute asymmetry; TOCTOU race; per-file vs group-atomic save; list_personas half-written-dir filter; `_redact_url` path false-positive; missing `isinstance` test; metadata `schema_version`). Round 2 caught what Round 1's fix commit introduced (concurrent `overwrite=True` `FileNotFoundError` race at 50-60% reproduction with 16 threads; regular-file-coercion; symlink-followed sentinel; stale docstring; `schema_version` type discipline; missing-file test symmetry). Round 3 reached convergence (P0=0, P1=0; only 1 P2 orphan-leak fix + 1 dead-code removal + 1 spec text addition). Five `fix(persona):` commits document the adversarial trail.

  **Tests:** 2404 baseline + 125 new = 2529 passing, 34 skipped. Zero regressions on the 166 existing `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites. Coverage 92% (P1+ gaps closed; remaining gaps are P3 follow-ups). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` both pass. Conformance suite parametrized across `FilesystemPersonaBackend` + in-memory `MockPersonaBackend` (registered under `"mock"` via `mock_registered` fixture, unregistered in teardown per D9 fold #3).

  **Zero behavior change.** `AtomicAgent.__init__` signature unchanged in PR 1. PersonaBackend activates only when `persona.link.md` is present (PR 3 introduces the trigger file format). The module registers at import via `register_persona_backend("filesystem", FilesystemPersonaBackend)` but no call site consumes it — PR 2 wires the bootstrap path with the `AtomicAgent(..., persona_backend=...)` kwarg, per-runner kwargs on `OutcomeRunner` / `EvalRunner` / `DreamRunner`, and `delegate.py` explicit-only threading per D-ER-2. PR 3 ships consumption (snapshot trio impl on `FilesystemPersonaBackend` flipping `supports_snapshot=True`; persona-only CLI subcommands; `AgentProfileBackend.snapshot/restore` drops persona fields when externally owned per D3). PR 4 spec-locks. The framework's status flips from "nine backend protocols shipped" to "ten backend protocols shipped" at PR 4 close.

  **Next slice:** PR 2 of 4 wires `AtomicAgent` kwarg threading, `AgentProfileBackend.is_persona_externally_owned()` Protocol method, `AgentProfileBackend.load_profile/save_profile` composition, `persona_link_md.py` parser, SQLite v1→v2 migration in `profile/sqlite.py`, and `doctor.check_persona_backend`. Per D-ER-5 test split, PR 2 ships ~50 tests (15-20 cross-Protocol composition + 10 SQLite migration + 15 parser tests). Arc test count target ~150 across all 4 PRs.

- **`PolicyDecision.model_from_per_call_override`** ([#274](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/274)). The model-selection emission now carries the `agent.call(model=...)` kwarg value when Policy supersedes it, so the caller can distinguish "Policy overrode `model.md`" from "Policy overrode the operator's explicit per-call choice." Populated only when the kwarg is supplied and the event is `axis="model_selection"`; absent otherwise (the emission helper drops `None` values from the JSONL `extra` dict so PR 3b consumers see the same shape they already pinned). `AtomicAgent.call()` docstring documents the fleet-config-wins precedence per spec/32 §"Composition math". Lands alongside the PR 4 flag flip — without the kwarg-audit field, the new default would silently override per-call kwargs with no caller-detectable signal.

- **End-to-end LLM-mocked tests for the three Policy non-cap consumption sites** ([#275](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/275)). The PR 3b coverage audit returned 71% — the load-bearing call-site control-flow branches inside `agent.call()` were simulated in isolation, not exercised through real `agent.call()` invocations. PR 4 closes the gap with `tests/test_policy_noncap_integration.py` (12 new tests). Each consumption site (tool dispatch, MCP discovery, model selection) is exercised under both flag states (log-only AND enforce); negative pins cover `pol_snap is None` (no `policy.md`), model-override suppression when Policy agrees with `model.md`, and the per-call dedup invariant. The flag flip is now a config change, not a behavior debut.

- **PolicyBackend non-cap consumption sites in log-only mode + `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP` env flag** ([#89](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/89) — PolicyBackend protocol arc **PR 3b of 4**). The three remaining policy surfaces (tool allowlist, MCP server allowlist, model override) now consult the per-call Policy snapshot at their consumption sites inside `agent.call()` — but ship in **log-only mode** by default. Denials emit `policy_decision` events with `enforced=False` and the action proceeds; operators can verify policy correctness in production before PR 4 flips the env-var default to `True`. Cost caps remain unaffected (PR 3a path enforces immediately; the new env-var only governs the three non-cap surfaces per spec/32 D3).

  **Three changes inside `_take_policy_snapshot`:**
    - Populates the previously-stub `tool_allow_fn` and `mcp_allow_fn` closures. Each closure binds the backend reference + agent name at snapshot time via default-arg capture, AND carries a per-snapshot result cache (`_cache: dict[str, bool] = {}`) so a multi-turn loop consulting the same tool/server name N times queries the backend ONCE per snapshot. This makes Premise 3's "frozen at call entry" promise visible at the implementation level — without memoization, the closures would re-stat `policy.md` on every consultation. Fresh cache dict per snapshot (default-arg evaluates per-call of the enclosing function), so caches cannot leak across calls.
    - Resolves `model_override` once at snapshot time via `get_effective_model(self.name)` (per design Premise 3 — model selection doesn't change inside a call).
    - Reads `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP` once via `_read_enforce_noncap_flag()` and stores on `PolicySnapshotForCall.enforce_noncap`. The env-var value is frozen for the duration of the call. Recognized truthy values: `1`/`true`/`yes`/`on` (case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant); anything else (including `0`/`false`/unset) reads as `False`.

  **Three consumption sites in `agent.call()`:**
    - **Tool dispatch** (inside the multi-turn loop, after judge_blocked / judge_deferred checks, before `tool_registry.execute(tu)`): consult `snap.tool_allow_fn(tu["name"])`. On deny, emit `PolicyDecision(decision_kind="deny", axis="tool_allowlist", enforced=snap.enforce_noncap)`. Flag-off: emission happens, control flow falls through to execute. Flag-on (PR 4 default): synthesize a `policy_blocked` ToolCallResult mirroring the judge_blocked shape so the LLM sees the refusal on the next turn, append to results, continue (no actual handler execution).
    - **MCP discovery** (before `MCPClientPool` construction): filter `self.config.mcp_servers` through `snap.mcp_allow_fn(spec.name)`. On deny, emit `PolicyDecision(decision_kind="deny", axis="mcp_allowlist")`. Flag-off: denied server still connects (audit-only). Flag-on: denied server is excluded from `effective_mcp_specs` and never spins up (subprocess cost saved). When `effective_mcp_specs` ends up empty, the `MCPClientPool` isn't constructed at all.
    - **Model selection** (after pre-policy effective model resolves from cost-cap fallback / per-call kwarg / `model.md` default): compare `snap.model_override` against `self.config.default_model`. On disagreement, emit `PolicyDecision(decision_kind="override", denying_layer=None, axis="model_selection", model_from_md=<self.config.default_model>, model_from_policy=<snap.model_override>)`. Flag-off: pre-policy model used, emission is audit-only. Flag-on: Policy's choice replaces the pre-policy model. Per Round 1 P0-2 fix, `model_from_md` is the literal `model.md` value, not the post-cost-cap-fallback effective model — operators reading the audit want the `model.md` vs Policy contrast, not the `model.md+fallback` vs Policy contrast (cost-cap fallback decisions have their own `policy_decision` event family from PR 3a).

  **Two-round Opus adversarial review** (CLAUDE.md taste rule 11; Codex skipped per standing project rule, per CLAUDE.md status block + commit `ec3dfa5` methodology flip).

  *Round 1 findings (all fixed inline):*
    - **P0-1 closure memoization:** the docstring promised "frozen at call entry" but the closures re-stat `policy.md` on every consultation. Fix: per-snapshot result cache via default-arg dict.
    - **P0-2 model_from_md audit honesty:** the emitted `model_from_md` field carried the post-cost-cap-fallback model, not the actual `model.md` value. Fix: capture `self.config.default_model` directly, compare against it for the emit condition.
    - **P1-1 enforce-mode test coverage:** the test file covered log-only mode + snapshot machinery but not the PR-4-default enforce branches. Fix: 6 new tests covering MCP filter behavior under both flag states, model override under both, policy_blocked ToolCallResult shape, closure memoization.
    - **P1-2 decision_kind="override" docstring drift in log-only mode:** spec/32 line 244 says `decision_kind="override"` means "Policy override applied without violation" — but in log-only mode the override does NOT apply. Fix: docstring on `PolicyDecision` clarifies the `enforced=False` disambiguation for both `"deny"` and `"override"`.
    - **P2-1 module-level logger:** closures used `import logging` inline. Fix: hoist to module-level `_logger` (already imported at agent.py:17).
    - **P2-3 fail-open test rigor:** the test asserted closures swallow per-call backend exceptions but didn't pin warning level or that the exception class name appears in the log message. Fix: assert `RuntimeError` in log messages + `rec.levelno == logging.WARNING` filtered to `name == "atomic_agents.agent"`.
    - **Comment fix at the model-selection site** clarifying log-only vs enforce semantics (Round 1 noted the existing comment was misleading in log-only context).

  *Round 2 findings (Round 2's load-bearing job — catching what Round 1's fix code introduced):*
    - Round 2 verified Round 1 fixes were structurally sound: the mutable-default-arg cache pattern is safe under nested-`def` semantics (fresh closure per outer call → fresh cache dict); format-string `%s` count matches arg count at all four warning sites; `PolicySnapshotForCall` only has two instantiation sites in the codebase, both compatible with the new field default; `MCPServerSpec` import path stable; spec/32 docstring matches `PolicyDecision.decision_kind` docstring after Round 1's P1-2 edit.
    - Round 2 surfaced 2 new P1s, both filed as follow-up issues per scope discipline (non-blocking for PR 3b log-only mode since the flag ships `False`):
      - [#273](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/273) — tool-allowlist emission amplification in log-only mode. An LLM that doesn't see refusals will re-attempt a denied tool on each iteration of the multi-turn loop; current implementation emits a `policy_decision` event every consultation, not once per denied name per call. Closure memoization suppresses BACKEND calls but not emissions. Should land before PR 4 flips the default.
      - [#274](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/274) — model-selection emission semantics for the per-call `model` kwarg. When `agent.call(work_item, model="X")` is supplied AND Policy disagrees, the audit records `model_from_md=<config.default_model>` rather than the kwarg layer — and in PR-4 enforce mode, Policy silently overrides the kwarg with no audit hook the caller can detect.
    - Round 2 also caught two test-hygiene P2s (landed inline): stale line-number references in test docstrings (Round 1 fix-commit shifted line numbers ~30 lines) and an overly-broad `caplog` WARNING-level assertion that captured records from any logger (fixed by filtering to `name == "atomic_agents.agent"`).

  **Three additional follow-up issues filed during Round 1 review** (not blockers; ship-then-track per scope discipline):
    - [#271](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/271) — MCP source-server correlation gap. In log-only mode, an operator sees `policy_decision: mcp_allowlist denied server-X` followed by `tool_call: scrape_pages` and can't tell whether `scrape_pages` came from `server-X`. Extending the `tool_call` emission with a `policy_log_only_source_denied` field requires tracking MCP-server → tool-name lineage at dispatch time.
    - [#272](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/272) — `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP` typo silently parses as `False`. A typo like `enbled` reads as log-only mode with no warning. Should warn on unrecognized values at module-first-read.
    - [#275](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/275) — agent.call() end-to-end coverage for the three consumption sites. The PR 3b test file covers snapshot machinery + emission shapes + closure memoization + fail-open semantics + env-flag reader (~71% by branch count, ~85% weighted by load-bearing-ness per the /ship coverage audit) but simulates the call-site filter logic rather than exercising it through a mocked LLM round-trip. Auditor's recommended next step before PR 4 flips the default to True.

  **Zero behavior change** for operators WITHOUT `policy.md` — snapshot's `tool_allow_fn` / `mcp_allow_fn` closures return `True` for every name (FilesystemPolicyBackend default-allow), `model_override` is `None`, `enforce_noncap` defaults to `False`. The three consumption sites guard on `pol_snap.tool_allow_fn is not None` / `pol_snap.mcp_allow_fn is not None` / `pol_snap.model_override is not None` and skip when absent. Existing cap-check arithmetic preserved verbatim (PR 3a regression pin in the new test file confirms cost-cap emissions ignore the `ENFORCE_NONCAP` flag in both states). The 115 existing `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites + every test in the suite continues to produce identical pre-PR-3b results. Test count: 2347 → **2384 passing** (+37 net: 35 PR 3b tests + a few unrelated additions from intervening commits) + 34 skipped. New `tests/test_policy_noncap_log_only.py` covers: env-flag reader (truthy/falsy/whitespace parametrized), snapshot extension (populates closures + model_override + reads env at entry), no-policy.md no-opinion, snapshot frozen against backend reassignment, fail-open on backend error with logging assertions, emission shapes for all three new axes, MCP filter behavior under both flag states, model override under both flag states, policy_blocked ToolCallResult shape, closure memoization per-snapshot, cost-cap regression pin (flag does not leak into PR 3a path).

  **Next slice:** PR 4 of 4 flips the `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP` env-var default from `False` to `True`, locks spec/32, status flip to "nine backend protocols shipped". Coverage gap #275 (agent.call() integration tests under both flag states) should land alongside or before the flag flip so PR 4 is a config change, not a behavior debut.

- **Public `ROADMAP.md` at repo root** ([#93](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/93) — post-flip launch shape). Active-backlog-only scope per the #93 office-hours design-doc D1 decision: 4 remaining v1.0 backend protocols (`PolicyBackend` in progress, `PersonaBackend`/`CorpusBackend`/`MCPServerRegistryBackend` planned), Tier 2 scaling unlocks (multi-tenant deployment shape, semantic memory retrieval, agent-as-package, skill marketplace, conformance test suite), QoL primitives, and a "What we won't build" callout with reasoning. The vault-side strategic ROADMAP (with personal-context paragraphs + retrospectives) stays vault-only; the public version is the operator-facing roadmap.

- **PolicyBackend cost-cap consumption + PolicySnapshot per-call machinery + MandateCheck integration + `policy_decision` event emission** ([#89](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/89) — PolicyBackend protocol arc **PR 3a of 4**). Cost caps are now LIVE — operators authoring `<project_root>/policy.md` with `cost_caps.daily_usd: 50` see fleet caps enforce on real money from this PR forward (closes the operator-multi-agent pain that was most acute today: fleet operators hand-syncing `model.md` budgets across N agents). PR 3b ships the non-cap surfaces (tool/MCP/model) in log-only mode behind `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP=false` flag — separate PR. **Three bisectable commits:**

  - **(1) Schema hygiene + validator + spec/32 amendments.** Per Dan's locked NEW DECISIONS resolved during plan-subagent prep: (D1) DEFER `cumulative_usd` cap dimension to v1.1 — dropped from `CostCaps`, `PolicyDecision.cap_dimension` Literal, `PolicySnapshot`, `AgentPolicyOverride`, parser, conformance tests, and spec/32 prose. Policy v1 ships with daily + monthly only, matching what `model.md cost_guardrails` already supports. (D2) LOOSEN `_validate_agent_name` to permit `[a-zA-Z0-9._@+-]+` (was `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+` strictly) — preserves existing operator deployments using dots / at-signs / plus / etc. in agent names; still rejects path-traversal tokens, control chars, newlines, leading dots, empty. (F6 plan-subagent finding) ADD `enforced: bool = False` field to `PolicyDecision` — semantics: `enforced=True` means denial blocked the action / override took effect; `enforced=False` means log-only (denial recorded but action proceeded). Cost-cap denials always emit `enforced=True`. PR 3b ships non-cap surfaces in log-only mode. Spec/32 §"Policy decision event schema" updated; new §"`enforced` field semantics" + §"`agent_name` character set" + §"Denying layer resolution" sections.

  - **(2) Cascade-aware policy.md scoping (closes [#236](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/236)).** PR 2's wiring used `self.agents_root` as the default scope_root. In cascaded layouts (`<system>/projects/<project>/agents/<role>/`), `self.agents_root` resolves to `<system>/projects/<project>/agents/` — NOT `cascade.project_root` (`<system>/projects/<project>/`). For cascade operators relying on defaults, PR 3 consumption would have silently missed the operator's policy.md, breaking the cost-cap-always-enforce promise for cascade deployments. The fix: `_init_policy_backend` now uses `cascade.project_root if self.cascade is not None else self.agents_root` as the default scope_root. Operator-supplied `policy_backend=...` kwarg STILL wins (programmatic-override discipline). `doctor.check_policy_backend` adds a cascade-aware warning per F12: when agent is in cascade layout AND `agent.policy_backend._project_root != cascade.project_root` (filesystem backend only; skip for SaaS/Postgres), emit WARN with remediation message. New tests pin: cascade layout uses cascade.project_root; non-cascade preserves PR 2 behavior; doctor warns on cascade scope mismatch when operator supplies explicit FilesystemPolicyBackend(agents_root).

  - **(3) Cost-cap consumption — the body.** New `PolicySnapshotForCall` frozen dataclass in `atomic_agents/policy/types.py` — per-call snapshot taken at `agent.call()` entry per design Premise 3 ("snapshot at call entry; consulted from snapshot for rest of call"). The snapshot is FROZEN for the duration of the call — operator edits to `policy.md` mid-call are deferred to the NEXT call (predictability over freshness within the call). `_take_policy_snapshot()` helper on AtomicAgent constructs the snapshot from the backend; called at `call()` entry alongside the other `_*_this_run` resets; cleared in finally block. **`_check_cost_guardrails` integration:** Policy daily + monthly caps MIN-compose with `model.md cost_guardrails` per-dimension (D2 — daily and monthly compose independently). When the cap fires due to Policy contributing, emit a `PolicyDecision` event with `denying_layer="policy"`, `axis="cost_cap"`, `cap_dimension="daily|monthly"`, populated `attempted_value` + `effective_cap`, `enforced=True`, `cache_ttl_s` from the snapshot. Tiebreaker per F14: when Policy + model.md tie, `_resolve_denying_layer` picks "policy" (most-fleet first; tiebreaker order policy > model_md). New helpers in `agent.py`: `_min_or_other(a, b)` (None-aware MIN — None means "no opinion at this layer"), `_resolve_denying_layer(...)` (the tiebreak resolver). **MandateCheck integration (F7):** `MandateCheck.__init__` accepts new `policy_effective_caps: CostCaps | None = None` kwarg. Steps 7 (token budget) + 8 (external budget) MIN-compose mandate's per-cap-kind values with Policy's daily + monthly. `agent.py:_ensure_mandate_check` passes `policy_effective_caps=self._policy_snapshot_this_call.effective_caps` so MandateCheck steps 7-8 share the same arithmetic as `_check_cost_guardrails`. **`policy_decision` event emission infrastructure:** `PRIMITIVE_POLICY_DECISION = "policy_decision"` added to `logs/types.py`; `_emit_policy_decision(decision, log_backend, run_id)` helper in `policy/types.py` builds a `RunRecord` from the `PolicyDecision` and calls `log_backend.append(record)`. Wire shape per spec/32 §"PolicyDecision LogBackend envelope": `primitive="policy_decision"`, `trigger="policy_decision"`, `extra=<all PolicyDecision fields>`. Operators query: `LogQuery(primitive="policy_decision")` or filter by `extra["decision_kind"]` for deny vs override. **New `tests/test_policy_cost_cap_consumption.py`** (12 tests): snapshot lifecycle (none-before-call, take-returns-correct-shape, no-policy.md is no-opinion), MIN composition helpers (`_min_or_other`, `_resolve_denying_layer` tiebreak), MandateCheck kwarg surface (accepts + defaults), `policy_decision` emission shape (deny + override kinds, RunRecord envelope), end-to-end snapshot drives effective cap, no-policy.md zero-behavior-change pin.

  **Plan-subagent pre-impl review:** 18 findings (5 SEVERE + 7 HIGH + 4 MED + 2 LOW). Surfaced 3 NEW architectural decisions for Dan (cumulative cap dimension v1 OR defer; agent_name validator strict OR loosened; PR 3 single OR split into 3a+3b) — all 3 resolved before impl. F1 cascade scoping was the highest-stakes finding (would have silently broken cost-cap-always-enforce for cascade operators); folded into commit 2. F2 PolicySnapshot per-call machinery was THE load-bearing structural finding (Premise 3 was unimplementable as-written without a per-call attribute); resolved via the `_policy_snapshot_this_call` attribute pattern matching `_helpers_this_run`/`_delegations_this_run`/`_mandate_allowed_proposals` precedents. F7 MandateCheck integration resolved via kwarg threading (clean — re-instantiate not required because MandateCheck constructor is cheap + the kwarg-store + step-7/8 read pattern stays linear).

  **Methodology that compounded:** plan-subagent dispatch (Sonnet, ~7-min wall-clock) caught risks worth 3+ re-architecture cycles. Two prior PR-3a implementation subagents hit session limits mid-impl (each completing partial work — schema + wiring — before timing out); third subagent picked up the integration; final inline pass wrote the dedicated tests + CHANGELOG + commit. The methodology track record holds: subagent dispatch works best for clearly-bounded scopes; when an impl spans 1500+ LOC across 5 files, the safer pattern is splitting (D3's PR 3a + 3b split was driven by this exact observation).

  **Zero behavior change** for operators WITHOUT `policy.md` — snapshot has all-None `CostCaps()`, `_min_or_other(model_md_value, None)` returns `model_md_value` unchanged, existing cap-check arithmetic preserved. The 115 existing `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites + every test in the suite continues to produce identical pre-PR-3a results. Test count: 2323 → **2344 passing** (+21 net: ~9 from commits 1-2 + 12 from commit 3) + 34 skipped.

  **#235** — pre-existing `check_mandate_backend` regression from #124 arc; surfaced during PR 2 plan-subagent prep; NOT addressed in PR 3a (scope discipline).
