  **Next slice:** PR 3b ships tool dispatch + MCP discovery + model selection consumption in log-only mode behind `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP=false` flag. PR 4 flips the flag default + locks spec/32 + status flip to "nine backend protocols shipped".

- **`PolicyBackend` wired through `AtomicAgent.__init__` + per-runner kwargs + delegate threading + `doctor.check_policy_backend`** ([#89](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/89) — PolicyBackend protocol arc **PR 2 of 4**). The Protocol surface shipped in PR 1 is now consumed by the framework's bootstrap path. `AtomicAgent.__init__` accepts a new keyword-only `policy_backend: PolicyBackend | None = None` kwarg; default resolves via `get_default_policy_backend(self.agents_root)` honoring the `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_BACKEND` env var. **`self.agents_root`** is the scope (NOT `self.agent_root` like Mandate) because Policy is fleet-shaped: a single `policy.md` at the project root applies to all agents per spec/32 §"Locked Answer — policy.md Placement" (the wrong choice would have silently no-opted every Policy lookup in PR 3 consumption). Public `self.policy_backend` attribute mirrors `self.log_backend` / `self.profile_backend` / `self.tool_registry_backend` / `self.mandate_backend`. Per-runner kwargs added to `OutcomeRunner` + `EvalRunner` + `DreamRunner` (DreamRunner stores for API parity but doesn't construct internal AtomicAgents — same shape as the other agent-internal backends). **`delegate.py` threads `policy_backend=self.policy_backend` per /plan-eng-review D1** — the coordinator's PolicyBackend instance flows into delegate-target AtomicAgent construction by reference. This is **the second fleet-scoped backend that threads to delegates** (Profile was the first; Lock + Log + ToolRegistry + Mandate are per-agent and deliberately don't thread). Without threading, an operator's custom PolicyBackend (Postgres SaaS, org-admin-console) would be bypassed in delegated work, defeating the entire purpose of fleet-wide Policy. `doctor.check_policy_backend` coherence check landed in `doctor.py` with PASS/WARN/FAIL ladder + capability snapshot (reads `cache_ttl_s` + `durable` from `backend.capabilities()` directly per plan-subagent F7 — never hard-coded; honest if a SaaS backend reports cache_ttl_s=60) + URL credential redaction inline (mirrors the 4-prior-check pattern; `password OR username` check covers token-as-username managed-service URLs) + `policy_md_exists: bool` informational signal in detail dict. PASS when backend resolves + policy.md exists; WARN when backend resolves but policy.md is absent (operator hasn't authored Policy yet — informational); FAIL on `BackendNotRegistered` (typo'd env var) or capability-probe failure. Wired into `run_doctor` as a per-agent check + added to the no-agent SKIP enumeration. `render_human`'s badge dict gained the missing `WARN: "[warn]"` entry (pre-existing latent bug — other checks emit WARN but the test surface hadn't triggered the render path until `check_policy_backend` did). New `tests/test_policy_integration.py` (14 integration tests) covers default-resolution, explicit-kwarg-wins, env-var dispatch, public-attribute discipline, explicit-None triggers default, zero-behavior-change for existing construction sites, all 3 runners' kwarg threading via the established monkeypatch-AtomicAgent-class pattern (captures kwargs at the boundary — canonical defense against the kwarg-drop trap), DreamRunner API-parity storage-only, **delegate identity-threading test** (`target.policy_backend is coordinator.policy_backend` — SAME INSTANCE, not just same type; the load-bearing D1 assertion per plan-subagent F3 — a future contributor refactoring `delegate()` who drops the threading would still pass an `isinstance` check, but the `is` identity test catches the regression loudly), two-agents-share-no-state isolation pin, doctor PASS/FAIL/WARN paths. Class-level type annotation `policy_backend: PolicyBackend` added to `AtomicAgent` for static-analysis narrowing — without this, mypy/pyright would narrow `agent.policy_backend` to the concrete `FilesystemPolicyBackend` default rather than the `PolicyBackend` Protocol, breaking SaaS/Postgres/org-admin-console downstream type-checking. **Zero behavior change** for existing operator deployments — `self.policy_backend` exists on every constructed AtomicAgent but NO call site reads it (PR 3 wires consumption via `_check_cost_guardrails` MIN composition + tool dispatch site + MCP discovery site + model selection site + `policy_decision` event emission behind `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP=false` flag). All 115 `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites in the test suite see byte-identical pre-PR-2 behavior. Test count: 2309 → **2323 passing** (+14 net) + 34 skipped. Parallelization: 4 Sonnet sub-agents on independent files (agent.py + runners + doctor + tests) completed in ~3 min wall-clock vs ~45 min sequential; integration phase caught 2 issues (doctor's `badge` dict missing `WARN` key — pre-existing latent; Lane B's TYPE_CHECKING block placement in `eval.py` was structurally identical to the pre-existing pattern in `outcome.py`/`dream.py` so no E402 regression). Plan-subagent pre-impl review caught 3 SEVERE + 7 HIGH + 3 MED + 1 LOW with ZERO new architectural decisions — all 14 folded into the lane briefs before dispatch. Key shape-corrections: use `self.agents_root` not `self.agent_root` (F1); thread in `agent.py:delegate()` method not `delegate.py` module (F10); identity-pinning test not isinstance (F3); doctor SKIP enumeration + dispatch (F6 — also surfaced that `check_mandate_backend` is missing from doctor.py as a regression from #124 arc; follow-up issue to be filed inline per scope discipline).

- **`PolicyBackend` Protocol scaffolding + `FilesystemPolicyBackend` reference impl + `policy.md` parser + parametrized conformance suite + spec/32 RFC** ([#89](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/89) — PolicyBackend protocol arc **PR 1 of 4**). Scaffolding-only PR: no call site routes through the new Protocol yet (`AtomicAgent.__init__` is unchanged); PR 2 wires the bootstrap path; PR 3 wires consumption (cost-cap MIN integration + tool/MCP/model log-only mode + `policy_decision` event emission); PR 4 spec lock + flag-flip ungate + status flip to "nine backend protocols shipped". The new module establishes the operator-facing surface for the cross-agent configuration layer that PRs 2-4 build on. New package `atomic_agents/policy/` follows the 8-backend established pattern (Memory / LLM / Judge / Lock / Log / AgentProfile / ToolRegistry / Mandate): `types.py` (canonical frozen dataclasses `CostCaps`, `PolicyCapabilities` with `cache_ttl_s: int | None` per /plan-eng-review D5, `PolicyDecision` log-event dataclass as discriminated union with `decision_kind: Literal["deny", "override"]` discriminator + axis-specific fields per /plan-eng-review D9 fold #4 + plan-subagent F5 — SaaS / Postgres adapters target the schema from day 1, exception hierarchy `PolicyError` / `PolicyInvalid`), `backend.py` (`@runtime_checkable` `PolicyBackend` Protocol with 4 query methods + `capabilities()` + registry primitives `register_policy_backend` / `unregister_policy_backend` / `get_policy_backend` / `list_policy_backends` / `get_default_policy_backend` honoring `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_BACKEND` env var — `unregister_*` matches the standard registry surface from Lock/Log/Profile/LLM/Judge), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemPolicyBackend(project_root)` reference impl with path-traversal refusal at the API boundary per spec/32 MUST #1, per-agent isolation enforced at the storage layer per MUST #2, **construction is side-effect-free** per MUST #4 — no stat, no parse, no env-var validation at `__init__`; first method call lazy-parses, **mtime+size-gated parse cache** keyed on `(st_mtime_ns, st_size)` composite per plan-subagent F8 — catches same-second edits on 1-second-mtime-granularity filesystems (HFS+, ext4 without sub-second timestamps) via size proxy; concurrent parse is idempotent — no lock per plan-subagent F10; capability advertises `cache_ttl_s=0` per plan-subagent F1 reconciliation — "operator-observable upper bound on staleness at the API boundary" semantic; URL factory `make_filesystem_policy_backend_from_url("filesystem:///path")` with credential-redacted `ValueError` per MUST #6). New top-level `atomic_agents/policy_md.py` parser supporting BOTH top-level fleet-default fields (cost_caps, tools, mcp_servers, model) AND a nested `agents: { <agent_name>: { ... } }` section for per-agent overrides per /plan-eng-review D7 — Premise 5 revised; mirrors `mandates_md.py` parser shape but without state machinery (Policy has no revocation, no expiry, no reservations). **Parser composition** per plan-subagent F7 resolution: cost caps MIN per dimension; tool / MCP allowlist field-merge with deny-takes-precedence within layer (effective_allow = fleet.allow ∪ agent.allow; effective_deny = fleet.deny ∪ agent.deny; is_allowed = NOT in effective_deny AND (effective_allow empty OR in effective_allow)); model selection per-agent REPLACES fleet; empty `agents: foo: {}` body means no override per plan-subagent F12 — fleet defaults apply (explicit revocation requires explicit `null` per field). Parametrized conformance suite `tests/test_policy_protocol_conformance.py` (~30 conformance tests across `FilesystemPolicyBackend` + `MockPolicyBackend` registered under `mock:` backend_id via pytest fixture with **register-in-setup + unregister-in-teardown** discipline per plan-subagent F3 — prevents cross-suite registry pollution) — each test pins a spec/32 invariant (construction-side-effect-free, no-policy-md returns no-opinion, `agent_name` path-traversal/control/newline/empty/leading-dot refusal across all 4 query methods, tool/server name validation accepts dots+dashes+colons, fleet-cost-caps apply to unmentioned agent, per-agent cap MIN composes with fleet, fleet tools.allow restricts unknown tools, fleet tools.deny overrides allow, per-agent tool allow merges with fleet, per-agent tool deny unions with fleet, model override replaces fleet, empty `agents:` body means no override). `tests/test_policy_filesystem_backend.py` (~15 filesystem-specific tests covering constructor accepts non-existent path, constructor does not stat filesystem, first method call lazy-parses, mtime-cache hit skips parse, mtime-cache invalidates on mtime change, size-change invalidates even when mtime same — the F8 same-second-edit defense, concurrent parse after mtime bump is idempotent — F10 5-thread regression test, malformed policy.md raises `PolicyInvalid` on first call NOT construction, URL factory accepts non-existent path, URL factory refuses other-scheme URLs with credential redaction — F6 / MUST #6 defense). **New `docs/spec/32-policy-backend.md`** lands as RFC (locks at PR 4 when implementation matches and conformance suite pins the contract). §"Implementer contract for policy backends" enumerates **7 normative MUSTs** derived from policy semantics (Policy has no state, no reservations, no lifecycle events beyond `policy_decision`, so fewer MUSTs than spec/22/24/25/29's 8): (1) `agent_name` validation at API boundary, (2) per-agent isolation, (3) fresh re-read with `cache_ttl_s`-bounded staleness, (4) construction side-effect-free, (5) capability honesty, (6) URL credential redaction, (7) `PolicyDecision` event schema compliance. Count provisional; PR 4 may adjust based on PR 3 adversarial review findings. **Plan-subagent pass before implementation** caught 4 SEVERE + 6 HIGH + 3 MED + 1 LOW design risks with ZERO new decisions surfaced — all 14 fold-and-fix within the 11 locked architectural decisions from /office-hours + /plan-eng-review (2026-05-19): F1 (cache_ttl_s semantics reconciled — filesystem returns 0, mtime+size cache is internal optimization conformant with the API-boundary staleness contract), F2 (registry shape pinned to `(backend_id, cls)` matching 8 priors instead of design's `(scheme, factory)` framing), F3 (`unregister_policy_backend` is standard surface not Policy-novel — Lock/Log/Profile/LLM/Judge already ship it; bootstrap is idempotent with silent-replace on collision matching Lock pattern), F4 (construction-side-effect-free promise pinned as MUST #4 — preserves the 115-byte-identical promise for AtomicAgent construction sites under PR 1's no-consumption scope and PR 2's wired-but-unconsumed scope), F5 (PolicyDecision schema pinned as discriminated union with axis-specific fields — SaaS adapters target the schema from day 1; bending it later breaks them), F6 (spec/23 was deliberately skipped at the time spec/24 was filed — no retained semantics; use spec/32), F7 (parser composition pinned as field-merge with deny-takes-precedence within layer), F8 (cache key = `(mtime_ns, st_size)` composite catches same-second edits via size proxy on legacy filesystems), F9 (`agent_name` validation `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+` runs BEFORE any storage/dict access; tool/server name validation rejects only control chars + newlines allowing `mcp:server:tool.name` convention), F10 (concurrent parse is idempotent — no lock; documented because SaaS adapters with expensive loads SHOULD lock-serialize their own cache loads), F11 (Implementer contract = 7 MUSTs for Policy, not 8 — fewer because no state semantics; count provisional), F12 (empty `agents: foo: {}` body = no override; explicit revocation requires explicit `null` per field), F13 (drop `PolicyStateSchemaUnsupported` — Policy has no state; would be dead exception), F14 (drop `decision_kind: "audit"` — no semantic defined and PR 3's log-only mode is encoded via "action proceeded after event emitted" rather than via a different event kind). **Implementation parallelization:** 4 Sonnet sub-agents on independent files completed in ~5 min wall-clock vs ~2 hr sequential (Lane A: `policy/types.py` + package marker; Lane B: `policy/backend.py` + registry + bootstrap; Lane C: `policy/filesystem.py` + `policy_md.py` parser; Lane D: both test files). Serial integration phase caught 1 real test-data discrepancy (one parametrized cost-cap test reused the same policy.md fixture for two sub-cases that needed different fleet values — fixed by adding a second fixture string). **README refresh folded into PR 1**: status block + Backend Protocol Roadmap table updated from "seven backend protocols shipped / 1953 tests" → "eight / 2201" (Mandate row added) AND further updated to "PolicyBackend in progress (scope-designed 2026-05-19)" + final paragraph updated to "eight of twelve / spec/32 RFC referenced". README diff combines two sessions' changes: the prior session's Mandate-refresh (left uncommitted) AND PR 1's spec/32 addition. **Zero behavior change** end-to-end — `AtomicAgent.__init__` signature unchanged; the new module is registered at framework import via `register_policy_backend("filesystem", FilesystemPolicyBackend)` but not consumed (PR 2 wires through `AtomicAgent.__init__` + per-runner kwargs + `delegate.py` threading per D1 + `doctor.check_policy_backend`; PR 3 wires consumption with `_check_cost_guardrails` MIN integration + MandateCheck steps 7-9 pre-composed cap consumption + tool dispatch site + MCP discovery site + model-selection site + `policy_decision` event emission behind `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP=false` log-only flag; PR 4 flips the flag default + locks spec/32 + flips status to "nine backend protocols shipped"). All 115 `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites in the test suite see byte-identical pre-#89 behavior. **Two-round Opus adversarial review** (CLAUDE.md taste rule 11; Codex skipped per standing project rule). Round 1 caught 2 P0s: (1) circular import — `import atomic_agents.policy_md` cold raised `ImportError: cannot import name 'AgentPolicyOverride'` because the policy_md → policy package init → backend bootstrap → filesystem → policy_md chain hit a partially-initialized module; (2) YAML alias-bomb DoS — `_load_yaml`'s docstring falsely claimed `yaml.safe_load` defends against alias-bomb expansion, but `safe_load` blocks code execution NOT alias expansion; a 7-level billion-laughs-shaped policy.md expanded to 178 MB RSS in <100 ms (same shape as the ToolRegistry spec/25 PR 1 lesson that hit 33 GB RSS pre-fix). Round 1 fixes: deferred-import in `policy/filesystem.py:_get_snapshot` for `parse_policy_md` + `PolicySnapshot` (with TYPE_CHECKING block for annotations); side-effect import `from . import policy as _policy_bootstrap` in `atomic_agents/__init__.py` so the package's load order resolves the cycle (matches the transitive-load pattern that makes Mandate's cold-import work); `MAX_POLICY_MD_BYTES = 256 * 1024` size cap in `parse_policy_md()` enforced via `path.stat()` BEFORE `read_bytes()`; corrected `_load_yaml` docstring. Round 2 caught 1 P2: the size-cap fix landed without a regression test — a future refactor swapping `>` for `>=` or moving the stat would silently regress the very bug Round 1 caught. Round 2 fix: 2 boundary tests added (`test_oversized_policy_md_raises_PolicyInvalid` at cap+1; `test_policy_md_at_exactly_cap_does_not_raise` at exactly cap — pins the `>` strict-inequality semantic). Test count: 2244 → **2309 passing** + 34 skipped (+65 net: ~48 conformance — 30 tests × parametrized backends with mock-specific skips — + ~15 filesystem-specific + 2 round-2 size-cap regression tests). The Protocol seam is in place from PR 1; `FilesystemPolicyBackend` is the only reference impl in v1 (per /office-hours Option 2 — build the seam upfront, defer alternate impls until concrete user demand); future Postgres / SaaS / org-admin-console adapters slot in via `register_policy_backend(...)` without forking core — same agent definitions, same `agent.call()` flow, same audit trail (`policy_decision` event family from day 1), different policy substrate.

- **`atomic-agents bundle` CLI command + spec/26 cascade-bundle pre-render for skill-mode loads** ([#231](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/231)). Filed from Muse Showrunner smoke test 2026-05-17 — end-of-session run took ~7 minutes wall time, dominated by per-file Read tool round-trips during skill-mode cascade load (30-90s before first substantive response on every invocation). The fix: a small framework primitive that pre-renders the cascade into one file the skill loads via a single Read. Wall-time for cascade load drops from 30-90s to 1-3s on a realistic 18-24 file cascade. **New module** `atomic_agents/bundle.py` (~430 LOC) composes the existing `_cascade.py` detect_cascade + resolve_tools_md + load_role_prompt + load_project_layer functions plus filesystem walks for memory INDEX + pinned + recent atomic notes + wiki INDEX + recent journal — exactly the spec/04 steps [1]-[10] (single-agent) or spec/06 three-layer equivalent (cascaded) that `AtomicAgent.load()` would assemble at startup. Section headers mirror spec/04 §"Cache breakpoints" so a future caller can map bundle sections back to Anthropic prompt-cache breakpoints by parsing the headers. Source files stay canonical (rule #1 — vault is source of truth); bundles are derived artifacts cached at `$ATOMIC_AGENTS_CACHE_DIR/<slug>.md` (default `~/.cache/atomic-agents/bundles/`). Atomic writes via `_io.atomic_write` — bundle path is either fully rewritten or unchanged. **New CLI subcommand** `atomic-agents bundle <agent>` in `atomic_agents/cli.py` with `--if-stale` (default skill-mode invocation — regenerates only when any source file's mtime exceeds the bundle's; sub-millisecond check on local SSD), `--refresh` (force regeneration), `--extra-file <path>` repeatable for operator-supplied extras, `--to-stdout` / `--print-path` / `--cache-dir` / `--agents-root`. **Operator extras mechanism** for skill-specific augments (the motivating case: Muse Showrunner reads `~/ObsidianVault/personal/identity/{SOUL,USER,HEARTBEAT}.md` which are NOT part of the framework cascade): declarative `<agent>/bundle.md` listing one path per line (supports `- path` markdown-list shape, `# comment` lines, backtick-quoted paths, globs) OR repeatable `--extra-file` CLI flag. Missing extras raise `FileNotFoundError` — no silent drops; operators learn about misspellings immediately. **No file watcher** — the prior session's draft referenced "existing file-watching infrastructure" which doesn't exist in atomic-agents-stack (verified by searching `watchdog` / `inotify` / `fswatch`); mtime comparison runs inside `--if-stale` and is invisible in the skill's wall-time. **No new backend protocol** — bundles are a *derived rendering* of state behind existing protocols (`MemoryBackend`, `AgentProfileBackend`); per CLAUDE.md rule #2, protocols exist for storage primitives, and pre-rendering is not a storage primitive. Future `PersonaBackend` (#62) composes cleanly: when it ships, the persona section of the bundle routes through it via an implementation-internal edit to `bundle.py`'s render functions; the bundle's external interface (CLI flags, file format, cache layout) stays stable. **New `docs/spec/26-cascade-bundle.md`** (DRAFT, locks when the implementation matches and tests pass) documents the 6 load-bearing decisions, the bundle file format with header + section markers, the contract `render_bundle` honors, the staleness model, the extras mechanism (including the trust model — operators reading their own filesystem; no privilege boundary), the composition path for future backends, and explicit non-goals (no watcher, no on-demand-content bundling, no new backend protocol, no bundle-aware runtime, no multi-agent bundles). **New doctor check** `check_bundle_cache_writable` (host-level, no `--agent` required since cache is per-operator-machine) validates the bundle cache directory exists and is writable with `fix_hint` for the two failure modes (unreadable dir → `chmod`; `ATOMIC_AGENTS_CACHE_DIR` points somewhere unwritable). **New tests** `tests/test_cascade_bundle.py` (35 tests, split across two commits): the initial 18 cover cascaded full layout, flat single-agent layout, tools.override merging, missing-files graceful omission, bundle.md declarative extras (with comments + list shape + globs), --extra-file CLI extras, missing-extras-raises, --if-stale skip when fresh, --if-stale regen on source mutation, atomic write (monkeypatched mid-write failure leaves no partial file), pinned-note detection via `pinned: true` frontmatter, recent-notes selection excluding pinned, recent-journal selection newest-first, `ATOMIC_AGENTS_CACHE_DIR` env var override, slug generation under agents-root, slug fallback when agent path is not under agents-root. A follow-up commit adds 17 more closing the coverage gaps flagged by /ship's subagent coverage audit (raised from 62% to ~90%): CLI subcommand coverage (`--print-path` no-regen, `--to-stdout`, missing-agent → exit 1, missing-extra-file → exit 1, `--if-stale`/`--refresh` argparse mutex), `check_bundle_cache_writable` PASS / mkdir-OSError / probe-write-OSError / env-var-source-string, and bundle.md edge cases (self-mutation triggering staleness, glob-matches-zero raising, relative-path resolution under agent_root, backtick-quoted paths, `pinned: yes` and `pinned: 1` truthy alternatives accepted alongside `pinned: true`). **Skill template doc updated** at `docs/implementation/claude-skill-agent.md` to show the bundle-then-Read pattern as primary with fallback to enumerated parallel reads when `atomic-agents bundle` is unavailable; on-demand recall pattern during conversation (INDEX → 1-3 atomic notes; wiki INDEX → specific pages) explicitly preserved per rule #6 (progressive disclosure). Test count: 2201 → **2244 passing** + 34 skipped (+43 net — 18 initial + 17 coverage-gap follow-up + 6 round-1 adversarial follow-up + 2 round-2 adversarial follow-up).

**Adversarial-review pre-merge fixes** (two Opus subagent rounds caught these — rule #11). Round 1 found 5 issues; round 2 verified the fixes AND caught 4 additional issues the round-1 commit introduced or missed — exactly the pattern rule #11 predicts:

*Round 1 fixes (commit 5 of 6):*

- **bundle.md write-protection threat documented** (#232 filed for hardcoded enforcement). Spec/26 §"Trust model" expanded to call out that `bundle.md` is operator-authored config in the same class as `tools.md` / `judges.md` / `mandates.md` / `persona/*.md` — an agent with broad-enough write access could exfiltrate sensitive paths into its own next-turn context via `bundle.md`. v1 mitigation is documentation + the `_capture.enforce_write_path` convention (operators don't grant `<agent>/` in `write_paths`); hardcoded refusal tracked at #232.
- **Mtime staleness made strict (F-5).** Changed `<=` to `<` so a same-second edit-and-regenerate on 1s-granularity filesystems (ext4, HFS+) doesn't silently keep the old bundle. Also force regeneration when zero sources exist (previously `default=0.0` made empty-cascade always-fresh).
- **Deletion-aware staleness via `_staleness_paths` helper (F-9).** Previously, deleting a memory note / journal entry / policy file left the bundle stale forever (no source mtime was newer than the bundle). Now also tracks parent directory mtimes (memory/, wiki/, journal/, policy/) — POSIX bumps directory mtime on child add/remove, which catches deletions.
- **Encoding-tolerant rendering (F-8).** A new `_safe_read_text` helper falls back to `errors="replace"` with a per-file warning header so one non-UTF-8 byte in a single source file doesn't crash the whole bundle. Wraps the cascade resolver calls (`load_role_prompt`, `resolve_tools_md`, `load_project_layer`) so canon.md / style_guide.md / policy/* / PROMPT.md / tools.md / model.md degrade gracefully alongside persona files. `_iter_pinned` also catches `UnicodeDecodeError` per-file.
- **Pinned-frontmatter comment stripping (F-4).** `_iter_pinned`'s lexical scan now strips inline `# comment` suffixes from the `pinned:` value before the truthy-set membership check, so `pinned: true  # set by /pin-this` correctly pins (previously parsed as `value = "true # set by /pin-this"` → not in truthy set → false negative).

*Round 2 fixes (commit 6 of 6) — caught what round 1 introduced or missed:*

- **`_safe_read_text` widened to catch OSError (R2-F4).** Round 1's commit message claimed "degrade gracefully" but the helper only caught `UnicodeDecodeError` — a chmod-000 source file (PermissionError) or IsADirectoryError still crashed the bundle. Now catches both, returns just a warning comment when the file is unreadable. The `_render_cascaded` cascade-resolver wrappers also widened from `except UnicodeDecodeError` to `except (UnicodeDecodeError, OSError)` to match.
- **Cache file mode tightened to 0600 (R2-F9).** `_io.atomic_write` writes with the inheriting umask, which is 0644 on many shared hosts. After Round 1's F-1 spec update explicitly warned about secret-adjacent content in the bundle (operator extras can pull in identity-adjacent files), leaving the cache world-readable would have been the "docs say one thing, code does another" pattern rule #13 forbids. Now `os.chmod(bundle_path, 0o600)` runs after `atomic_write`; falls through silently on filesystems that don't support chmod (FAT32, some network mounts).

Zero behavior change for the runtime — none of these touch `AtomicAgent.load()` or `assemble_system_prompt()`. The bundle module is purely additive — removing the bundle CLI leaves the runtime unchanged. Filesystem-default deployments with no skill mode see zero new behavior. Zero behavior change for the runtime — `AtomicAgent.load()` does NOT consult the bundle; bundles are a skill-template optimization, not a runtime cache. Bundle module is purely additive — removing the bundle CLI leaves the runtime unchanged. Filesystem-default deployments with no skill mode see zero new behavior. - **Mandate validation steps 7-9 ungated + reservation pattern + crash recovery + post-action verification events** ([#124](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/124) — mandate primitive arc **PR 3b of 6**). Closes the load-bearing security property: cumulative token + external budget caps are now actually enforced (the PR 3a stub returned `mandate_budget_check_unavailable` BLOCK; this PR makes the checks real). Operators authoring `cumulative_external_usd: 6000` on a mandate now have that cap meaningfully defended against concurrent action races via the reservation pattern; crash-restart no longer silently loses budget tracking on real money; post-hoc divergence audits surface when an action's executed target differed from the authorization at proposal time. **Plan-subagent pre-implementation review** caught 3 amendments worth landing first (spec/29 commit `15089f2`): Risk 1 (cap_kind priority rule — adopted `monthly_external > daily_external > cumulative_external > monthly_token > daily_token > cumulative_token > per_action_max`; real-money first, the operator's most-authored window first); Risk 4 (`mandate_used` collapsed into the cost event as a derived view — `LogBackend.append(record)` is the framework's atomic primitive and adding a batch operation would amend a locked spec); Risk 7 (step 9 ESCALATE preempts step 8 BLOCK when both fire — operator-decision-required is the more nuanced surface than hard BLOCK). **Implementation:** 4 parallel Sonnet sub-agents on independent files completed in ~7 min wall-clock (vs ~3 hr sequential), one sub-agent wrote the 72-test suite; serial integration phase caught 3 real impl bugs (uncomputed projections under escalation-only mandates, wrong LogQuery filter in `_scan_orphan_reservations`, ActionClass.IRREVERSIBLE typo). **Two-round Opus adversarial review** (CLAUDE.md taste rule 11) caught additional findings: Round 1 found 2 P0s — agent_call cost event missing mandate_id (`_sum_prior_token_cost` would never match, cumulative_token_usd caps unenforceable) + reservations carrying `projected_usd=0.0` (compute_outstanding's stale-budget race defense vacuous); Round 2 found 3 P1/P2s — projection cache leak on BLOCK paths, replacement test tautological (re-implemented under-test logic inline), `mandate_cites_in_call` field impl-only without spec coverage. All fixed in commits `aec5404` (Round 1) and `2bc76a7` (Round 2). (1) **NEW** `atomic_agents/judge/mandate_reservations.py` (~720 LOC): `ReservationRecord` frozen dataclass; `MandateReservationManager` with create/commit/rollback/_expire/shutdown lifecycle, threading.Lock-serialized in-process state, threading.Timer-driven TTL watchers (in-process only per spec/29 Risk H — cross-process orphans route through `recover_orphan_reservations`); module-level `compute_outstanding(log_backend, scope, mandate_id)` implementing the 4-clause definition (no _committed/_rolled_back/_expired/_committed_on_recovery AND **no cost event with matching proposal_id** AND age < ttl_s) — clause 3 is the load-bearing addition that closes the cost-event-landed-without-_committed window; module-level `build_mandate_log_record_extras(successful_cites)` pure helper for agent_call mandate tagging (R2-2 extraction). (2) **`atomic_agents/judge/mandate_check.py`** steps 7-9 ungated (~676 LOC added): `_step7_token_budget` projects via preceding-iteration `LogQuery` with Risk 2 defensive fallback (if most-recent matching event's `ts` is before current iteration's start, fall back to `expected_cost_per_call_usd` so stale-baseline drift doesn't compound across multi-iteration runs); apportionment is simple `turn_cost / N` (v1 simplification per spec/29 line 376; argument-token-weighted form is follow-up); `_step8_external_budget` uses `CostEstimatorRegistry.estimate` with try/except fail-closed to spec-stable `mandate_external_cost_unprojectable` BLOCK reason when (a) no estimator OR static field set, (b) estimator raises, (c) estimator returns inf or non-numeric; `_step9_escalation_thresholds` ESCALATE-preempts step 8 BLOCK per Risk 7; `_build_cap_exceeded_judgment` applies the spec/29 line 629 priority rule + emits `mandate_cap_exceeded_block` with `additional_caps_exceeded` tuple + per-action-max-aware detail string (R1 F11); `record_projection`/`pop_projection` API caches per-proposal projections so agent loop creates reservations with non-zero headroom (R1 F6); recorded ONLY on ALLOW path (R2 F1 leak fix). (3) **`atomic_agents/mandate/types.py` + `atomic_agents/mandate/filesystem.py`** (+443 LOC): `MandateBackend.recover_orphan_reservations(log_backend, scope, *, lock_backend=None, lock_ttl_s=30)` Protocol method + capability flag `supports_crash_recovery`; FilesystemMandateBackend implementation with scan-inside-lock discipline per spec/29 Risk 3 (LockBackend lease serializes both the orphan scan AND the recovery emission so concurrent recoverers can't double-emit); token orphans → `mandate_reservation_committed_on_recovery` at projected_usd (pessimistic over-report — operator over-billed once, recoverable via reconcile CLI vs silent under-billing); external orphans → BOTH `_committed_on_recovery` AND `mandate_reservation_external_unverified` (operator-facing doctor surface for "did the external action actually run? verify in Stripe/vendor"). (4) **`atomic_agents/agent.py`** (+406 LOC): `AtomicAgent.__init__` invokes agent-scope recovery via `_run_mandate_recovery_for_all_scopes` (BEFORE `_load_config` so orphans reconcile before any check fires this run); `_init_mandate_reservation_managers` runs project-scope recovery (after cascade detection) + instantiates per-scope MandateReservationManager instances; cost events for mandate-citing actions get `mandate_id` + `proposal_id` on RunRecord (the LogQuery shape `_sum_prior_token_cost` relies on for cumulative-spend defense — single-mandate-per-call sets exactly; multi-mandate-per-call attributes to most-recent cite + emits `mandate_cites_in_call: list[str]` audit hint); `_verify_post_action` emits the 3 new lifecycle events for `external_side_effect` / `high_risk` action classes AFTER cost commit (Risk 6 ordering — operator REVISE-driven divergence is correctly surfaced as `_diverged` per Risk 10). (5) **spec/29** second-pass amendments (commits `15089f2` + `2bc76a7`): cap_kind priority rule, `mandate_used` derived-view collapse, step 9 ESCALATE precedence, `mandate_cites_in_call` field documentation. (6) **`tests/test_mandate_reservations.py`** NEW (510 LOC, 22 tests) + extensions to `test_mandate_check.py` (+1355 LOC), `test_mandate_filesystem_backend.py` (+349 LOC), `test_mandate_integration.py` (+428 LOC). - **Pre-#124-PR-3b scaffolding — `CostEstimatorRegistry` per-agent named registry + `ToolDefinition.cost_estimator_id` / `expected_external_cost_usd` fields + fail-fast validation at `tool_registry.register()` time + `PRIMITIVE_MANDATE_RESERVATION` log primitive + spec/29 amendments for 2 SEVERE + 4 HIGH plan-subagent risks** (#124 PR 3b prep — no separate issue; lands ahead of the budget-projection + reservation-pattern body PR). Plan-subagent pass before PR 3b identified 2 SEVERE risks (cost projection's `Callable` field on `ToolDefinition` can't satisfy spec/25 MUST #4 Tier B round-trip — same shape as the target_extractor Callable risk that re-architecture-cycled PR 3a prep #218; outstanding-reservation definition gap that lets sequential proposals double-count) + 4 HIGH risks (multi-process crash-recovery double-counting without LockBackend serialization; token-cost projection baseline drift; cap-overshoot precision bound undefined; cap-priority resolution ambiguity when multiple caps fire simultaneously) + 1 missing post-action verification event family. This prep PR resolves them all in advance so PR 3b's body is purely budget-projection + reservation-pattern logic, not scaffolding + re-architecture. (1) **`atomic_agents/judge/cost_estimator_registry.py`** (~200 LOC) — new `CostEstimatorRegistry` mirroring `TargetExtractorRegistry` shape exactly: per-agent in-memory `dict[str, Callable[[dict], float]]`; `register(name, callable)` rejects collisions + non-alphanumeric/underscore name characters; `replace()` for explicit overwrite path; `has(name)` membership check; `estimate(tool_name, tool_arguments, estimator_id=None)` returns `float('inf')` when (estimator missing, estimator raises any exception, estimator returns non-numeric) — fail-closed per spec/29 line 380 so caller (`MandateCheck` step 8) emits `mandate_external_cost_unprojectable` BLOCK; named estimator missing on `tool_registry.register()` raises `UnknownCostEstimator` (loud failure at register time per spec/29 §"Registration order discipline" so operator misconfiguration surfaces BEFORE first mandate-citing dispatch — same shape + same discipline as `UnknownTargetExtractor` from PR 3a). **No built-in estimators ship by default** — unlike target extractors (where heuristic field-name fallback is sensible for the common case), external-cost projection is so tool-specific that "guess from arg shape" is the wrong default; tools with dynamic pricing register an explicit estimator at agent setup, tools with static pricing use the new `expected_external_cost_usd` field, mandates with `*_external_usd` caps over tools with neither registered fail-closed at evaluation time. (2) **`atomic_agents/tools.py`** — `ToolDefinition` gets two new optional fields: `cost_estimator_id: str | None = None` (names the registered estimator) and `expected_external_cost_usd: float | None = None` (static-pricing alternative for tools with deterministic per-call cost). `ToolRegistry.register()` signature extends with `cost_estimator_registry: CostEstimatorRegistry | None = None` kwarg; when both the kwarg is passed AND the tool's `cost_estimator_id` is set, validates the ID is registered or raises `UnknownCostEstimator` immediately (mirrors the existing `target_extractor_id` validation block byte-for-byte in shape). (3) **`atomic_agents/agent.py`** wiring — `self._cost_estimators = CostEstimatorRegistry()` initialized BEFORE tool_registry loading; new `register_cost_estimator(name, callable)` public method (per-agent scoping — coordinator estimators do NOT flow to delegates per spec/15 delegate isolation + spec/25 Decision 9); both `self.tool_registry.register(...)` call sites in the descriptor-loading loop now pass `cost_estimator_registry=self._cost_estimators` alongside the existing `target_extractor_registry=self._target_extractors` kwarg so the fail-fast validation actually fires during agent construction. (4) **`atomic_agents/logs/types.py`** — new `PRIMITIVE_MANDATE_RESERVATION = "mandate_reservation"` log primitive constant for the reservation-event family PR 3b introduces (`mandate_reservation_created` / `mandate_reservation_committed` / `mandate_reservation_rolled_back` / `mandate_reservation_expired`); landed in prep so PR 3b's body doesn't ship a new constant alongside the reservation logic. (5) **spec/29 amendments** resolving the 6 plan-subagent risks + 1 event-family gap: Risk A (outstanding-reservation definition — explicit "outstanding = created AND NOT (committed OR rolled_back OR expired)" with `proposal_id` pairing rule so two sequential MandateCheck calls on the same in-flight proposal don't double-reserve); Risk E (atomic emission discipline — `mandate_used` event MUST emit atomically with the cost path's reservation commit, not on a separate code path that can crash between); Risk B (crash recovery serialization — multi-process recovery MUST acquire a `LockBackend.acquire(scope='mandate-recovery:<scope>')` lease before reconciling orphan reservations; without it, two processes recovering the same orphan reservation under pessimistic "over-report > under-report" semantics double-charge); Risk H (TTL expiry clarification — reservation TTL expiry is IN-PROCESS-ONLY; cross-process crash recovery routes through LockBackend reconciliation, not TTL — closes the cross-process double-expire window); Risk F (token-cost projection baseline pinned to "immediately preceding iteration" + cap-overshoot bound — projection MAY overshoot the cap by `≤ 1 × (max_observed_iteration_cost_usd × 1.5)` before falling back to fail-closed BLOCK); Risk G (cap-priority resolution — `mandate_cap_exceeded_block` event extended with `additional_caps_exceeded: list[str]` field listing all caps simultaneously exceeded + explicit cap_kind priority rule when multiple fire: `monthly_external_usd > daily_external_usd > monthly_token_usd > daily_token_usd > per_action_max_usd` so operators see the primary trigger first); Risk I + event-family gap (3 new lifecycle events for post-action verification: `mandate_action_verified` when the post-action outcome matches the cited mandate's permitted scope, `mandate_action_diverged` when the action's actual effect exceeded what was authorized — operator-facing audit signal, NOT a refund mechanism in this version, and `mandate_action_verification_unavailable` when the action class doesn't support post-hoc verification — emitted exactly once per `external_side_effect` or `irreversible` action so dashboards can count verifications without inflation). Spec/29 §"Validation steps" step 8 prose rewritten around the named-registry shape (was: `cost_estimator: Callable`); spec/29 §"Cost reservation pattern" and §"Crash recovery for reservations" now reflect the LockBackend serialization + TTL clarification + outstanding-reservation pairing rule. (6) **`tests/test_mandate_check.py`** — new `TestCostEstimatorRegistry` class (10 tests) mirroring `TestTargetExtractorRegistry`: empty-at-construction (no built-ins), collision-refusal, invalid-name-character rejection, replace-overwrites, estimate-with-args, unknown-id-raises, none-id-returns-inf, exception-returns-inf, non-numeric-return-returns-inf, int-coerced-to-float; new `TestToolRegistryCostEstimatorValidation` class (4 tests) pinning the fail-fast register-time validation: unknown id raises `UnknownCostEstimator`, registered id succeeds, no-estimator-no-registry succeeds (back-compat for tools that don't declare a cost_estimator_id), `expected_external_cost_usd` field round-trips through `ToolRegistry.get`; new `test_atomic_agent_register_cost_estimator_public_api` (1 test) pinning the `agent.register_cost_estimator()` public surface mirrors `agent.register_target_extractor`. Filesystem-default deployments with no `mandates.md` files AND no `cost_estimator_id` declared on any `ToolDefinition` see ZERO behavior change end-to-end — the registry is constructed empty, the new fields default to None, the validation block is a no-op. Test count: 2110 → **2125 passing** + 34 skipped (+15 net). Zero behavior change for all 115 existing `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites in the test suite. PR 3b body (budget projection + reservation pattern + crash recovery + post-action verification events) lands next, consuming this scaffolding.

- **`MandateCheck` judge specialist + validation steps 1-6 + lifecycle dedup + target_extractor registry + judges.md `## Mandates` parser** ([#124](https://github.com/dep0we/agentic-stack/issues/124) — mandate primitive arc **PR 3a of 6**). The highest-risk PR in the arc per /plan-eng-review. Plan-subagent pass before implementation caught 3 SEVERE + 5 HIGH risks (resolved upfront in prep PR #218 — spec/29 amendments, target_canonical field, MandateSettings dataclass, test helpers); this PR ships the load-bearing impl without the re-architecture risk. Four Sonnet sub-agents implemented the independent pieces in parallel (~6 min wall-clock vs ~3 hr sequential), one Sonnet sub-agent wrote the 51-test conformance/integration suite (caught 2 real impl bugs fixed in this commit). **`atomic_agents/judge/mandate_check.py`** (~730 LOC): `MandateCheck` class implementing validation steps 1-6 (existence, source-hash stub for PR 4 binding wire, state, tool allowlist, target allowlist via per-agent named extractor registry, time window); steps 7-9 stubbed fail-closed with reason `mandate_budget_check_unavailable` (forever-stable per spec/29; PR 3b ungates); suspicious-rebind throttle check via persistent state (closes the prompt-injection attack window per spec/29 §"Suspicious-rebind throttle"); pass-through for non-mandate-citing proposals (zero overhead). **`atomic_agents/judge/mandate_state.py`** (~475 LOC): `MandateStateManager` handling lifecycle event dedup (granted/revoked/expired transitions) + throttle persistence on-disk in `MandateBackend.read_state` shape under `"throttles"` key (in-memory-only forbidden per plan-subagent Risk C — crash-restart bypass defeats the prompt-injection defense); per-scope `threading.Lock` serializes within single agent process (eventual-consistency at cross-process layer documented as known limitation per spec/29 §"Concurrent state writes"). **`atomic_agents/judge/target_extractor_registry.py`** (~263 LOC): `TargetExtractorRegistry` per-agent in-memory dict + 7 built-in heuristic extractors (recipient_to / recipient_field / target_field / url_field / repository_field / customer_id_field / channel_id_field) pre-registered at construction; MCP tools prefix extracted target with `mcp:<server>:` per spec/29 line 466; `UnknownTargetExtractor` raised at `tool_registry.register()` time (loud early failure per plan-subagent Risk A) when a tool's `target_extractor_id` references an unregistered name. **`atomic_agents/agent.py`** wiring: `self._target_extractors = TargetExtractorRegistry()` initialized BEFORE tool_registry loading; new `register_target_extractor(name, callable)` public method; `_ensure_mandate_check()` lazy constructor cached per agent; MandateCheck **conditionally** inserted into judge ensemble `[PolicyJudge, (MandateCheck if mandate-cite), LLMCatchAll]` per spec/29 line 392 — **zero behavior change** for non-mandate-citing actions (matches PR 2's zero-behavior-change discipline). **`atomic_agents/judges_md.py`** parser extended (+318 LOC): `_parse_mandates_section` populates `JudgesConfig.mandate_settings` from the `## Mandates` section; cascade-merge logic for project-floor pattern (floor-wins where stricter for safety: longer throttle, "block" beats "escalate", etc); backward-compat for files without the section (default-fills from spec/29). **`atomic_agents/tools.py`** modification: `target_extractor_id: str | None = None` field on `ToolDefinition`; validation at `tool_registry.register()` time when the agent's `target_extractor_registry` is passed via kwarg. New conformance + integration test file `tests/test_mandate_check.py` (~1300 LOC, 51 tests): per-step validation (existence, source-hash PR-3a-stub, state, tool allowlist, target allowlist with all 8 sub-cases, time window contiguous + midnight-wrap, budget-cap fail-closed); MandateStateManager (granted/revoked/expired transitions, dedup, throttle persistence including crash-restart-bypass-defense regression test, schema_version=999 raises); TargetExtractorRegistry (built-ins pre-registered, collision-refusal, MCP prefix); AtomicAgent end-to-end (mandate-citing proposals invoke MandateCheck; non-mandate-citing don't see it; unknown extractor_id fails at tool register). Bug-catches in implementation: (1) `MandateCheck` passed `proposal.mcp_server` as positional arg landing as `extractor_id` parameter — fixed to keyword `mcp_server=` so MCP tools' target prefixing works; (2) test helper `make_mandate_md_content` was missing the required `scope:` field (free-text prose authority description) per the parser's `_REQUIRED_FIELDS` — added so PR 3b/4 tests don't trip the same way. Filesystem-default deployments with no `mandates.md` files see zero behavior change end-to-end — the new judge specialist + state manager + registry are constructed but not consumed. Test count: 2059 → **2110 passing** + 34 skipped (+51 net). **Post-action verification event** for external_side_effect + irreversible action classes deferred to a follow-up PR — keeping PR 3a's adversarial-review surface tight (same scope-discipline lesson that split PR 3 into 3a/3b).

Plan-subagent pass before PR 3a identified 3 SEVERE risks + 5 HIGH risks (same magnitude as #63 PR 3 and #64 PR 3 plan-subagent catches that re-architecture-cycled both arcs); this prep PR resolves them all in advance so PR 3a's body is purely `MandateCheck` logic, not scaffolding + re-architecture. (1) **`ActionProposal.target_canonical: str | None = None`** added to `atomic_agents/judge/types.py` (additive; backward-compatible; not in `ProposalAmendment` since the field is framework-owned end-to-end). (2) **`MandateSettings` sub-dataclass** added to `atomic_agents/judges_md.py` — frozen dataclass on `JudgesConfig.mandate_settings` carrying `suspicious_rebind_throttle_s` (default 60), `unextractable_target_action` (default "block"), `reservation_ttl_s` (default 60; PR 3b consumes), `cap_breach_action_class_default` (PR 3b), `high_risk_lock_timeout_s` (PR 4 consumes), `no_expiry_warning` (PR 4 doctor consumes). (3) **`tests/_mandate_test_helpers.py`** — `make_mandate_in_backend(backend, scope_root, scope, mandate_id, **fields)` writes a section to the appropriate `mandates.md`; `make_mandate_md_content(...)` builds the YAML section body; `make_project_root_meta(...)` writes the `_meta` section; `register_extractor(agent, name, callable)` injects a target extractor (forward-compatible with PR 3a's `AtomicAgent.register_target_extractor` public API); `make_proposal_citing(mandate_id, **overrides)` builds a fully-bound `ActionProposal` with `Authorization.granted_by="mandate:<id>"`. Same shape as `make_tool_in_backend` from `tests/test_tool_registry_protocol_conformance.py` — landed before PR 3a per the plan-subagent Risk G "conformance helper trap" finding (same trap that re-architecture-cycled #63 PR 3 when `make_agent_dir` couldn't serve SQLite-backed conformance tests). (4) **spec/29 amendments** resolving the 3 SEVERE risks + 2 HIGH risks: Risk A (target_extractor registry "owned by AgentProfile" wording clarified — it means *scoped per agent instance*, NOT *stored on the AgentProfile snapshot* which is JSON-serializable per spec/24 and cannot store Callable; registry lives on `AtomicAgent._target_extractors` in-memory; built-in heuristics MUST register BEFORE tool_registry loading; tools whose `target_extractor_id` references an unregistered name fail loud at `tool_registry.register()` time, not at MandateCheck evaluation time); Risk B (added the `ActionProposal.target_canonical` field referenced above; spec/29 already documented it as an RFC extension, the implementation now matches); Risk C (suspicious-rebind throttle persistence — explicitly on-disk in `MandateBackend.read_state` shape under `"throttles"` key; in-memory-only forbidden because crash-restart loop defeats the prompt-injection defense the throttle was designed to close); Risk H (source-hash-vs-derived-EXPIRED interaction — explicit walkthrough: hash covers file content only NOT derived EXPIRED state; clock-driven expiry surfaces via step 3 independently; PR 3a tests will pin the timeline); Risk D (concurrent-state-write race — documented as eventual-consistency at the cross-process layer; PR 3a uses `threading.Lock` per-scope within a single agent process; cross-process operators needing strict atomicity wait for SQL-backed MandateBackend post-#124). - **`MandateBackend` wired through `AtomicAgent.__init__` + per-runner kwargs on OutcomeRunner / EvalRunner / DreamRunner** ([#124](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/124) — mandate primitive arc **PR 2 of 6**). Public `self.mandate_backend` attribute mirrors `self.log_backend` / `self.profile_backend` / `self.tool_registry_backend`. Per-runner kwargs added to `OutcomeRunner` + `EvalRunner` + `DreamRunner` (DreamRunner stores for API parity but doesn't construct internal AtomicAgents — same shape as the other agent-internal backends). `delegate.py`-driven recursive AtomicAgent construction deliberately does NOT thread the backend (per-agent scoping — spec/29 + spec/25 Decision 9; target builds its own backend over its own scope, distinct from fleet-scoped backends). New `tests/test_mandate_integration.py` (~11 integration tests) covers: default-resolution, explicit-kwarg, env-var dispatch, runner threading via the established monkeypatch-AtomicAgent-class pattern from #61/#63/#64 (captures kwargs at the boundary — the canonical defense against the kwarg-drop trap), delegate non-threading, per-agent isolation, no regression on the standard agent fixture. **Zero behavior change** for existing operator deployments — empty `<scope>/mandates.md` yields zero MandateBackend consumption; the new attribute exists but no call site loads or evaluates mandates yet (PR 3a wires `MandateCheck` into the judge dispatch). All 115 `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites in the test suite see byte-identical pre-#124 behavior. Test count: 2032 → **2043 passing** + 34 skipped (+11 net). Parallelization: one Sonnet sub-agent mirrored the established #61 PR 2 / #63 PR 2 / #64 PR 2 wiring pattern in ~6 min wall-clock vs ~45 min sequential; integration zero-touch (no fixups needed). Post-action verification event for `external_side_effect` + `irreversible` action classes deferred to PR 3a where it composes naturally with the mandate lifecycle events (`mandate_granted` / `mandate_revoked` / `mandate_expired` / `mandate_used`) rather than living in isolation in PR 2 — same scope-discipline lesson that split PR 3 into 3a/3b at /plan-eng-review.

- **PersonaBackend Protocol wiring** ([#62](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/62) — PersonaBackend protocol arc **PR 2 of 4**). Wires shared-persona composition into the agent bootstrap path. `AtomicAgent(..., persona_backend=...)` constructor kwarg + per-runner kwargs on `OutcomeRunner` / `EvalRunner` / `DreamRunner` + `delegate.py` explicit-only threading (D-ER-2): delegates inherit the coordinator's `PersonaBackend` ONLY when the operator supplied it explicitly via kwarg; default-resolved backends do not leak the coordinator's `<agents_root>/.personas/` directory to cross-vault delegates (mirrors `PolicyBackend`'s `_policy_backend_was_explicit` precedent at `agent.py:401`). `AgentProfileBackend` Protocol gains `external_persona_ref(agent_id) -> str | None` (D-PP-3, supersedes D-ER-1's boolean) and `set_persona_ownership(agent_id, persona_id)` (D-PP-7) — both implemented on `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend` and `SQLiteAgentProfileBackend`. `persona_link_md.py` parser (D-ER-3) at top level (D-PP-5): YAML in a code block with `kind: shared` + `persona_id:` fields; raises `PersonaLinkInvalid` on malformed YAML, missing `kind:`, unsupported kind, missing `persona_id:`, or invalid `persona_id:` charset. Filesystem agent sentinel admits either `persona/IDENTITY.md` or `persona.link.md` (D-PP-1). SQLite v1→v2 schema migration adds `agents.persona_id` column (D-PP-2) with correct race-loser semantics (`catch sqlite3.OperationalError "duplicate column name"` + re-read `schema_version` — Python's `sqlite3` module issues implicit commits before DDL, so ALTER survives a transaction rollback by the race loser). `doctor.check_persona_backend` coherence check: PASS / WARN / FAIL ladder with capability snapshot (`supports_save`, `supports_clone`, `supports_snapshot`, `supports_subscribe`, `supports_templates`, `durable`) + `list_personas()` persona-count probe + URL credential redaction across all error paths. spec/33 §"`delegate.py` threading (D-ER-2)" wording updated (D-PP-9): the stale "delegates do NOT inherit" sentence replaced with the correct explicit-kwarg-only threading rule. PR 2 prep caught 4 SEVERE + 5 HIGH risks pre-implementation (D-PP-1 through D-PP-9); track record extended to 17 SEVERE + 16 HIGH across 7 prep passes.

- **`MandateBackend` Protocol scaffolding + `FilesystemMandateBackend` reference impl + `mandates.md` parser + parametrized conformance suite** ([#124](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/124) — mandate primitive arc **PR 1 of 6**). Scaffolding-only PR: no call site routes through the new Protocol yet (`AtomicAgent.__init__` is unchanged); PR 2 wires the bootstrap path. The new module establishes the operator-facing surface that PRs 2-5 build on. New package `atomic_agents/mandate/` follows the 7-backend established pattern (Memory / LLM / Judge / Lock / Log / AgentProfile / ToolRegistry): `types.py` (canonical dataclasses `Mandate`, `MandateConstraints`, `TargetPattern`, `TimeWindow`, `ProjectMandateMeta`, `MandateCapabilities`, `RevocationState`/`ActionClass` enums, exception hierarchy `MandateError` / `MandateInvalid` / `MandateNotFound` / `MandateStateSchemaUnsupported`), `backend.py` (`@runtime_checkable` `MandateBackend` Protocol + `register_mandate_backend` / `get_mandate_backend` / `list_mandate_backends` / `get_default_mandate_backend` honoring `ATOMIC_AGENTS_MANDATE_BACKEND` env var), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemMandateBackend(scope_root)` reference impl with path-traversal refusal at the API boundary per spec/29 MUST #1, per-scope isolation enforced at the storage layer per MUST #2, atomic state writes via `_io.atomic_write` per MUST #3, source-hash recomputation on every `load_mandate` per MUST #4, derived-EXPIRED state computed at load time per spec/29 line 548, lexicographic ordering of `list_mandates` mirroring tool registry convention; URL factory `make_filesystem_mandate_backend_from_url("filesystem:///path")`). New top-level `atomic_agents/mandates_md.py` parser: parses per-section markdown with embedded YAML matching the `judges_md.py` convention; enforces mandate ID regex `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*` (≤64 chars) per spec/29 line 298; refuses duplicate IDs within a file; enforces constraint enforceability discipline (mandates without enforceable constraints AND without `unconstrained: true` + non-empty justification are rejected per spec/29 §"Constraint enforceability"); parses `_meta` section discipline (project-root files honor `per_agent_mandate_policy: open|listed|forbidden`; per-agent files with `_meta` log a warning + skip — `check_mandate_meta_misplaced` will surface in PR 4); computes `sha256:` algorithm-prefixed source hash on the canonical section bytes. Parametrized conformance suite (`tests/test_mandate_protocol_conformance.py`): 42 conformance test functions + 10 parametrized bad-id cases run across registered backends — each test pins a spec/29 MUST (path-traversal refusal, scope isolation, state-via-Protocol-methods, source-hash recomputation, list includes revoked/expired, MandateNotFound vs MandateInvalid, schema_version raises, capability honesty). `tests/test_mandate_filesystem_backend.py`: 20 filesystem-specific tests covering constructor refusals, disk layout discipline (`<scope_root>/<name>/mandates.md` for agent scope vs `<scope_root>/mandates.md` for project scope), `.judge-state/mandates.json` placement, URL factory parsing. **Zero behavior change** end-to-end — `AtomicAgent.__init__` signature unchanged; the new module is registered at framework import via `register_mandate_backend("filesystem", FilesystemMandateBackend)` but not consumed (PR 2 wires through `AtomicAgent.__init__` + all 4 runners + `doctor.check_mandate_backend`). All existing 1961 tests unaffected. Test count: 1961 → **2032 passing** + 34 skipped (+71 net: 42 conformance + 20 filesystem-specific + 9 helper / registry-resolution tests). Depends on spec/29 amendments shipped in #213 (Implementer contract section + named target_extractor registry + state-via-Protocol-methods + schema_version discipline + suspicious-rebind throttle + forever-stable BLOCK reasons + reservation IDs in cap-exceeded events + step-9 split-line); rebase landed against post-#213 main.

### Fixed

- **Per-call dedup for tool-allowlist denial emissions** ([#273](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/273) — PolicyBackend arc PR 4 follow-up). In log-only mode, the LLM doesn't see refusals and may re-attempt a denied tool every iteration of the multi-turn loop; without dedup the audit log records N events per denied tool per call. PR 4 tracks emitted denials in a per-call `set[str]` reset at `agent.call()` entry; emission skips when the tool name is already in the set. The audit shape stays uniform across log-only and enforce modes; in enforce mode the synthesized `policy_blocked` `ToolCallResult` naturally bounds re-attempts via LLM feedback, but the dedup ensures the same shape if log-only is re-enabled. MCP discovery already emits at most once per server per call by construction (single discovery loop), so no equivalent set is needed there. New `tests/test_policy_noncap_integration.py::test_repeated_denied_tool_emits_once_per_call` + `::test_different_denied_tools_each_emit_once` regression pin.

- **Caldwell sample JSONL now matches the shipped audit-trail shape** ([#137](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/137)). The sample log records in `docs/samples/caldwell/log/2026-05/*.jsonl` now include `run_id`, helper child rows resolve `parent_run_id` back to an in-sample run, and `docs/spec/09-cost-observability.md` names `run_id` as a minimum log field. The sibling JSONL example in `docs/spec/01-anatomy.md` was aligned in the same pass so the two anatomy/observability spec docs no longer contradict each other on minimum log fields. A docs-consistency regression test pins the sample shape so future curated samples do not drift from `AtomicAgent._log()`.

- **`SQLiteToolRegistryBackend` cold-start WAL race — same shape as #208, now closed for the tool registry too** ([#215](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/215)). The #208 fix landed for `atomic_agents/logs/sqlite.py` last cycle (busy_timeout PRAGMA + 7-attempt retry on `SQLITE_BUSY`/`SQLITE_LOCKED`). `atomic_agents/registry/sqlite.py` had the busy_timeout PRAGMA from #64 PR 3 but was missing the retry-on-locked loop — the same defense gap. Surfaced as a CI Python 3.11 flake on PR #214: `tests/test_tool_registry_sqlite_backend.py::test_concurrent_schema_init_no_race` failed with `database is locked` on the WAL transition (PR #214 doesn't touch registry/ — pre-existing flake exposed). `registry/sqlite.py:_get_conn` now wraps the WAL pragma in the same 7-attempt retry loop matching `exc.sqlite_errorcode in (SQLITE_BUSY, SQLITE_LOCKED)` (Python 3.11+) with exponential backoff (~3.15s cumulative sleep, plus up to 5s busy_timeout per attempt). Pre-fix: existing concurrent-schema-init test passed locally on macOS but CI 3.11 flaked. Post-fix: same test passes 10/10 consecutive runs locally; new regression tests pin both the primitive (`test_busy_timeout_set_before_wal_pragma` — `PRAGMA busy_timeout` returns 5000ms on a fresh connection) and the end-to-end behavior (`test_concurrent_schema_init_no_race_under_repeated_runs` — 10 fresh dbs × 16 concurrent threads). Spec/25 §"Implementer contract for registry-backed tool backends" MUST #5 extended with the third lesson (mirrors spec/22 MUST #4 from #208): SQLite-shape backends MUST set busy_timeout before WAL AND retry the WAL transition on SQLITE_BUSY/SQLITE_LOCKED. Implementation now meets its own spec. Filesystem-default deployments unaffected — only operators pinning `ATOMIC_AGENTS_TOOL_REGISTRY_BACKEND=sqlite` saw the flake (and only at high concurrency). No operator-visible behavior change beyond reliability.

### Documentation

- **README spec-doc count drift removed in both occurrences** ([#136](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/136)). Two README lines hardcoded the spec-doc count — the "Spec is the product" section opener at line 154 ("28 locked spec docs (plus 3 RFCs)" — drifted from the "21 + 1 RFC" claim originally filed in #136, which itself drifted again to "28 + 3" while the issue sat queued) and the "Why this exists" tagline at line 31 ("28 locked spec docs + 3 RFCs ... 2381 tests"). Both replaced with "locked spec docs (plus active RFCs)" matching the hero diagram footer's existing drift-sidestepping convention; the line 31 test count switched from a frozen integer to "2400+ tests" matching the hero diagram's "720+ tests" floor pattern (counts grow over time; a floor stays true without re-edits). Initial PR [#278](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/278) from contributor @YuuGR1337 appended the new wording at the bottom of the file rather than replacing line 154; the in-place rework also caught the line 31 instance via the issue's `grep -n "locked spec docs" README.md` acceptance check. Co-authored with the contributor.

- **Explainer polish iteration after live-deploy review.** Two follow-up PRs after the long-scroll restructure ([#261](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/261)) shipped and the maintainer eyeballed the rendered page in a browser. [#268](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/268) fixed four layout failures: TOC was rendering on the LEFT instead of the right (DOM-order bug — `<aside>` was sequenced before `<article>` in source); content column was choked to ~490px on a 1920px viewport because the page used the narrow `.container` (760px) with a grid stealing 220px for TOC + 48px gap; hero stats laid out 2-over-1 because `.intro-row` was hardcoded to `1fr 1fr` without a 3-stat variant; two hero CTAs scrolled to essentially the same anchor (dead-weight second button). Fixes: swapped DOM order so article renders left + TOC renders right, introduced `.container-explainer` (max 1200px) for the body while hero stays in `.container` (760px) for centered readable hero, added `.intro-row-3` modifier (`repeat(3, minmax(0, 1fr))` at desktop, stacks below 720px), reduced to a single CTA "Read the guide →". [#269](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/269) fixed three Mermaid diagram nodes that rendered with unreadable white-on-light-fill text in dark mode: the orange Project + blue Director boxes on the Section 4 cascade diagram and the blue Agent box on the Section 5 protocols-moat diagram. Mermaid's dark theme forces white text on nodes regardless of an inline `color:` directive in `style X fill:...,color:#000` syntax — dropping the custom styles entirely keeps both light and dark mode readable while losing the orange/blue node emphasis. Iteration lesson captured: for any Mermaid edit, render the page in a browser + toggle theme + verify each diagram before claiming "done" — the "looks fine in source / breaks at render time" pattern caught me twice on this explainer (judge-flow text-squish was the same shape).

- **Explainer guide restructured as a single long-scroll page with self-select depth.** Replaces the 5-chapter format (`01-the-agent-is-a-folder.html` through `05-portable-to-anywhere.html`) with a single `docs/explainer/index.html` — six anchored sections, sticky right-sidebar TOC with active-section highlighting, twin hero CTAs (5-min essentials vs keep-scrolling for everything), figure containers with titles + captions, collapsible deep-dives via native `<details>` for operator-level detail. 5-min reader skims bold ledes + figures; 25-min reader expands every deep-dive. New content covers the Mandate primitive (durable cumulative authorization), PolicyBackend (in-progress fleet-wide caps), and the Postgres umbrella ([#258](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/258)) — all previously absent or thin. Old chapter files deleted (anyone with chapter bookmarks will 404; for a 9-day-old public repo with no external traffic, acceptable cost). Mermaid diagrams rewritten with explicit text labels (no more emoji-shorthand that caused the node-text-squish the maintainer flagged on the judge-flow diagram). README hero link unchanged — still points at the index, which is now the canonical surface.

- **README's "Planned: Postgres adapters" claim now points at concrete tracking** ([#258](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/258)). Filed an umbrella issue with sub-task checklist (Log / Profile / ToolRegistry / Memory+pgvector / Mandate / Lock Postgres adapters in priority order) so the calling-card claim has auditable backing instead of being a directional gesture. Surfaced when the maintainer read the new substrate-swap paragraph and assumed Postgres adapters were already shipped — exactly the "soft-pass" P1 the round-1 reviewer flagged on PR [#256](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/256). Closes that gap.

- **README "Why this exists" gains substrate-swap paragraph.** Calling-card framing closed on the abstract throughline ("home user with one agent / org with a fleet experience the same framework") but never proved it. New paragraph after the throughline names the actually-shipped non-filesystem backends (LogBackend + SQLite; LockBackend + Redis; AgentProfileBackend + ToolRegistryBackend + SQLite), flags Postgres as planned, and reinforces that the agent definition / persona / folder layout / audit trail don't change when the substrate swaps — only the backend registration does. Closes the "is this serious?" doubt for the 60-second drive-by reader who would have bounced before reaching the "Backend protocols — the scaling story" section.

### Security

- **Repo-wide security + automation hardening (Tier 1 + Tier 2).** Tier 1 (enabled via `gh api` at the repo-settings layer, no PR): GitHub secret scanning + push protection (blocks accidental commits of API keys / tokens), Dependabot vulnerability alerts + automated security fixes (auto-PR on CVE), `delete_branch_on_merge: true`, `allow_auto_merge: true`. Tier 2 (this PR): `.github/dependabot.yml` for weekly Python (pip ecosystem reading `pyproject.toml`) + GitHub Actions version updates with minor+patch grouped per ecosystem and 5-PR caps; `.github/CODEOWNERS` (`* @dep0we`) so all PRs auto-request maintainer review per `MAINTAINER.md` tiered review process; `.github/workflows/test.yml` permissions tightened to explicit `contents: read` at workflow level (was inheriting permissive defaults). Closes the most common solo-OSS hardening gaps without taking on the friction of required reviews or signed commits.

Removed "ChatGPT skill" and "your own HTTP service" from the runtime portability sentence in `README.md` line 18 (both are tracked as unshipped on the next-on-the-roadmap thread); added "launchd" and "embedded Python" which are both currently shipped (`extras/launchd/` ships three plist templates per [#71](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/71); the programmatic-Python API is documented in `docs/deployment/programmatic.md`). - **`MAINTAINER.md` ships the tiered PR review process for incoming contributions** (no issue — operator-facing doc for OSS contributors). New top-level doc telling contributors what to expect when they open a PR: a four-tier risk classification (trivial docs → security-sensitive code → stranger + larger change), the specific signals that auto-elevate any PR's tier (dependency changes to `pyproject.toml` / `uv.lock`, `.github/workflows/` edits, binary additions, PR description not matching diff scope, disabling or removing existing tests, brand-new GitHub account + non-trivial change), how to make a PR easy to merge (pick from `good first issue` / `help wanted`, keep scope focused, write a description that matches the diff, open an issue first when touching design-rule territory or adding dependencies), what review tooling is run (pre-landing diff analysis, independent cross-model review for security-sensitive changes, OWASP-style dependency + secret scans), and realistic response-time expectations for an alpha single-maintainer project. The doc references `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, and `SECURITY.md` so contributors can navigate to the deeper community-health set. Surfaced after recent uptick in external PR activity ([#241](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/241) from `@thehanslevi` was the first external PR — picked up within hours of `good first issue` tagging on [#135](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/135)). Pre-existing review discipline was implicit per CLAUDE.md rule 11 ("Adversarial review in rounds, not passes"); `MAINTAINER.md` makes the process explicit at the surface so contributors don't have to read the codebase to figure out what scrutiny their PR will face.

- **spec/29 mandates — pre-implementation amendments before #124 PR 1 opens** (no issue — recorded under spec/29 RFC convention; spec stays RFC until #124 PR 5 locks it). Six structural amendments landed via /office-hours 2026-05-17 + /plan-eng-review 2026-05-17: (1) New §"Implementer contract for mandate backends" section adds the `MandateBackend` Protocol surface with 8 normative MUSTs (path-traversal refusal, per-scope isolation, state-via-Protocol-methods, source-hash recomputation per load, lifecycle events through LogBackend, base reservation event discriminator shape, pessimistic crash recovery, capability honesty) — mirrors spec/22/24/25 Implementer contract patterns. `FilesystemMandateBackend` is the only reference impl in v1; future SaaS / mobile / Slack-bot adapters slot in via `register_mandate_backend(...)` without forking core. (2) §"Target extraction" rewritten — `target_extractor` is now a named per-agent registry (`agent.register_target_extractor(name, callable)`) with built-in heuristics pre-registered per-agent; `ToolDefinition.target_extractor_id: str | None` references the registry by string ID. The earlier `Callable` shape was rejected because `Callable` fields cannot satisfy spec/25 MUST #4 Tier B's lossless round-trip obligation (which we just pinned with conformance tests in PR #211). Per-agent scoping matches spec/25 Decision 9 + spec/15 delegate-isolation precedent. (3) §"Lifecycle event deduplication" updated — state persistence is a `MandateBackend.read_state(scope) / write_state(scope, state)` Protocol contract, NOT a filesystem-path contract. `FilesystemMandateBackend` persists at `<scope_root>/.judge-state/mandates.json` (the prior path); future backends use SQL tables / API stores. State shape now carries `schema_version: 1` from PR 3a onward; unknown versions raise `MandateStateSchemaUnsupported`. (4) New §"Suspicious-rebind throttle" closes the source-hash-before-state edit window — when `MandateCheck` surfaces `mandate_state_inconsistent`, the framework refuses re-binding on the same mandate for 60s default (`judges.md ## Mandates / suspicious_rebind_throttle_s`). New lifecycle event `mandate_rebind_suspicious_throttled`; new doctor check `check_mandate_rebind_throttle_active`. Closes a prompt-injection / hallucinating-actor exploitation window without requiring filesystem locks. (5) New §"BLOCK reason naming discipline" — `BLOCK` reasons emitted by `MandateCheck` MUST be forever-stable strings (no PR identifiers / version markers / phase suffixes leak into JSONL audit reasons). Temporary-cause stories belong in CHANGELOG entries, not reason fields. Applied immediately: PR 3a's stubbed budget reason is `mandate_budget_check_unavailable`, NOT `_in_3a`. (6) `mandate_cap_exceeded_block` lifecycle event extended with `contributing_reservation_ids` array + `reconcile_cli_hint` string — operators see WHY they're blocked (which orphan reservations pushed cumulative over) and WHAT to run (`atomic-agents mandate reconcile <id> --action ...`), not just THAT they are. Cross-link from §"Crash recovery" → §"Audit shape" makes the surfacing path explicit. (7) New §"Validation step split between PR 3a and PR 3b" documents the implementation discipline locked at /plan-eng-review — PR 3a ships steps 1-6 + stub 7-9; PR 3b ungates 7-9. Step 9 (escalation thresholds) explicitly belongs in PR 3b because it operates on projected costs from steps 7+8 (which are stubbed in 3a). Status banner gains a "Pre-implementation amendments" subsection listing all six. Zero behavior change — this is spec-only prep before the implementation PRs (#124 PR 1 of 6) open. Test count unchanged at 1961.

The flip documents what the project actually does.
