New §"Plan-subagent — pre-implementation design review" documents the second-layer review surface added in #63/#64 — five SEVERE architectural risks caught pre-implementation across two arcs (#63 PR 3: `shutil.copytree` non-atomic snapshot design → JSON-based snapshot trio, `make_agent_dir`-only conformance helper trap that would have broken ~20 SQLite tests; #64 PR 3: base64-exec handler rejection → hybrid metadata-in-SQL + handler-bodies-on-disk, composite `PRIMARY KEY (agent_scope, name)`, `make_tool_in_backend` conformance helper) that would have re-architecture-cycled if caught by Step 11 alone. CLAUDE.md rule 11 "Codex review in rounds" flipped to "Adversarial review in rounds" to match; operational Codex references at CLAUDE.md:194, :280, :317 + methodology.md:347 flipped to adversarial-review-default language (historical Codex references in verify-before-claim examples preserved). Pure docs change — no code or test changes; behavior unaffected.

### Fixed

- **`SQLiteLogBackend` cold-start WAL race — concurrent thread/process appends to a fresh db no longer flake** ([#208](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/208)). `atomic_agents/logs/sqlite.py:_get_conn` now sets `PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000` BEFORE `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` and wraps the WAL transition in a 7-attempt retry loop that matches on `sqlite_errorcode in (SQLITE_BUSY, SQLITE_LOCKED)` (Python 3.11+) with exponential backoff (0.05/0.1/0.2/0.4/0.8/1.6s cumulative ~3.15s of sleep, plus up to 5s `busy_timeout` per attempt). Same shape as the parallel #64 PR 3 fix in `atomic_agents/registry/sqlite.py`: when N threads/processes race the very first WAL transition on a fresh db file, `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` raises `sqlite3.OperationalError: database is locked` because the WAL transition needs an EXCLUSIVE lock and SQLite's journal-mode-switch path is not fully covered by the busy_handler. The busy_timeout PRAGMA alone is insufficient under heavy contention (empirically reproduced at ~65% fail rate locally on macOS even after the busy_timeout was added — verified during this fix); the additional retry loop closes the gap. Matching on `sqlite_errorcode` (not message text) means a future SQLite wording change can't silently swallow legitimate `SQLITE_CORRUPT` / `SQLITE_READONLY` errors. Pre-existing `tests/test_log_sqlite_backend.py::test_concurrent_appends_from_threads` macOS flake (~80% local fail rate per the #64 arc handoff) now passes 20/20 consecutive runs. New regression tests pin both the primitive (`test_busy_timeout_set_before_wal_pragma` — `PRAGMA busy_timeout` returns 5000ms on a fresh connection) and the end-to-end concurrent behavior (`test_concurrent_appends_no_wal_race_under_repeated_runs` — 10 fresh dbs × 4 threads × 10 appends each, all 10 iterations succeed). Spec/22 §"Implementer contract for queryable backends" MUST #4 extended to require `busy_timeout` before WAL + retry on `SQLITE_BUSY`/`SQLITE_LOCKED` for SQLite-shape backends (parity with spec/25 §"Implementer contract for registry-backed tool backends" MUST #5; the SQLiteLogBackend implementation now meets its own spec). Filesystem-default deployments unaffected — only operators pinning `ATOMIC_AGENTS_LOG_BACKEND=sqlite` for indexed query/aggregate/retention saw the flake. No operator-visible behavior change beyond reliability.

### Added

- **`ToolRegistryBackend` Tier B conformance pins — `input_schema` / `description` / `handler` round-trip enforced for structured-storage backends** ([#207](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/207)). Spec/25 §"Implementer contract for registry-backed tool backends" MUST #4 Tier B is normative — structured-storage backends MUST round-trip every `ToolDefinition` field that affects dispatch losslessly. Before this PR, the parametrized conformance suite pinned `name`, `classification`, and `version` round-trip but NOT `input_schema` / `description` / `handler` — a future Tier B backend (PyPI, company-internal HTTP, SaaS-database) could silently drop or normalize those fields and pass green. Three new parametrized conformance tests in `tests/test_tool_registry_protocol_conformance.py` close the gap: `test_load_tool_round_trips_input_schema` asserts strict dict equality on a non-trivial schema (nested objects + integer constraints `minimum`/`maximum`/`default` + booleans `additionalProperties: false` + required arrays); `test_load_tool_round_trips_description` asserts exact-string round-trip with quotes + unicode (catches whitespace normalization, quote coercion, escape rewriting); `test_load_tool_round_trips_handler_callable` invokes the materialized handler with structured input exercising real branches (`msg` + `count` + nested `options.upper`) and asserts the return value matches source semantics (catches a stub or no-op substitution). Both reference backends (`FilesystemToolRegistryBackend` + `SQLiteToolRegistryBackend`) pass without code changes — the tests are pure regression pins for future backends. Test counts: +3 functions × 2 backends = +6 invocations; no skips. Spec/25 MUST #4 footnote updated from "Closing that gap is tracked in #207" to "closed by [#207](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/207) ... via three parametrized conformance tests". Filed inline during the #64 PR 4 round-2 Step 11 adversarial — the new tests are the "regression-prevention for future PyPI/HTTP backends" the issue body called for.

- **`ToolRegistryBackend` Protocol locked + `Implementer contract for registry-backed tool backends` documented + README/CLAUDE.md status refresh** ([#64](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/64) — tool-registry arc **PR 4 of 4; closes the #64 arc**). `docs/spec/25-tool-registry-backend.md` status flips from DRAFT to **LOCKED** — the spec now matches the shipped implementation across all four arc PRs. New §"Implementer contract for registry-backed tool backends" (8 normative MUSTs) consolidates the registry-backend pattern that PRs 1–3 introduced into a single section so future PyPI / git / company-internal-HTTP / SaaS-database contributors have one place to read the obligations: path-traversal refusal for operator-supplied `name` AND `agent_scope` at the API boundary (PR 1 Step 11 adversarial defenses); cross-scope isolation enforced at the storage layer (`WHERE agent_scope = ?` on every query for SQL backends; per-agent `<agent>/tools/` natural scoping for filesystem); `install()` atomicity via **INSERT-first + atomic_write-on-success-only** ordering (PR 3 Step 11 P1 REPRODUCED 50/50 TOCTOU race fix — the original handler-atomic_write-first order caused concurrent installs to destroy the winner's handler file via the loser's rollback `unlink()`); two-tier descriptor round-trip (Tier A raw-text-preserving for filesystem-shape backends; Tier B lossy-parse-documented for structured-storage backends like SQLite that store `descriptor_json` + handler `.py` on disk — operator notes / YAML comments NOT preserved on Tier B; both tiers round-trip every `ToolDefinition` dispatch field losslessly); idempotent schema initialization + `PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000` BEFORE `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` for SQLite-shape backends (PR 3 Step 11 P1 REPRODUCED 3/5 multi-process WAL race fix — same shape as the pre-existing `test_log_sqlite_backend.py::test_concurrent_appends_from_threads` flake queued as a one-line follow-up); capability honesty (claim-vs-behavior parity enforced by the parametrized conformance suite — `test_capability_parity_install` round-trip pin from PR 3 Step 11 testing CRITICAL fix); trust-model framing for shared-catalog backends (the catalog is process-shared but trust is NOT — multi-tenant deployments MUST scope at the process level, one framework process per tenant; the judge layer is the runtime safety net, not the registry layer); connection / handler lifecycle safe under thread-life-tied cleanup (`threading.local` for pool-bearing backends; `check_same_thread=True` for `:memory:`; per-instance `tempfile.mkdtemp()` for `handlers_root` honoring the non-persistent promise; `handlers_root` refuses `<= 1`-component paths). The parametrized conformance suite (`tests/test_tool_registry_protocol_conformance.py`) runs against every registered backend so the contract is verified by the same tests that pin `list_tools` / `load_tool` / `install` / `uninstall` / `validate` / `capabilities` semantics — no separate "registry-backend" test suite required.

  **README.md** `Backend protocols` table: `ToolRegistryBackend` row flips from `Planned` to **✅ Locked at PR 4**, with the full PR-3-shipped surface inlined (filesystem + SQLite reference impls, parametrized conformance, hybrid metadata-in-SQL + handler-bodies-on-disk storage on SQLite, `install` / `uninstall` capability flipped True on SQLite with TOCTOU-safe atomicity, cross-scope isolation enforced at SQL layer, operator override via `ATOMIC_AGENTS_TOOL_REGISTRY_BACKEND=sqlite` + optional URL with `?agent_scope=`, `doctor.check_tool_registry_backend` coherence check, Implementer contract). The README's "v1 direction" + opening "honest about what isn't shipped" bullet + final Status block all bump from "six backend protocols shipped" to "**seven backend protocols shipped**" with the ToolRegistryBackend bullet appended. Test count: 1750 → 1953 under full CI extras (`uv sync --extra dev --extra openai --extra validation --extra redis`).

  **CLAUDE.md** `Architecture in one breath` diagram: `ToolRegistry 🟡` flips to `ToolRegistry ✅ (locked at #64 PR 4)` on its own line — the diagram is one of the load-bearing onboarding documents (loaded in every session via the `claudeMd` mechanism) and pinning the status here prevents future-Claude from re-proposing already-shipped work. Total under full CI extras (`uv sync --extra dev --extra openai --extra validation --extra redis`): 1750 → 1953 tests (+203 net across the arc — 4 new test files contribute 150 functions parametrized to 221 invocations: 43 conformance ×2 backends + 35 filesystem-specific + 52 SQLite-specific + 20 wiring; 18 skip on capability gates, 203 pass = arc delta). Per-PR delta lines in earlier CHANGELOG entries (PR 1/2/3) reported counts as observed at write time and don't sum exactly to +203 due to baseline drift; the canonical end-state is the 1953-passing line above. This PR is spec/docs-only with no test additions. Zero regressions, zero unresolved P0/P1 findings — twelve Step 11 adversarial P0/P1 findings addressed across the arc pre-merge (per the per-PR CHANGELOG entries: PR 1 caught 2 P0 + 4 P1 including REPRODUCED YAML alias-bomb DoS at 33 GB RSS and REPRODUCED control-char log-injection; PR 2 caught 3 P1 including REPRODUCED `chmod-000 tools/` PermissionError-blocks-every-agent-construction; PR 3 caught 3 P1 REPRODUCED — TOCTOU race in install() 50/50, multi-process WAL race 3/5, URL factory credential leak). Protocol seam in place; two reference impls (filesystem + SQLite) shipped; future PyPI / git / company-internal-HTTP / SaaS-database adapters register via `register_tool_registry_backend(...)` without forking core — same agent definitions, same `agent.call()` flow, same audit trail, different tool catalog. Filesystem-default deployments see zero behavior change end-to-end across the arc — `<agent>/tools/` walk preserves operator-edit-with-text-editor UX; all 115 `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites in the test suite unaffected.

  **PR 4 ships docs only** — no code changes, no test additions, no operator-visible behavior change. Test count unchanged at 1953 (full CI extras). The lock is the contract.

- **`SQLiteToolRegistryBackend` reference impl + parametrized conformance over both backends + `install` / `uninstall` flipped True** ([#64](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/64) — tool-registry arc **PR 3 of 4**). Operators can now pin `ATOMIC_AGENTS_TOOL_REGISTRY_BACKEND=sqlite` (+ optional `ATOMIC_AGENTS_TOOL_REGISTRY_BACKEND_URL=sqlite:///path/to/tools.db?agent_scope=<name>`) to back the tool catalog on a single SQLite file instead of per-agent `<agent>/tools/`. Without the URL env var, defaults to `<agent_root>/.tools.db` with `agent_scope=<agent_root.name>` so single-host operators get a working SQLite default by flipping ONE env var. Stdlib `sqlite3`, no optional dependency. **SaaS-shaped tool catalogs are now one Protocol implementation away** — the eventual PyPI / git / company-internal-HTTP backends slot in via the same `register_tool_registry_backend("<id>", cls)` API. Hybrid storage shape (plan-subagent Risk A fix): SQLite stores metadata only (descriptor JSON + handler path + version + classification + scope + timestamps); handler bodies live on disk under `<handlers_root>/<agent_scope>/<name>.py` for inspectability + closures-over-module-globals + module-level imports + `session = requests.Session()` patterns at handler-module top-level — base64-exec'd-source was rejected at the plan-subagent stage because it silently breaks all of these. Schema: `tools(agent_scope, name, descriptor_json, handler_path, version, classification, created_at, updated_at, PRIMARY KEY (agent_scope, name))` — composite PK so two scopes can both have a tool named the same. `meta(key PK, value)` schema-version tracking with idempotent `INSERT OR IGNORE` cold-start race fix (#61 PR 3 + #63 PR 3 lesson). Cross-scope isolation enforced at every query (`list_tools` / `load_tool` / `uninstall` ALL filter `WHERE agent_scope = ?`); the scope is hardcoded from the constructor and refused at construction for path-traversal tokens (defense-in-depth on top of SQL parametrization). `install()` validates descriptor + imports handler at install time (rejects malformed YAML, missing handler symbol, non-callable handler, descriptor name-mismatch — operators don't ship broken tools that fail at every agent construction). `uninstall()` is idempotent. Capabilities: `supports_install=True, supports_uninstall=True, supports_versioning=False (column round-trips for forward compat but PR 3 rejects non-None version at install — capability honesty per plan-subagent Risk L), supports_sandbox_validate=False, supports_skills_catalog=False, durable=True (False for :memory:)`. URL factory `make_sqlite_tool_registry_backend_from_url` honors `sqlite:///path?agent_scope=<name>`; refuses non-sqlite scheme, netloc, fragments, duplicate query params, multi-value scope, unknown query params (silent drops are operator-intent footguns). `:memory:` mode is single-threaded test-only (check_same_thread=True; ProgrammingError at first cross-thread use rather than silent corruption); handlers_root defaults to a per-instance tempdir to honor the non-persistent promise.

  **Plan-subagent caught 3 SEVERE risks pre-implementation** (saved a re-architecture cycle): Risk A (base64-exec handler storage breaks closures + module imports + top-level setup) → switched to filepath storage using the same `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` path the filesystem reference uses; Risk D (schema PK `name` alone can't work multi-scope) → composite `PRIMARY KEY (agent_scope, name)` from the start; Risk J (conformance suite needs `make_tool_in_backend` helper before SQLite factory lands — same shape as #63 PR 3's `make_agent_in_backend`) → added pre-implementation. Risks B, K, L, F, G, E all documented + addressed in spec/25.

  **Step 11 adversarial (Opus subagent — Codex skipped per the standing rule) caught 3 P1 REPRODUCED + 2 P2 + 3 critical testing gaps**, all fixed pre-merge:
  - **P1 TOCTOU race in `install()` REPRODUCED 50/50 pre-fix** — original order (atomic_write handler → INSERT) caused concurrent installs to destroy the winner's handler file via the loser's rollback `unlink()`. Fix: reordered to INSERT-first + atomic_write-on-success-only; loser sees `rowcount=0` and raises `ToolAlreadyInstalled` WITHOUT touching disk. Regression test pins the invariant (concurrent install race with assertion that winner's handler file survives + remains importable).
  - **P1 multi-process WAL race REPRODUCED 3/5** — `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` raised `database is locked` when N processes raced fresh-db open. Fix: `PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000` before WAL pragma so concurrent WAL-transition contention waits instead of failing.
  - **P1 URL factory credential leak REPRODUCED** — `make_sqlite_tool_registry_backend_from_url('postgres://user:secret@host/db')` echoed the raw URL with credentials in its `ValueError` messages, propagating to any caller of `get_default_tool_registry_backend` → `AtomicAgent.__init__`. Fix: new `_redact_url` helper applied to all 5 ValueError sites in the URL factory.
  - **P2 :memory: handlers_root wrote to disk** — defaulted to `Path.cwd() / .handlers/`, contradicting the "non-persistent / test-only" docstring promise + letting two :memory: instances clobber each other's on-disk handlers. Fix: per-instance `tempfile.mkdtemp()` default.
  - **P2 handlers_root accepted `/`** — constructor took any path without validation; running as root on misconfigured Linux would write to `/<scope>/`. Fix: refuse paths with `<= 1` component after resolution.
  - **Testing CRITICAL**: `test_capability_parity_install` was a tautology (only checked `NotImplementedError` not raised; swallowed all other exceptions via `except Exception: pass`). Fix: rewritten as a real install + verify-in-list_tools round-trip — catches "backend whose install() silently no-ops" — exactly plan-subagent's Risk C.
  - **Testing CRITICAL**: install() previously accepted non-callable `handler = 42` (only `validate()` caught it). Fix: `_import_handler` now raises `ToolHandlerImportFailed` on non-callable too — install refuses up-front (filesystem reference inherits the strengthened check; the docstring-promise that install validates handlers is now honest).

The parametrized conformance suite is the load-bearing artifact — future PyPI / git / company-internal-HTTP backends drop into `BACKEND_FACTORIES` and inherit ~37 conformance tests.

Public `self.tool_registry_backend` mirrors `self.lock_backend` / `self.log_backend` / `self.profile_backend`. New wiring loop at agent construction: backend-discovered tools register into the existing in-memory `self.tool_registry` after the operator's `tools=ToolRegistry()` kwarg — operator intent wins on collisions (backend tools register with `allow_overwrite=False` so collisions surface loudly as `ToolNameCollision`). **Empty / missing `<agent>/tools/` directory yields zero registrations** — every existing 96 `AtomicAgent(...)` test site sees byte-identical pre-#64 behavior; backend wiring is opt-in via filesystem layout. Per-runner kwargs added to OutcomeRunner + EvalRunner + DreamRunner (DreamRunner stores for API parity but doesn't construct internal AtomicAgents). `delegate.py`-driven recursive AtomicAgent construction deliberately does NOT thread the backend (per-agent scoping — spec/25 Decision 9; target builds its own backend over its own `tools/`, distinct from the fleet-scoped `profile_backend` which IS threaded). New `doctor.check_tool_registry_backend(agent_root)` mirrors `check_agent_profile_backend`: PASS/WARN/FAIL ladder + capability snapshot + tool-count probe + URL credential redaction on the unknown-id FAIL path. Step 11 adversarial (Opus subagent — Codex skipped per the standing project rule) caught + fixed pre-merge: a `chmod-000 <agent>/tools/` previously raised `PermissionError` from `iterdir()` and blocked EVERY agent construction (REPRODUCED P1) — defense pushed into `FilesystemToolRegistryBackend.list_tools` (treat unreadable as empty, log at WARN); wiring loop's exception swallow broadened to also catch `ValueError` (control-char filenames) and `OSError` (per-tool filesystem failures); debug logging added to per-tool swallow so operators have a triage breadcrumb. Step 11 also caught + replaced two tautology tests: runner threading tests now monkeypatch the internal AtomicAgent class to capture kwargs at the boundary (catches the kwarg-drop trap #61/#63 PR 2 prior arcs documented); delegate non-threading test now actually invokes `coord.delegate()` and inspects target's kwargs (previously constructed two parallel agents — which would pass regardless of threading behavior). Spec/25 §"Decision 5" updated to acknowledge PR 2 wiring is **eager at construction** (not lazy at first dispatch); §"Known gaps" refreshed: TOCTOU + double-parse re-targeted to a successor follow-up issue, handler module re-import gap documented for runner-loop deployment shape. New `atomic_agents/registry/` package containing the Protocol contract, canonical types (`ToolRef`, `ToolRegistryCapabilities`, `ValidationResult`), reference filesystem backend that walks `<agent>/tools/<name>.{md,py}` for descriptors + handler modules, and the operator-config factory `get_default_tool_registry_backend(agent_root)` honoring `ATOMIC_AGENTS_TOOL_REGISTRY_BACKEND` env var (mirrors the `_PROFILE_` / `_LOG_` / `_LOCK_` env-var conventions). Discovery is lazy — `list_tools()` parses frontmatter only; `load_tool(name)` runs the Python import (spec/25 Decision 5). DRAFT spec at `docs/spec/25-tool-registry-backend.md` (locks at PR 4) documents 10 load-bearing decisions including: separation between the new discovery-layer Protocol and the existing in-memory `ToolRegistry` (`atomic_agents/tools.py` — preserved unchanged); strict carve-out keeping per-agent **mounted** skills on `AgentProfileBackend.list_skills` (spec/24 Decision 2) while reserving the catalog skill surface here as `supports_skills_catalog`; MCP server discovery left in its own module (future `MCPServerRegistryBackend` is a separate Protocol — successor issue to be filed); `ToolRef.version` field ships round-trip-safe with `supports_versioning` capability reserved (pip-style resolution as the default-target); `validate()` is static-only (descriptor parse + handler import + signature check, NO handler execution — spec/25 Decision 6); `install` / `uninstall` capability-gated to False on filesystem (operators edit `<agent>/tools/` directly — Decision 7); per-agent backend instance (NOT process-shared — Decision 9, mirrors the structural difference from `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend` which is rooted at `agents_root`). Tracked in spec/25 §"Known gaps deferred to follow-up issues": unconstrained symlink resolution under `tools/` (legitimate single-tenant operators symlink shared tools from fleet-wide paths — defense-in-depth flag deferred), TOCTOU race between `list_tools` and `load_tool` (PR 2 wiring will close via parsed-descriptor passthrough), `ValueError` from path-traversal validation escapes `AtomicAgentsError` hierarchy (consistent with `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend._agent_root` precedent — sibling parity matters more than hierarchy purity in v1). AND agent_id = ?` for SQL backends; path-scoping + metadata cross-check for filesystem backends); `agent_mode` re-derivation discipline on `save_profile` (spec/24 Decision 6 — single source of truth is `persona_identity`); raw-text round-trip preservation for all `*_md_raw` fields (spec/24 Decision 1 — `mcp_md_raw` is security-critical because `$VAR` env refs would otherwise bake resolved secrets into persistent state); idempotent schema initialization across processes (`INSERT OR IGNORE` pattern from #61 PR 3 SQLiteLogBackend); snapshot id entropy budget (≥48-bit random tail + timestamp prefix, per Step 11 F-8); thread-life-tied connection management (`threading.local` for pool-bearing backends); `supports_skills` capability honesty (claim-vs-behavior parity enforced by parametrized conformance).   **Closes the #63 AgentProfileBackend arc.** Four PRs over the agent-profile span: scaffolding (PR 1 #192), wiring (PR 2 #193), SQLite reference impl + snapshot trio + parametrized conformance + Step 11 adversarial review fixes (PR 3 #194), spec lock + status refresh (this PR — PR 4). Total: 1589 → 1750 tests (+161 net across the arc — 60 PR 1 + 19 PR 2 + 82 PR 3; this PR is spec/docs-only with no test additions), zero regressions, zero unresolved P0/P1 findings (six Step 11 adversarial P0/P1 findings fixed in PR 3 pre-merge — including the reproduced cross-agent path-traversal in filesystem `list_snapshots`/`restore`). **The SaaS-shape cliff that motivated the arc is closed:** operators with SaaS UIs, multi-tenant agent registries, hot-reload requirements, snapshot/clone primitives, or distributed deployments now plug a custom `AgentProfileBackend` into the framework via the env-var or constructor-kwarg surface; the framework's bootstrap path (`AtomicAgent.__init__` → `_load_config` → all 4 runners → doctor) routes through their backend without forking core. Filesystem-default deployments see zero behavior change end-to-end across the arc — `<agent>/persona/IDENTITY.md|SOUL.md|USER.md` + config files + `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` on-disk artifacts preserved byte-for-byte; all 96 existing `AtomicAgent(...)` test sites + 9 cascade integration tests unaffected.

  **PR 4 ships docs only** — no code changes, no test additions, no operator-visible behavior change. Test count unchanged at 1750. The lock is the contract.

- **`SQLiteAgentProfileBackend` reference impl + parametrized conformance + snapshot trio implementation + `supports_skills` capability** ([#63](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/63) — agent-profile arc **PR 3 of 4**). Operators can now pin `ATOMIC_AGENTS_PROFILE_BACKEND=sqlite` (+ optional `ATOMIC_AGENTS_PROFILE_BACKEND_URL=sqlite:///path/to/profiles.db`) to back the agent registry on a single SQLite file instead of a directory tree. Same agent definitions, same `agent.call()` flow, same audit trail — different substrate. Stdlib `sqlite3`, no optional dependency. Without the URL env var, defaults to `<scope_root>/.profile.db` so single-host operators get a working SQLite default by flipping ONE env var. **SaaS-shaped agent registries are now one Protocol implementation away from the framework's existing operator-config surface.**

  **`atomic_agents/profile/sqlite.py`** — new `SQLiteAgentProfileBackend` (~480 LOC). Schema: `agents(name PK, agent_mode indexed, profile_json, updated_at)` for the registry; `profile_snapshots(snapshot_id PK, agent_id indexed, label, created_at indexed, profile_json)` for the snapshot trio; `meta(key PK, value)` for schema version tracking. **JSON blob + indexed scalars** approach (Decision 1 of PR 3) — `AgentProfile.to_dict()` serializes to `profile_json` with `default=str` (PosixPath coercion at the json.dumps boundary; `from_dict` re-derives structured fields from raw text on load, so the JSON shape is forward-compatible with new AgentProfile fields without `ALTER TABLE` migrations). `agent_mode` duplicated as an indexed scalar column for registry-by-mode queries. **Cold-start race mitigated via `INSERT OR IGNORE`** on the schema_version row (mirrors the #61 PR 3 SQLiteLogBackend Step 11 P0 #2 fix — multi-process deployment shape where N replicas open the same db file simultaneously). Schema version mismatch raises with a forward-pointer error at first connection. **`threading.local` connection pool** gives each thread its own `sqlite3.Connection`; WAL journal mode + `synchronous=NORMAL` for multi-process append safety on local filesystems (network-mounted filesystems NOT supported — SQLite WAL on NFS is documented-broken upstream; documented in the module docstring).

  **`save_profile` re-derives `agent_mode` from `persona_identity`** before writing (Decision 6 — same documented-derived invariant the filesystem backend enforces via its load-from-disk parser path; SQLite enforces at write time). Without this, operators who construct an AgentProfile with a bogus `agent_mode` field would write the bogus value and read it back unchanged — breaking the cross-backend agent_mode parity that conformance tests pin.

  **`make_sqlite_profile_backend_from_url(url)`** mirrors the `logs/sqlite.py:make_sqlite_backend_from_url` template — accepts `sqlite:///absolute/path/to/profiles.db` (three-slash convention, empty netloc), `sqlite::memory:` / `sqlite:///:memory:` for in-memory (test-only, emits `RuntimeWarning` about non-persistence; reports `durable=False`). Rejects non-`sqlite` schemes, netloc-bearing URLs (`sqlite://host/path` ambiguity guard — operator likely meant `sqlite:///host/path`), empty paths.

  **Snapshot trio implementation (Decision 3 — JSON-based, NOT `shutil.copytree`)**: BOTH backends now declare `supports_snapshot=True` and ship `snapshot()` / `restore()` / `list_snapshots()`. **Plan-subagent flagged the original `shutil.copytree` design as non-atomic at the agent level** — crash mid-copy leaves the agent partially snapshotted; restore via `copytree(snapshot_dir, agent_root, dirs_exist_ok=True)` has no good atomicity story either. Revised design: `snapshot()` loads the current `AgentProfile` and serializes `profile.to_dict()` as JSON; `restore()` reconstructs via `AgentProfile.from_dict()` and writes via the backend's normal `save_profile()` (atomic via existing per-file `_io.atomic_write` discipline for filesystem; transactional for SQLite). **Snapshot ID format**: `snap_<YYYY-MM-DDTHHMMSS+TZ>_<6hex>` — timestamp prefix gives free lexicographic-sort fallback, the 6-hex random tail eliminates same-second collisions, no colons in the time field for Windows-share portability. Filesystem backend additionally validates the shape via `_validate_snapshot_id` (refuses operator-supplied path-traversal tokens) and path-scope-checks the snapshot dir via `relative_to(snapshots_root)` before any disk access. **Cross-agent isolation** enforced via two mechanisms: SQLite's `WHERE snapshot_id = ? AND agent_id = ?` clause in `restore()`; filesystem's `metadata.agent_id == agent_id` cross-check on top of the path-scope check. A `SnapshotNotFound` raised by either path is indistinguishable to the caller — the rule is "snapshots scope per-agent" regardless of mechanism. **Skills are NOT snapshotted** — they aren't part of `AgentProfile` (Decision 2 of PR 1), so the JSON shape doesn't carry them; documented explicitly in spec/24 Decision 3.

  **`supports_skills: bool` capability added to `ProfileCapabilities`** (Decision 8). `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend` declares `True` (walks `<agent>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`); `SQLiteAgentProfileBackend` declares `False` — `list_skills` returns `[]` for present agents (raises `AgentProfileNotFound` for missing agents — GAP-11 parity); `load_skill_body` always raises `FileNotFoundError`; `clone` does not copy skills. **Why skills stay filesystem-only in PR 3**: `SkillManifest` carries filesystem-specific `Path` fields (`skill_dir`, `skill_md_path`); storing skill bodies in the database would require a new `save_skill` Protocol method — a Protocol expansion that PR 3 scope-disciplines away. Tracked under spec/24 §"Reserved future capabilities" for the SaaS UI editing path; conformance tests gate skill content on `supports_skills` so adding the method + capability flip later will exercise existing test coverage on the new backends.

  **Conformance suite parametrized across both reference backends.** `tests/test_profile_protocol_conformance.py` now runs every test once per registered backend (filesystem + sqlite) via a `backend_factory` fixture. New helper `make_agent_in_backend(backend, scope_root, agent_id, **kwargs)` uses the Protocol surface (`save_profile`) for setup so SQLite-backed conformance tests pick up agents created via the Protocol — **Plan-subagent caught the trap that the previous `make_agent_dir`-only helper writes filesystem state which SQLite's `load_profile` can't see; would have broken ~20 of the conformance tests for SQLite**. Skill-content tests + filesystem-shape tests (hidden-dir exclusion, dir-without-identity exclusion) gate via `pytest.skip` on the relevant capability / backend_id. **7 new snapshot conformance tests** added in this PR pin the snapshot contract on every backend that declares `supports_snapshot=True`: round-trip (snapshot → mutate → restore returns snapshotted state), `list_snapshots` empty for fresh agent, chronological ordering, label preservation, restore-unknown raises `SnapshotNotFound`, cross-agent isolation refuses restoring agent A's snapshot onto agent B, snapshot_id uniqueness across N rapid back-to-back calls.

  **`tests/test_profile_sqlite_backend.py`** — 36 SQLite-specific tests covering: schema creation (agents + profile_snapshots + meta tables), schema_version row presence + idempotent cold-start race + version mismatch detection (refuses opening a db with `schema_version=999`), WAL journal mode probe (`PRAGMA journal_mode`), in-memory `RuntimeWarning` + non-durable capability + in-memory round-trip, JSON blob round-trip preserves persona/tools/roster/mcp byte-for-byte, `save_profile` agent_mode normalization (Decision 6 enforcement), indexed columns probe (`idx_agents_mode`, `idx_snapshots_agent_id`, `idx_snapshots_created_at`), skill semantics (`list_skills=[]` for present agent, `list_skills` raises `AgentProfileNotFound` for missing agent, `load_skill_body` always `FileNotFoundError`, `supports_skills=False`), snapshot table inserts directly visible via raw SQL, cross-agent isolation at the query level + restore cross-agent refusal + empty `snapshot_id` refusal, clone via `save_profile` re-normalizes agent_mode, clone refuses overwrite + rejects unknown override fields, URL parsing (absolute path, in-memory shorthand `sqlite::memory:`, three-slash memory `sqlite:///:memory:`, wrong scheme `postgres:///` rejected, netloc rejection `sqlite://host/path` rejected, empty path `sqlite:///` rejected), registry resolution (`sqlite` in `list_profile_backends()`, `get_profile_backend("sqlite")` returns the class), env-var dispatch (sqlite default path under scope, with URL, filesystem default, unknown id raises `BackendNotRegistered`, credential redaction when operator pastes a URL into `ATOMIC_AGENTS_PROFILE_BACKEND` instead of `..._URL`), constructor creates parent dir for nested db paths.

  **`atomic_agents/profile/__init__.py`** — registers `sqlite` at module-import time alongside the eager `filesystem` registration; `get_default_profile_backend(scope_root)` adds the `sqlite` branch (reads `ATOMIC_AGENTS_PROFILE_BACKEND_URL`, falls back to `<scope_root>/.profile.db` when absent so single-host operators get a working default without setting two env vars). `SQLiteAgentProfileBackend` + `make_sqlite_profile_backend_from_url` added to `__all__`.

  **`atomic_agents/profile/filesystem.py`** — snapshot trio now JSON-based (the 3 PR-1 `NotImplementedError` stubs replaced); `supports_snapshot` flipped from `False` to `True`; `supports_skills=True` declared; `_validate_snapshot_id` helper added (refuses path-traversal in operator-supplied snapshot_id).

  **`tests/test_profile_integration.py`** updated: the doctor assertion `result.detail["supports_snapshot"] is False` flipped to `True` to match the now-implemented snapshot trio.

  **Step 11 adversarial review fixes** (Claude + Codex cross-model pass — see PR body for the full finding list):

  - **F-1 (concurrency safety lie):** `:memory:` constructor had `check_same_thread=False` and allocated an unused `_shared_lock`. Module docstring claimed "standard thread-safety pattern" — false; the lock was never acquired anywhere. Fix: dropped the dead lock, restored `check_same_thread=True` (sqlite3 default) so cross-thread access on `:memory:` raises `ProgrammingError` immediately at the misuse site instead of producing `SystemError` / `InterfaceError` corruption. `:memory:` is now honestly documented as single-threaded test-only.
  - **F-3 (cross-agent path-traversal information disclosure — REPRODUCED):** `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend.list_snapshots(agent_id)` and `restore(agent_id, ...)` built `<scope>/.snapshots/<agent_id>` via raw path concat and only validated the snapshot_id segment, leaving operator-controlled `agent_id` free to read `metadata.json` from outside `scope_root`. Fix: both methods now call `self._agent_root(agent_id)` at the API boundary BEFORE any disk access — refuses `"../../other"`-shape inputs structurally. Two new conformance tests (`test_list_snapshots_refuses_path_traversal_agent_id`, `test_restore_refuses_path_traversal_agent_id`) pin the invariant on both backends.
  - **F-4 (`:memory:` case-typo file pollution):** URL `sqlite::Memory:` (operator typo) silently fell through to the file-backed path and created a real file named `:Memory:` in cwd. Fix: case-insensitive `.strip().lower()` matching for the `:memory:` sentinel (both `sqlite::memory:` and `sqlite:///:memory:` shapes).
  - **F-5 (URL query/fragment silent drop):** SQLAlchemy-style `sqlite:///foo.db?mode=ro` silently dropped the `?mode=ro`, landing read-only intent as read-write in production. Fix: `make_sqlite_profile_backend_from_url` now raises `ValueError` for any URL with `parsed.query` or `parsed.fragment`.
  - **F-8 (snapshot ID collision at fleet scale):** 6-hex random tail (24 bits) had ~52% collision probability per second at 4K snapshots/sec. Fix: bumped to 12 hex (48 bits) — same-second collision at 4K/sec down to ~6e-8. Conformance test `test_snapshot_id_uniqueness_across_rapid_calls` bumped from 5 to 100 calls + structural assertion on the random-tail length.
  - **F-PLR-3 (doctor capability disclosure gap):** `doctor.check_agent_profile_backend` exposed `supports_save/clone/snapshot/subscribe/durable` in the detail dict but not the new `supports_skills`. Operators couldn't see this dimension when comparing filesystem vs SQLite via doctor. Fix: added `supports_skills` to both detail dicts (filesystem + non-filesystem branches); pinned in `test_doctor_check_profile_backend_filesystem_pass`.
  - **F-PLR-4 (composite snapshot index):** `idx_snapshots_agent_id` + `idx_snapshots_created_at` as two separate indexes let SQLite use one for the WHERE filter and then sort by `created_at`. Replaced with composite `idx_snapshots_agent_created(agent_id, created_at)` — single index serves both the lookup and the sort in `list_snapshots`.

  Test count: 1668 → **1750** (+82 across this PR: 36 SQLite-specific + 7 new snapshot conformance tests × 2 backends + 2 new path-traversal conformance tests × 2 backends + the existing conformance suite re-parametrized so backend-agnostic tests run on both backends). 16 skips (capability-gated: skill-content tests skip on SQLite, filesystem-shape tests skip on SQLite). Zero regressions across the existing 1668-test surface; the filesystem-default operator surface is byte-identical to PR 2 — operators see no behavior change without flipping the env var. PR 4 locks spec/24 and adds the §"Implementer contract for registry-backed backends" section based on what shipped across PRs 1–3.

- **`AgentProfileBackend` wired into `AtomicAgent.__init__` + bootstrap path + all runners + doctor** ([#63](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/63) — agent-profile arc **PR 2 of 4**). Operators can now plug a custom `AgentProfileBackend` into the framework via `AtomicAgent(..., profile_backend=...)` constructor kwarg or `ATOMIC_AGENTS_PROFILE_BACKEND=<id>` env var. The filesystem default produces byte-identical state to today's direct-file-read code, so all existing deployments see no behavior change. SaaS / database / git-backed agent registries are now ONE Protocol implementation away — register your backend via `register_profile_backend("my-backend", MyBackendClass)`, set the env var, and every `AtomicAgent` constructed in the process loads its config through your backend.

  **Wiring details:**

  - `AtomicAgent.__init__` accepts `profile_backend: AgentProfileBackend | None = None` (keyword-only, after `lock_backend` + `log_backend`). Default-resolves via `get_default_profile_backend(self.agents_root)`. Stored as **public** `self.profile_backend` (mirrors `self.lock_backend` / `self.log_backend`) so diagnostic code and runners can reuse the same instance.
  - `self._profile = self.profile_backend.load_profile(self.name)` snapshots the agent config ONCE at init. Private cache — not a stable operator surface. `_load_config()`, `_load_persona()`, `_load_goal_text()`, and the `agent_mode` derivation all read from this snapshot instead of re-reading filesystem.
  - `_load_config()` cascade branch deleted — `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend.load_profile` handles cascade internally (single source of truth). `self.cascade` attribute preserved for `_load_role_prompt`, `_load_project_layer_text`, `_load_tools_text`, and tool-registration paths that need it.

  **Cascade unblocker** (`atomic_agents/profile/filesystem.py:_agent_root`): PR 1 refused `/` in `agent_id` as overly-restrictive belt-and-suspenders. Cascade agents have multi-segment names like `"muse/projects/the-unfinished/agents/writer"` (per spec/06's `<system>/projects/<project>/agents/<role>/` layout) — the slash refusal would have broken all 9 cascade integration tests under PR 2's wiring. The `.resolve() + .relative_to(scope_root)` belt remains the actual security boundary; all other validations (empty rejection, leading `.` refusal, backslash refusal, explicit `..` refusal) stay in place.

  **Runner threading** (the load-bearing kwarg-drop-trap shape that recurs every arc):

  - `OutcomeRunner.__init__` adds `profile_backend` kwarg; threaded to internal `AtomicAgent`.
  - `EvalRunner.__init__` adds BOTH `log_backend` AND `profile_backend` kwargs — fixed a pre-existing `log_backend` drop-trap gap (an #61 PR 2 miss) at the same time.
  - `DreamRunner.__init__` adds `profile_backend` kwarg; pre-resolves `self._profile` once at init. **Critical Step 11 P1#3 fix from PR 1**: DreamRunner had TWO `model.md` call sites — `dream.py:1128` (pipeline) AND `dream.py:672` (`_check_cap` cost-guardrail, easy to miss). BOTH now route through the profile backend. Without wiring both, an operator using a non-filesystem profile backend would have correct config for `AtomicAgent.call()` but stale cost caps applied to dream runs because `_check_cap` would silently fall back to filesystem `model.md`.

  **`_check_cap` signature** (`atomic_agents/dream.py:650`): accepts new `model_config: dict | None = None` kwarg. When provided (DreamRunner always passes `self._profile.model_config`), the function uses the pre-resolved config; when omitted, falls back to legacy `_model.parse_model_md` for backward compatibility with any external caller. This avoids re-resolving the agent_name from `agent_root` inside `_check_cap` (Decision 2 — cleaner than threading `profile_backend` + agent_name through the cost-guardrail).

  **Doctor** (`atomic_agents/doctor.py:check_agent_profile_backend`): new function mirroring `check_log_backend` shape — PASS/WARN/FAIL ladder, URL credential redaction via `urlparse + _replace(netloc=...)`, capability snapshot + agent-count probe. Wired into `run_doctor()` between `check_log_backend` and `check_memory_backend`. `"profile-backend"` added to the skip-names tuple so `doctor` without `--agent` emits an explicit SKIP entry (parity with `memory-backend`).

  Test count: 1649 → **1668** (+19 across this PR: 2 in Phase 0 cascade-unblocker tests + 17 in new `tests/test_profile_integration.py` covering AtomicAgent wiring, cascade compatibility, runner threading for all 4 runners, the load-bearing DreamRunner two-call-site regression test, `_check_cap` model_config passthrough + legacy fallback, and 5 doctor coherence-check paths). Zero regressions across the 96 existing `AtomicAgent(...)` test sites + 9 cascade integration tests. **The filesystem default's byte-identical-state property is what makes this PR safe to land**: `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend.load_profile` produces the same structured output the old `_load_config` produced from direct file reads, so existing operators see no behavior change.

- **`AgentProfileBackend` Protocol scaffolding + `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend` reference impl + DRAFT `docs/spec/24-agent-profile-backend.md`** ([#63](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/63) — agent-profile arc **PR 1 of 4; opens the #63 arc**). New `atomic_agents/profile/` module establishes the sixth backend protocol in the protocol-pattern series alongside the five shipped backends (Memory, LLM, Judge, Lock, Log). `AgentProfileBackend` Protocol contract at `atomic_agents/profile/backend.py` exposes `load_profile` / `save_profile` / `list_agents` / `exists` / `list_skills` / `load_skill_body` / `clone` / `snapshot` / `restore` / `list_snapshots` / `capabilities`. `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend(scope_root)` reference impl wraps the legacy directory layout — every existing parser (`_model.parse_model_md`, `_tools.parse_tools_md` + `parse_tool_classifications`, `judges_md.load_judges_config`, `_roster.parse_roster_md`, `mcp.parse_mcp_md_text`, `goal.parse_agent_mode_text`, `skills.discover_skills`) is consumed; on-disk `<agent>/persona/IDENTITY.md|SOUL.md|USER.md`, `<agent>/{model,tools,judges,roster,mcp,goal}.md`, `<agent>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` artifacts preserved byte-for-byte. Operator override surface stubbed via `get_default_profile_backend(scope_root)` reading `ATOMIC_AGENTS_PROFILE_BACKEND` env var (default `"filesystem"`); URL credential redaction in `BackendNotRegistered` error messages mirrors the same fix from `logs/__init__.py:316` (the `locks/__init__.py` analog is a pre-existing gap, tracked separately). New exceptions in `atomic_agents/exceptions.py`: `AgentProfileNotFound`, `AgentProfileExists`, `SnapshotNotFound`. Test count: 1589 → **1649** (+60 across two new test files: 37 conformance + 23 filesystem-specific). All public symbols re-exported from `atomic_agents/__init__.py` per the prior arc (LockBackend, JudgeBackend) precedent.

  **`AgentProfile` carries typed shadow + raw text** for every config file (Decision 1 in the spec). The structured forms (`model_config`, `tool_config`, `tool_classifications`, `judges_config`, `roster`, `mcp_servers`) ship for DB-backend query / inspection; the raw markdown text fields (`model_md_raw`, `tools_md_raw`, `judges_md_raw`, `roster_md_raw`, `mcp_md_raw`) are the source of truth for filesystem write-back and audit. **The reason this is mandatory rather than aesthetic**: `mcp.parse_mcp_md` resolves `$VAR_NAME` env-var refs to literal values at parse time (`mcp.py:560`); a `save_profile()` rendering from `MCPServerSpec` would bake those resolved secrets — `GITHUB_PAT=ghp_real_token` — into on-disk `mcp.md`. **This is a security issue** and the load-bearing reason raw-text storage is mandatory rather than a structured-only design. Two other lossy parsers (`_tools.parse_tools_md` strips operator comments, `_roster.parse_roster_md` strips comments after the first separator) compound the case. Conformance test pinning the invariant: `test_save_profile_preserves_mcp_dollar_var_refs`. `renderers.py` is intentionally NOT shipped in PR 1 — deferred to PR 3 if the second reference impl needs canonical export back to markdown.

  **Skills are separate Protocol methods, not a profile field** (Decision 2). `SkillManifest` carries `skill_dir: Path` and `skill_md_path: Path` — meaningless for non-filesystem backends. Embedding `list[SkillManifest]` with bodies in `AgentProfile` would also violate spec/18's progressive-disclosure principle (only metadata in the system prompt; bodies via `load_skill` tool). The Protocol exposes `list_skills(agent_id) -> list[SkillManifest]` and `load_skill_body(agent_id, skill_name) -> str` so a future DB backend can store skills in a separate table per-agent.

  **Snapshot trio declared on Protocol, implementation deferred to PR 3** (Decision 3). `FilesystemAgentProfileBackend.capabilities().supports_snapshot=False`; `snapshot()` / `restore()` / `list_snapshots()` raise `NotImplementedError`. PR 3's second reference impl (likely `SQLiteAgentProfileBackend` — snapshots are a `snapshots` table) ships the trio and flips the capability flag for both backends in parallel. Conformance suite skips snapshot exercises when capability is False (claim-vs-behavior parity rule from spec/22 §"Conformance test surface").

  **Cascade carve-out** (Decision 5): for cascaded agents (`<system>/projects/<project>/agents/<role>/`), `load_profile()` calls `_cascade.detect_cascade(agent_root)` and reads `tools_md_raw`/`model_md_raw` as the merged text from `_cascade.resolve_tools_md()` / `_cascade.resolve_model_md()`. **`save_profile()` writes only instance-layer files** — project-floor `judges.md` and role-layer `tools.md`/`model.md` are read paths only. Operator-edits-via-save_profile cannot corrupt the shared role layer. Conformance tests pin both behaviors: `test_cascade_load_picks_up_role_tools` + `test_cascade_save_writes_instance_only_for_role_tools` + `test_cascade_save_writes_to_instance_when_instance_file_exists`.

  **`agent_mode` is documented-derived** (Decision 6): the field stays on `AgentProfile` (DB backends will want it as an indexable column) but its source of truth is `persona_identity`. Filesystem backend derives via `goal.parse_agent_mode()` on load and ignores the field on save (re-derived next load). Conformance test pins the asymmetry: `test_save_profile_ignores_agent_mode_field`. Database backends MAY persist `agent_mode` as a column for registry queries but MUST re-derive on update to avoid divergence.

  **Zero call-site changes.** `AtomicAgent.__init__` is untouched. `_load_config()` is untouched. `cli.py`, `delegate.py`, `outcome.py`, `eval.py`, `dream.py`, `doctor.py`, `migrate.py` are untouched. PR 2 wires the bootstrap path (constructor kwarg + env-var default + `_load_config()` refactor + `doctor.check_agent_profile_backend` coherence check + per-runner kwarg threading). PR 3 ships the second reference impl + parametrized conformance + snapshot implementation. PR 4 locks the spec.

- **`LogBackend` Protocol locked + `Implementer contract for queryable backends` documented + README/CLAUDE.md status refresh** ([#61](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/61) — log layer arc **PR 4 of 4; closes the #61 arc**). `docs/spec/22-log-backend.md` status flips from DRAFT to **locked** — the spec now matches the shipped implementation across all four arc PRs. New §"Implementer contract for queryable backends" (8 normative MUSTs) consolidates the indexed-query + native-aggregate pattern that PR 3 introduced into a single section so future Postgres / Datadog / Loki / Cloud Logging contributors have one place to read the obligations: push every LogQuery predicate to native primitives with appropriate indexes; lenient agent_name filter for legacy compat; backward-compat cost_source NULL-as-actor semantics; atomic schema initialization across processes (idempotent INSERT patterns to avoid cold-start race); ValueError on naive datetime in delete_older_than; aggregation pushdown with allowlist-validated group_by identifiers; threading.local connection management; multi-tenant scoping via LogQuery.agent_name with optional native backend isolation. The conformance suite (`tests/test_log_protocol_conformance.py`) parametrizes across every registered backend so the contract is verified by the same tests that pin append/query/aggregate/delete/stats semantics — no separate "queryable backend" test suite required.

  **README.md** `Backend protocols` table: `LogBackend` row flips from 🟡 Planned to **✅ Locked at #61 PR 4**, with the full PR-3-shipped surface inlined (`FilesystemLogBackend` + `SQLiteLogBackend` + six indexes + WAL + per-thread connections + JSON1 aggregation + LogQuery.agent_name + operator override + doctor.check_log_backend). The README's "v1 direction" + "What's shipped" + final status block all bump from "three backend protocols shipped" to "five backend protocols shipped" with the LockBackend + LogBackend bullets appended. Test count: 1425 → 1589.

  **CLAUDE.md** `Architecture in one breath` diagram: `Log 🟡` flips to `Log ✅ (locked at #61 PR 4)` and rolls up onto the same line as `Lock ✅` — the diagram is one of the load-bearing onboarding documents (loaded in every session via the `claudeMd` mechanism) and pinning the status here prevents future-Claude from re-proposing already-shipped work. CLAUDE.md `§ What this is` opening paragraph mentions LogBackend alongside the four prior shipped Protocols. CLAUDE.md `§ Status` updates: 1425 → 1589 tests; "Four backend protocols shipped" → "**Five backend protocols shipped**" with the LogBackend bullet appended.

  **Closes the #61 LogBackend arc.** Four PRs over the log-layer span: scaffolding (PR 1 #185), wiring (PR 2 #186), SQLite reference impl + cross-agent isolation (PR 3 #187), spec lock + status refresh (this PR — PR 4). Total: 1425 → 1589 tests (+164 net across the arc — 66 PR 1 + 19 PR 2 + 79 PR 3; this PR is spec/docs-only with no test additions), zero regressions, zero unresolved P0/P1 findings. **The dashboard-perf cliff that motivated the arc is closed:** operators on Cloud Run / Kubernetes can pin `SQLiteLogBackend` for O(log N) indexed queries instead of O(N) JSONL parses; the same Protocol seam admits future `Datadog` / `Loki` / `Postgres-with-pgvector` adapters without forking. Filesystem-default deployments see zero behavior change end-to-end across the arc — `<agent>/log/YYYY-MM/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` on-disk artifact preserved byte-for-byte, every `self._log({...})` call site keeps its dict-literal shape verbatim, cost-guardrail discipline (CLAUDE.md #4) preserved via filesystem backend's legacy file-walk semantic for malformed-ts records.

  **One follow-up filed:** cross-primitive run records (`eval._write_run_log` + dream manifest reroute through `agent.log_backend` with `primitive="eval"` / `primitive="dream"`) — those artifacts live at separate paths (`evals/runs/`, `dreams/runs/`) and carry rich primitive-specific shapes that warrant their own schema-design discussion separate from the log-backend abstraction layer. The LogBackend Protocol itself is load-bearing-complete across both reference backends; the eval/dream reroute is a primitive-layer concern, tracked separately.

- **#61 PR 3 review-pass fixes** — Step 9.1 specialist army + Step 11 Opus adversarial findings on the SQLite backend:
  - **`LogQuery.agent_name` filter added** (Step 11 P0 #1 — multi-tenant data leak). Without this filter, shared-backend deployments (single SQLite/Postgres file across multiple agents) silently mix cross-agent records into reader call sites — alice's cost guardrails sum bob's spend; alice's dashboard renders fleet records stamped with alice. The lock arc's PR 3 Step 11 caught the equivalent multi-host data-race; the log arc's PR 3 had the same trap shape. Wired through every reader: `_costs.sum_cost_for_period` (new `agent_name=` kwarg threaded from `agent.py` 6 call sites + `dream.py` `_check_cap`); `dashboard/costs.load_runs` passes `agent_name=agent`; `dashboard/quality._count_provenance` passes `agent_name=agent`; `dream._read_log_lines` passes `agent_name=self.agent_name`. Lenient match semantics: records with `agent_name is None` (legacy pre-PR-2 records on disk) match any filter — under filesystem's per-agent-dir scoping, every record in the dir IS the named agent's; strict filtering would break dashboard reads of legacy data. Cross-agent isolation property holds for explicitly-stamped records (every post-PR-2 record carries `agent_name` set by `agent._log()`). New conformance test `test_query_filters_by_agent_name_isolates_explicit_agent_records` pins the strict isolation; `test_query_filters_by_agent_name_lenient_on_missing` pins the backward-compat path.
  - **Cold-start race in `_ensure_schema` fixed** (Step 11 P0 #2 — multi-process deployment shape). Two processes simultaneously initializing a fresh SQLite db both SELECT the missing `schema_version` row, both try to INSERT, the second hits a `UNIQUE PRIMARY KEY` constraint and fails to initialize. Fix: `INSERT OR IGNORE` makes the schema-version write idempotent; losing the race is a no-op. New test `test_cold_start_race_idempotent_schema_init` opens two backend instances against the same fresh db and asserts both succeed. The schema migration ladder pattern is documented in `_ensure_schema`'s docstring for future schema bumps (PR 4+ adding columns via `ALTER TABLE`).
  - **`_row_to_record` empty-string preservation fix** (Step 11 P2 — backend divergence). Previous code used `row[col] or DEFAULT` which collapsed empty strings to defaults (Python `or` treats `""` as falsy). Filesystem backend uses `d.get(key, fallback)` which only fires on missing keys — empty strings round-trip preserved. Fix: SQLite now uses `row[col] if row[col] is not None else DEFAULT`. New test `test_row_to_record_preserves_empty_required_strings`.
  - **URL parser rejects netloc** (Step 11 P2 — silent data-loss via misconfiguration). `sqlite://host/path` is ambiguous (two-slash typo for `sqlite:///path`? netloc-bearing alternative?). Previously fell through to a relative-path landing under `cwd`. Now raises `ValueError` with a clear "use sqlite:///absolute/path (three slashes)" hint. New test `test_url_rejects_netloc`.
  - **In-memory backend emits `RuntimeWarning`** (Step 11 security #4 — data-loss surprise). `sqlite::memory:` is non-persistent; operators who pin it in production lose all logs on every restart. Doctor previously reported PASS without flagging. Fix: `warnings.warn("non-persistent...")` in `make_sqlite_backend_from_url` and `SQLiteLogBackend(":memory:")`. New test `test_in_memory_construction_warns`.
  - **`_CANONICAL_COLUMNS` derived from `RunRecord.__dataclass_fields__`** (Step 9.1 maintainability — drift hazard). Manual frozenset would silently drift out of sync when RunRecord gains a field — new field would route through `json_extract` instead of a direct SQL column, breaking query plans. Fix: derive at module load time. New test `test_canonical_columns_derived_from_run_record`.
  - **`stats()` consolidated to single SQL statement** (Step 11 perf #2). Previous 3 separate COUNT(*) scans collapsed to one query with conditional aggregation — one O(N) covering-index scan instead of three. Dashboard home tab perf improvement.
  - **`stats()` docstring corrected** (Step 11 P1 — documentation honesty per CLAUDE.md rule #13). Previous "O(1) with the ts index" claim was false; `COUNT(*)` over the full table is O(N). New docstring states the actual complexity: COUNT is O(N) covering-index scan (I/O-cheap); MIN/MAX are O(1) index-edge lookups; conditional aggregates use idx_ts.
  - **`_INSERT_SQL` precomputed at module load** (Step 11 perf — hot path micro). Previous code rebuilt the INSERT statement string via two `.join()` operations on every `append()` — 27+ times per `agent.call()`. Now computed once.
  - **WAL-on-NFS caveat documented** in `SQLiteLogBackend` module docstring (Step 9.1 maintainability — upstream-known footgun). SQLite WAL on NFS is documented-broken by upstream; module docstring now warns operators with network-mounted SQLite deployments.
  - **Forward-pointer `| {"sqlite"}` unions removed** from `get_log_backend`, `get_default_log_backend`, and `doctor.check_log_backend`. Sqlite is eagerly registered in PR 3; the registry's own list is authoritative now.
  - **Dead lazy import in `get_default_log_backend`** removed — `make_sqlite_backend_from_url` and `SQLiteLogBackend` are imported eagerly at module load; the inner `from .sqlite import ...` blocks were no-op redirects creating "this is lazy" misreads.
  - **Schema migration ladder documented** in `_ensure_schema` docstring — explicit insertion point for future `ALTER TABLE` migrations and `meta` row updates. Cross-references CHANGELOG `### BREAKING` discipline.
  - **9 new tests** pinning the review fixes (66+ conformance now × 2 backends + 31 sqlite-specific). Tests: 1578 → 1589 (+11 net; 7 skips preserved).

- **`SQLiteLogBackend` reference impl + parametrized conformance + URL parsing operator surface + doctor stats probe** ([#61](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/61) — log layer arc **PR 3 of 4**). New `atomic_agents/logs/sqlite.py` ships `SQLiteLogBackend` using stdlib `sqlite3` — no optional dependency, no install step. Schema: one `run_records` table mirroring the `RunRecord` dataclass (canonical fields as columns; `extra` as JSON-text); six indexes (`idx_ts`, `idx_run_id`, `idx_primitive`, `idx_parent_run_id`, `idx_cost_source`, `idx_mandate_id`) covering the predicate patterns the dashboard + cost-guardrail readers use. **WAL journal mode + per-thread connections** via `threading.local` — concurrent reader/writer interleaving plus thread-safe writes (the single-process default `DELETE` journal would serialize writes against any concurrent SELECT — unacceptable for "dashboard reading while call writes"). **Multi-process append safe**: WAL mode supports multiple `SQLiteLogBackend` instances pointing at the same db file from different processes — the load-bearing property for operators running atomic-agents on Cloud Run / Kubernetes with N replicas pinned to a shared SQLite file (the framework's motivating multi-host story). Schema version recorded in a `meta` table; mismatches at open time raise with a forward-pointer error. **Aggregation pushdown** via SQL `GROUP BY` for canonical column group_bys, and via SQLite's stdlib JSON1 `json_extract(extra, '$.NAME')` for primitive-specific `extra`-field group_bys (e.g., `group_by=("iteration",)` for outcome iteration records). `aggregate()` SQL injection guard: `group_by` field names that aren't canonical columns are validated against an alphanumeric-plus-underscore charset before interpolating into the JSON path expression. **Capabilities** — `supports_aggregation_pushdown=True` (vs filesystem's `False`); `supports_retention=True` with index-driven `DELETE WHERE ts < :threshold`; `durable=True` via WAL fsync; `supports_streaming=False` (reserved). **`make_sqlite_backend_from_url`** parses `sqlite:///absolute/path/to/db.sqlite` (three-slash absolute) and `sqlite::memory:` (in-memory shortcut); rejects empty paths, non-sqlite schemes. Registered eagerly via `register_log_backend("sqlite", SQLiteLogBackend)`. **Operator surface**: `ATOMIC_AGENTS_LOG_BACKEND=sqlite` + optional `ATOMIC_AGENTS_LOG_BACKEND_URL=sqlite:///path/to/db.sqlite` (without URL, defaults to `<scope_root>/.logs.db`). **Conformance suite parametrized** across both backends: every conformance test runs once with filesystem, once with sqlite — **88 conformance tests up from 44** (44 conformance × 2 backends). Third-party backends import `BACKEND_FACTORIES` from `tests/test_log_protocol_conformance.py` to verify their own conformance against the same contract. **`tests/test_log_sqlite_backend.py` adds 24 SQLite-specific tests** pinning: schema creation + version tracking + version-mismatch refusal; index set (6 indexes — `idx_ts` etc.); WAL journal mode enabled; round-trip preserves every RunRecord field including `extra` JSON encoding; `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` shows `idx_run_id` use; `cost_source="actor"` backward-compat includes NULL via `WHERE cost_source = ? OR cost_source IS NULL`; index-driven `delete_older_than`; concurrent multi-threaded appends (4 threads × 10 records each = 40 records); aggregate group_by via JSON1; SQL-injection guard on bad identifiers; URL parsing for absolute paths, in-memory, malformed scheme rejection, empty-path rejection; registry resolution; `get_default_log_backend("sqlite")` with-and-without URL. **`doctor.check_log_backend`** now constructs and probes any non-filesystem backend via `stats()` — sqlite paths return PASS with `total_records`, `records_today`, `records_this_month`, optional `size_bytes` in `detail`. Schema-corrupted or unreachable backends WARN (not FAIL — same pattern as `check_provider_keys`; doctor never crashes on optional infrastructure). URL credential redaction preserved across the new probe path. Tests: 1510 → 1578 (+68 net: 44 new parametrized conformance + 24 sqlite-specific; 7 skips preserved). **Cross-primitive run records (`eval._write_run_log` + dream manifest write reroute through `agent.log_backend.append(primitive="eval" / "dream")`) DEFERRED to a follow-up** — those artifacts (`evals/runs/<date>.jsonl`, `dreams/runs/<id>/manifest.json`) carry rich primitive-specific shapes (eval scores + judge justifications, dream consolidations) that warrant their own schema-design discussion separate from the log-backend abstraction layer. The LogBackend Protocol itself is now load-bearing-complete across both reference backends.

- **`LogBackend` Protocol wired into all production call sites + `AtomicAgent(log_backend=...)` + `OutcomeRunner(log_backend=...)` + `DreamRunner(log_backend=...)` kwargs + `doctor.check_log_backend` + 19 integration tests** ([#61](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/61) — log layer arc **PR 2 of 4**).

### Changed

- **#61 PR 2 review-pass fixes** — addressing Step 9.1 specialist army + Step 11 Opus adversarial findings on the wiring PR:
  - **`OutcomeRunner.__init__` now accepts `log_backend=` kwarg** (Step 11 P0 #3) and threads it to the internal `AtomicAgent` constructed at `run()`. Mirrors the `DreamRunner` kwarg-drop-trap mitigation from #60 PR 3 — without this, operator-pinned backends would be silently dropped at the runner→agent construction boundary.
  - **`dashboard/quality._count_provenance` now queries WITHOUT primitive filter and discriminates helper records in-Python** (Step 11 P0 #2). The earlier `LogQuery(primitive="helper")` filter excluded legacy on-disk records that lack the `primitive` field (`RunRecord.from_dict` defaulted them to `PRIMITIVE_OTHER`, which the backend's predicate evaluator rejected BEFORE the in-method belt-and-suspenders check could run). Legacy helper records (every record written before PR 2 shipped) are now correctly counted via the `trigger == "helper"` fallback.
  - **`dream._read_log_lines` now filters helpers via `primitive OR trigger`** belt-and-suspenders (same root cause as the `_count_provenance` fix).
  - **`_costs.sum_cost_for_period` preserves the legacy file-walk semantic for `FilesystemLogBackend`** (Step 11 P0 #4). The cost guardrail is the framework's safety-load-bearing invariant per CLAUDE.md #4. Routing through `backend.query(LogQuery(since, until))` would silently drop records with malformed/missing `ts` content (legacy on-disk records, rotated/corrupted writes) — loosening the cap by an unknown amount. The fix special-cases `FilesystemLogBackend` to use the legacy walk (file location implies date, ts content ignored); SQL/Datadog backends (PR 3+) use the indexed query path where the malformed-ts case doesn't apply.
  - **`AtomicAgent` class-level annotation `log_backend: LogBackend`** added for parity with `lock_backend` (Step 9.1 maintainability CRITICAL). Without it, static analysis narrows `agent.log_backend` to the concrete `FilesystemLogBackend` default rather than treating it as any `LogBackend` implementer — breaking operator-pinned-SQLite/Postgres/Datadog cases in PR 3 forward.
  - **`doctor.check_log_backend` adds URL-credential redaction** (Step 9.1 security CRITICAL — security parity with `check_lock_backend`). Connection errors from backend constructors commonly embed full URLs including passwords; the redaction strips `userinfo` via `urlparse + _replace` before echoing in `CheckResult.message` / `detail`. Exception class name + full text omitted from the message (full exception still available via DEBUG logging).
  - **Stale forward-pointer block in `atomic_agents/logs/__init__.py`** rewritten past-tense — describes what IS wired (7 sites) and what's DEFERRED to PR 3 (`EvalRunner._write_run_log` + dream manifests, both of which live at separate artifact paths — `evals/runs/` and `dreams/runs/`).
  - **Dead imports removed**: `atomic_append_jsonl` in `outcome.py` (replaced by backend routing); `get_agents_root` inline in `dashboard/costs.py` (unused).
  - **Dead `cost_source_filter = source if source is not None else None`** no-op in `_costs._sum_via_backend` simplified to inline `cost_source=source`.
  - **5 new integration tests** in `tests/test_log_integration.py` pinning the review-fix invariants: legacy-record helper counting in `_count_provenance`, OutcomeRunner kwarg threading, sum_cost end-to-end with real `FilesystemLogBackend`, malformed-ts-record cost-cap counting (the safety-critical guardrail), and `doctor.check_log_backend` credential redaction. Tests: 1505 → 1510 (+5).
  - **Cost-cap filter behavior:** when an operator selects a non-filesystem backend (PR 3 forward), `sum_cost_for_period` will filter records by `ts` content via SQL `WHERE ts >= :since`. Records with malformed/missing `ts` content (no longer realistic for production records since `agent._log()` always emits valid tz-aware ISO-8601, but possible for hand-edited or pre-#122 on-disk records) will NOT be counted by SQL backends. The filesystem backend specifically preserves the legacy file-location-based date filter to avoid this case. Document this divergence in spec/22 PR 4. `AtomicAgent.__init__` now accepts `log_backend: LogBackend | None` (mirrors `lock_backend` shape) — operators pin a backend programmatically; default falls through to `get_default_log_backend(self.agent_root)` which honors `ATOMIC_AGENTS_LOG_BACKEND` env var (matches the locks operator surface from spec/21). `self.log_backend` is public alongside `self.memory` / `self.lock_backend` so diagnostic code reuses the same instance. **`agent._log()` converted to thin wrapper:** builds a `RunRecord` from the legacy dict literal (every existing 27+ `self._log({"trigger": "..."}, ...)` call site keeps its shape verbatim), derives `primitive` from the legacy `trigger` string via `_PRIMITIVE_BY_TRIGGER` mapping table with `"other"` fallback, defaults `run_id` to `self.run_id`, and routes through `self.log_backend.append(record)`. Default `FilesystemLogBackend` preserves the legacy on-disk shape byte-for-byte (writes to `<agent>/log/YYYY-MM/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` via `_io.atomic_append_jsonl` — same path as the pre-PR-2 code). **`outcome.OutcomeRunner._append_iteration_log`** routes through `agent.log_backend.append(...)` instead of writing directly — outcome iteration records now land in the operator's pinned backend (multi-backend split-brain fix forward — same DreamRunner-kwarg-drop trap shape the lock arc PR 3 Step 11 caught). **`_costs.sum_cost_for_period`** accepts an optional `backend: LogBackend | None = None` kwarg; when set, the period sum is computed via `backend.query(LogQuery(since, until, cost_source, mandate_id))` — pushdown-ready for SQLite/Postgres backends in PR 3 forward. Backward-compatible: `backend=None` (omitted) preserves the legacy filesystem walk for external callers. Every cost-cap site in `agent.py` (`call()` multi-turn loop, `_check_cost_guardrails`, `_emit_cost_warning`) and `dream.py` (`_check_cap`) passes `self.log_backend`. **`dream.DreamRunner`** accepts `log_backend=` kwarg and threads it through `_read_log_lines` + `_check_cap` + `_run_pipeline` — same forward-pointer trap mitigation as DreamRunner's lock_backend kwarg fix from #60 PR 3. `_read_log_lines` now queries through `backend.query(LogQuery(since=cutoff))` and filters helpers in-memory; legacy filesystem walk preserved as the `backend=None` fallback. **`dashboard/costs.load_runs`** routes through `get_default_log_backend(agents_root / agent).query(LogQuery(since, until))` — the dashboard now honors the operator's pinned backend (same env-var resolution path as `AtomicAgent.__init__`, keeping runtime writes and dashboard reads in sync). **`dashboard/quality._count_provenance`** routes through `backend.query(LogQuery(primitive="helper"))` and reads `provenance_preserved` from `record.extra` rather than parsing raw JSONL. **`doctor.check_log_backend`** added as a coherence check (matches `check_lock_backend` shape): unset/`filesystem` → PASS with `stats()` summary in the detail dict; `sqlite` (PR 3 forward-pointer) → FAIL with "not yet registered" hint; unknown id → FAIL with known-id list including `sqlite`. `tests/test_log_integration.py` adds 14 tests pinning the wiring contract: public `agent.log_backend` attribute + Protocol conformance, kwarg-overrides-default for the operator-supplied backend (DreamRunner-trap mitigation), `_log()` primitive derivation from every legacy trigger string, `run_id` defaulting, byte-for-byte legacy-reader compat at the end-to-end boundary, `OutcomeRunner._append_iteration_log` routing through the agent's backend, `sum_cost_for_period` with-and-without backend kwarg, `DreamRunner.log_backend` kwarg threading, `dashboard.load_runs` routing, `doctor.check_log_backend` PASS/FAIL paths. Tests: 1491 → 1505 (+14 net; 7 skips preserved). Zero regression. PR 3 ships `SQLiteLogBackend`.

- **`LogBackend` Protocol scaffolding + `FilesystemLogBackend` reference impl + 50 conformance tests + DRAFT spec/22** ([#61](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/61) — log layer arc **PR 1 of 4**). New module `atomic_agents/logs/{__init__.py, types.py, backend.py, filesystem.py}` mirrors the LockBackend module shape exactly (#60's well-rehearsed template now applied across five arcs). `@runtime_checkable LogBackend` Protocol surface: `append(record)` / `query(filter)` / `tail(n)` / `aggregate(filter, agg)` / `delete_older_than(threshold)` / `stats()` / `capabilities()` plus the `backend_id` property. Canonical types (all frozen dataclasses): `RunRecord` (required fields universal across every existing `_log({...})` site + common-but-optional fields + `extra: dict` for primitive-specific keys + `to_dict`/`from_dict` with byte-shape-preserving round-trip), `LogQuery` (AND-filter spec), `LogAggregate` (fixed metric vocabulary), `LogStats`, `LogCapabilities`. Twelve canonical primitive constants (`PRIMITIVE_AGENT_CALL`, `PRIMITIVE_OUTCOME_ITERATION`, `PRIMITIVE_DREAM`, `PRIMITIVE_EVAL`, `PRIMITIVE_HELPER`, `PRIMITIVE_DELEGATE`, `PRIMITIVE_TOOL`, `PRIMITIVE_COST_WARNING`, `PRIMITIVE_CAPTURE`, `PRIMITIVE_ESCALATION`, `PRIMITIVE_JUDGMENT`, `PRIMITIVE_OTHER`) document the open vocabulary PR 2 will derive from the legacy `trigger` string. `FilesystemLogBackend(scope_root)` preserves the legacy on-disk shape byte-for-byte: writes to `<scope_root>/log/YYYY-MM/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` via `_io.atomic_append_jsonl` (same path as `agent.py:3427`); the JSONL line places `ts` first and flattens `extra` to top-level keys so `dashboard/costs._record_from_dict` parses through unchanged (the load-bearing PR 2 invariant). Read paths: month-dir walk with cheap date-window prefilter; permissive `RunRecord.from_dict` tolerates heterogeneous legacy field shapes; per-line `json.JSONDecodeError` silently skipped (matches legacy reader at `_costs.py:147`). `delete_older_than` drops whole-file days strictly before threshold and rewrites the partial threshold-day file atomically via `_io.atomic_write`; idempotent (second call returns 0). `tail(n)` reverse-walks month dirs → day files → lines, accumulating to `n`, then reverses to chronological order. `aggregate` supports the canonical metric set (`count`, `sum_cost_usd`, `sum_input_tokens`, `sum_output_tokens`, `avg_latency_ms`) with in-memory grouping after `query()`; unknown metrics raise `ValueError`; all-`None` `avg_latency_ms` buckets return `None` (not 0.0, not crash); group-by names resolve through `extra` for primitive-specific aggregations. Registry (`register_log_backend` / `unregister_log_backend` / `get_log_backend` / `list_log_backends`) stores classes (not instances — matches LockBackend registry pattern). `get_default_log_backend(scope_root)` factory honors `ATOMIC_AGENTS_LOG_BACKEND` env var (default `filesystem`); unknown id raises `BackendNotRegistered` with a known-id list that includes the lazy `sqlite` forward-pointer (per the LockBackend Step-11-adversarial-P0-3 pattern that dodged "Available: ['filesystem']" operator-typo confusion). 30-test conformance suite parametrized via `BACKEND_FACTORIES` ready to receive PR 3's `("sqlite", _sqlite_factory)` entry (one-line edit). 20 filesystem-specific tests pin the on-disk-shape invariant (byte-for-byte legacy reader compat, atomic_append_jsonl integration, retention rewrite atomicity, multi-file tail walk, registry resolution, malformed-line resilience). DRAFT `docs/spec/22-log-backend.md` (locks at PR 4): full Protocol contract + normative `MUST/SHOULD` clauses for `append` semantics, aggregation pushdown, retention contract, `stats` diagnostic-only racy-by-design + operator surface (NOT a `logs.md` config; env vars + constructor kwarg parallel to LockBackend) + reserved future capabilities (`AsyncLogBackend`, `StreamingLogBackend`, `RetentionPolicyBackend`) + related-specs cross-reference. **Zero call-site changes** — `agent.py`, `outcome.py`, `_costs.py`, `dashboard/*.py`, `dream.py`, `eval.py` all untouched; PR 2 wires. Tests: 1425 → 1475 (+50 net; 7 expected skips preserved).

- **`LockBackend` Protocol locked + `Implementer contract for lease-backed backends` documented + README/CLAUDE.md status refresh** ([#60](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/60) — lock layer arc **PR 4 of 4; closes the #60 arc**). `docs/spec/21-lock-backend.md` status flips from DRAFT to **locked** — the spec now matches the shipped implementation across all four arc PRs. New §"Implementer contract for lease-backed backends" consolidates the heartbeat-driven lock-loss-detection contract that PR 3 introduced into a single normative section so future Postgres advisory / Cloud Run-specific / third-party adapter contributors have one place to read the obligations: spawn a heartbeat thread, surface lock loss via a ``lock_lost`` attribute on ``handle.backend_state`` (structural — auto-integrates with ``check_lock_lost`` without editing the dispatcher), log at WARNING level on detection, atomic release with token-check, ``renew()`` returns False on lease lost. The conformance suite (``tests/test_lock_protocol_conformance.py``) parametrizes across every registered backend so the contract is verified by the same tests that pin acquire/release semantics — no separate "lease backend" test suite required.

Status header test count: 1356 → 1425.

  **CLAUDE.md** ``Architecture in one breath`` diagram: ``Lock 🟡`` flips to ``Lock ✅ (locked at #60 PR 4)`` — the diagram is one of the load-bearing onboarding documents (loaded in every session via the `claudeMd` mechanism) and pinning the status here prevents future-Claude from re-proposing already-shipped work. CLAUDE.md ``§ What this is`` opening paragraph mentions LockBackend alongside the prior three shipped Protocols. CLAUDE.md ``§ Status`` updates: 1327 → 1425 tests; "Three backend protocols shipped" → "**Four backend protocols shipped**" with the LockBackend bullet appended. Remaining-backlog line drops "Lock" from the list of unshipped protocols.

  **Closes the #60 LockBackend arc.** Four PRs over the lock-layer span: scaffolding (PR 1 #180), wiring (PR 2 #181), distributed reference impl (PR 3 #182), spec lock + status refresh (this PR — PR 4). Total: 858 → 1425 tests (+567 net across the arc; +54 in PR 3 + spec-lock-only this PR), zero regressions, zero deferred work items. The multi-host cliff that motivated the arc is closed: operators on Cloud Run, Kubernetes, gizmo, or any deployment with managed Redis can now run atomic-agents against a Redis advisory-lock backend without forking. Filesystem-default deployments see zero behavior change end-to-end across the arc — `<agent>/.lock` on-disk artifact preserved byte-shape, `AgentLockBusy` exception still raised (via the `AgentLockBusy = LockBusy` alias), `_locks.AgentLock` import path preserved as a deprecation shim through v1.0.

- **`RedisLockBackend` distributed reference impl + `scope()` Protocol method + `LockLost` exception + operator override surface** ([#60](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/60) — lock layer arc PR 3 of 4).
